## Upstream advance (c62d191c4c..3052bbcf8c)

- `8e2afc0912` #44636 recover OAuth metadata discovery from 503 via OIDC -
  **done**. `mcp/oauth_discovery.go` now carries the metadata HTTP status
  (`mcpOAuthMetadataHTTPStatusError`, message unchanged) and, when an
  authorization-server metadata candidate returns 503, tries the same issuer's
  OIDC discovery endpoints in RMCP's order
  (`/.well-known/openid-configuration{issuerPath}` then
  `{issuerPath}/.well-known/openid-configuration`) via
  `fetchMCPOAuthOIDCDiscoveryFallback`/`mcpOAuthOIDCFallbackURLs`. A 200 with
  valid metadata is accepted; 404/405/503 try the next candidate; 408/425/429
  and other 5xx propagate; any other response preserves the original 503 error
  instead of enabling a lower-priority endpoint fallback; fallback requests do
  not follow redirects (`mcpOAuthNoRedirectClient`). Tests cover candidate
  order, the 503->OIDC success path, the path-suffixed candidate, original-error
  preservation, terminal-status propagation, and the no-redirect rule.
  - Note: Rust implements this inside the rmcp OAuth HTTP adapter (which also
    scopes the fallback to GET + redirect-policy Stop and shares the original
    request timeout); Go's discovery is hand-written, so the fallback lives in
    `discoverMCPOAuthAuthorizationServer` with the same observable behavior.
- `dbe2f6d528` #43513 expose a stable executor build identity - **done
  (exec-server protocol surface)**. New `execserver/build_identity.go` ports
  Rust's `build_id` (SHA-256 of `git:<lowercase 40-hex commit>:<target>`,
  `sha256:<hex>`, unavailable for unstamped/invalid input) plus
  `SetExecServerBuildIdentity`/`SetExecServerVersion` (cached at startup) and
  `ExecServerProviderID`/`ExecServerExecutorVersion`. `EnvironmentInfo` gains
  `executorVersion` (always serialized, `0.0.0` when unknown, matching Rust's
  legacy default) and optional `providerId`, and `InitializeResponse` now
  carries `environmentInfo` alongside `sessionId` as Rust does. Tests cover the
  deterministic build-id vectors (uppercase commit normalization + a second
  target), five invalid/unstamped inputs, the `0.0.0`/omitted-id default, and
  the stamped metadata. `go test ./execserver` and `./parity` (exec-server
  surface) green.
  - Residual: Go's exec-server client does not yet read `executorVersion` /
    `providerId` for behavioral-verification gating (Go still lacks Rust's
    `tempDir` metadata field as well), so the fields are advertised but not
    consumed yet; and Go has no STABLE_GIT_COMMIT stamping, so the id is absent
    unless the host calls `SetExecServerBuildIdentity`.
- `f8ab57359d` #44629 add manual callback input to MCP OAuth login - **done
  (core + CLI flag)**. `mcp/oauth_login.go` gains
  `ParseMCPOAuthCallbackURL`, porting Rust's `parse_callback_url` validation
  (64 KiB bound, no credentials/fragment, every configured redirect query
  parameter present unchanged, no response parameter reusing a redirect
  parameter name) before the flow checks state and exchanges the code;
  `OAuthLoginServer` retains its session/token client/store and gains
  `CompleteWithCallbackURL`, which validates the pasted URL, completes the
  callback, and saves the tokens. The CLI now accepts
  `mcp login <name> --no-browser` (`cli.MCPOptions.NoBrowser`), `app` threads
  stdin into `runMCP`/`runMCPLogin`, and `performMCPCLIOAuthLogin` prints the
  authorization URL, reads one pasted redirect URL (bounded 64 KiB), and
  completes it while the HTTP callback stays live - the pasted reader is reused
  across the discovered-scope retry. Tests cover the parser's valid/oversized/
  fragment/credentials/missing/changed/duplicate/path/provider-error cases, the
  server completion + state mismatch, the app completion helper + bounded input,
  and the `--no-browser` parse. `go test ./mcp ./app ./cli` green.
  - Residual: Go echoes the pasted callback (no hidden terminal input) and the
    discovery-unknown fallback (`service.OauthLogin`, which only prints the URL)
    does not accept manual input; both are cosmetic/edge relative to the
    validation and exchange behavior ported here.
- `3052bbcf8c` #44915 remove the deprecated `thread/rollback` API - **done**.
  Removed the Go app-server surface: `MethodThreadRollback`, the
  `ThreadRollbackParams`/`ThreadRollbackResponse` types, the
  `threadRollbackFailed` error + `rollbackItems` helper (`appserver/protocol.go`),
  the `Router`/`RuntimeRouter` handlers and dispatch, the deprecation-notice
  emission (`appserver/router.go`, `appserver/runtime_router.go` filter-staged -
  voice-owned), and the schema method/signature entries (`appserver/schema.go`
  filter-staged). Requests now fall through to the generic unknown-method
  rejection. Kept the legacy pieces Rust retains: `rollout.ThreadRolledBack`
  markers and the `rollout.rollbackItems` replay trimming, plus the
  `threadRollbackFailed` code as a documented legacy-deserialization-only
  emission gap in `parity/rust_enum_surface_test.go`.
  - L0 re-freeze to `3052bbcf8c`: re-vendored the precomputed exports
    (`stable` 150043 / `experimental` 156251) with new SHA pins, and re-pinned
    the `app-server-protocol/src/protocol/common.rs` hash, the
    `app-server/tests/suite/v2/mod.rs` hash, and the
    `app-server/tests/suite/v2` (143 -> 142) / `core/tests/suite` (186 -> 185)
    fixture counts.
  - Tests: removed the dedicated rollback tests
    (`TestRouterThreadRollbackValidation...`,
    `TestThreadRollbackRejectsActiveTurnLikeRust`, the five
    `TestRuntimeRouterThreadRollback*` runtime tests) and dropped the rollback
    assertions from the retained search/inject/repair tests (renamed
    `TestRouterSearchLoadedTurnsAndInjectItems`,
    `TestRouterInjectItemsRepairRolloutOnlyThread`, whose rollout-replay
    assertion now reads the three repaired items).
  - Verification: `go build ./...` clean; `go test ./appserver -count=1` green
    (88.6s); `go test ./parity/ -count=1` green with `CODEX_RUST_ROOT` at the new
    HEAD; `go test ./tui/...` green.

2. `2c9e1a5775` #44872 Add managed network policy support to the Windows MXC
   sandbox - native `mxc-sandbox` crate (argv/policy translation, MXC lib,
   README). Go's Windows sandbox is a stub and the MXC native library is
   outside the Go port. **status: N/A (native Windows sandbox)**.
3. `7b491281c8` #44877 Return public key metadata from user verification
   enrollment - `userVerification/enroll` gains optional `algorithm` and
   `publicKey` (unpadded base64url SPKI-DER); current servers populate both.
   **status: done (wire contract + round-trip tests)** - Go declares
   `UserVerificationEnrollResponse.Algorithm`/`.PublicKey` as `*string`,
   `algorithm`/`publicKey`, serialized as explicit null when unset (matching
   Rust `Option<String>` with no `skip_serializing_if`). Go still has no native
   provider, so enroll reports the typed `unavailable` error exactly as before;
   the runtime population half remains N/A until a native Go provider exists
   (same platform gap already documented for #43265). Committed as `687cefd`.
   Residual: the vendored experimental precomputed export
   (`appserver/schema/precomputed/app-server-exports-experimental.json.zst`) is
   owned by the parallel voice process and was not refreshed; the Go wire type
   is additive and `go test ./appserver` is green against the current export.
4. `33bdf976cc` #44879 Fade Astra composer stars and stabilize cursor redraws -
   TUI composer animation. **status: deferred to batch-tui-sync31 (TUI last)**.
5. `4dcce4f0c4` #44883 Reject token-budget history notes for unsupported
   starting models - after startup config/model defaults resolve, reject
   `features.token_budget.use_history_notes_extension` when the starting model
   lacks `supports_experimental_context`.
   **status: done (runtime validation + tests)** - `appserver` gains
   `validateTokenBudgetHistoryNotesModel`, called from `appTurnConfig` after the
   token-budget config/model-default resolution and the context-management
   activation override. The context-management path is unaffected because
   eligibility already requires `supports_experimental_context`; the explicit
   extension gate on an unsupported (or unresolved) model now fails the turn
   with the Rust message
   ("... is not supported by model `...`; disable it or select a model that
   supports experimental context"). Committed as `060ad89`.
   Note: Rust raises this at `Session::new` (thread start); Go resolves token
   budget per turn, so the same rejection surfaces on the first turn instead of
   `thread/start`. Behavior (reject) matches; the phase differs by Go
   architecture.

### Untriaged-commit triage (this pass)

Dispositions for the remaining `574a36ff99..c62d191c4c` commits not previously
referenced (non-voice/non-release). No further code changes for these here:
- `434efa95e6` #44311 honor shared `Retry-After` deadlines for remote control -
  **partial (server-api deadline semantics)**. `remotecontrol/server_api.go`
  now accepts an equal deadline (`Retry-After: 0` / an HTTP date equal to receipt
  is a deadline; Rust changed `>` to `>=`) and adds the #44311 jitter
  (`RetryAfterWithJitter`, bounded 0..30s, sampled once per response via the new
  `ServerAPIOptions.RetryAfterJitterMillis` hook) at the response-error
  construction site, so the deadline stays a lower bound while clients spread
  out. Tests cover zero/max/over/negative jitter, the equal-deadline case, and a
  missing header. `go test ./remotecontrol` green.
  - Residual: Rust also shares one observed deadline across enrollment, token
    refresh, pairing, pairing status, and WebSocket handshakes (an
    enrollment-level `retry_at` with `check_retry_after`/`record_retry_after`
    and `WouldBlock` deferral), and honors `Retry-After` on 429/503 handshake
    responses. Go currently tracks retry only on the refresh path
    (`NextRefreshAt`) and builds the remote-control websocket handshake
    separately, so the shared-deadline and handshake halves remain.
- `aa88a0333c` #44248 preserve tool output truncation budgets across resume/fork:
  **blocked by Go architecture** - Rust persists
  `history_truncation_token_limit` on tool outputs and reuses it during replay;
  Go has no per-item history-truncation override (tool output is truncated at
  execution time with a global policy), and `TruncateFunctionOutputItemsWithPolicy`
  has no non-test caller (same root gap as #44867).
- `b64de2f3ad` #44243 / `b4507997e0` #44200 / `8ff4aa8ee4` #44202 /

- `537278c65f` #44489 reset cached WebSocket state on auth ownership changes:
  **done (owner-revision invalidation)** - the auth-identity signal now exists:

  `RuntimeRouter.authOwnerRevisionSnapshot()`. With a cached, router-built

    `go test ./appserver/ -count=1` (92.7s) green.

  **done**. `auth/change_state.go` ports `AuthChangeState`/`same_owner` (as
  `SameAuthOwner`) and `AuthChangeTracker` with Rust's exact
  generation/owner-generation semantics (verified against the upstream
  `change_state_tests.rs` vectors), and `RuntimeRouter.noteAuthChanged` records
  the credential snapshot so `authOwnerRevisionSnapshot()` exposes the ownership
  revision separately from the monotonic `authRevision`.
  - `mcp/auth_changes.go` ports `auth_changes.rs`: the client offers the
    experimental `codex/auth-change` capability during initialization when a
    source is attached, and after initialization it sends
    `notifications/codex/authChanged` with `{generation, ownerGeneration}` (and
    `_meta: {}`) once initially and again on every change, each bounded to 5s.
    A server that does not echo the capability back gets nothing; a send failure
    tears the connection down (Rust's `client.shutdown()`). The watcher is tied
    to the connection and stopped by `Close`/`failTransportFor`.
  - `MCPService.SetAuthChangeSource`/`stdioClientForServer` attach the source to
    stdio clients, and `appserver` wires `routerMCPAuthChangeSource` (backed by
    the router's tracker) through `configureMCPService`, so every MCP service
    (process-wide and per-thread) forwards this router's revisions.
  - Tests: `mcp` covers capability advertisement on demand, server opt-in
    parsing, an end-to-end stdio subprocess receiving the initial `{0,0}` and a
    subsequent `{5,3}` notification, and a non-opted-in server receiving none;
    `appserver` covers the wired source reporting and signalling revisions.
  - Verification: `go test ./mcp/ -count=1`; full `go test ./appserver/ -count=1`
    (100.7s) green; `go build ./...` clean.
  - Residual: Go still marks MCP runtimes dirty on auth change
    (`mcpRuntimes.invalidateAll`), so the connection is rebuilt lazily on the
    next MCP access whereas Rust keeps it and relies on the notification. The
    notification is delivered before that lazy teardown, so opted-in servers
    observe the documented behavior; the extra rebuild is pre-existing Go
    behavior.
- `3436cad5ab` #44238 honor MCP elicitation cancellation + reset state on
  reconnect: **done** - superseded the earlier "already satisfied" reading. Go
  passed the request context to `HandleMCPElicitation` uniformly, but
  `mcpElicitationResult` did not itself answer `cancel` when that context was
  cancelled, and the stdio read loop dropped server `notifications/cancelled`
  frames entirely (no per-connection cancellation memory), so a cancelled
  form/URL elicitation could still wait or report `decline`. See the
  "Audit-backlog triage" section below for the ported behavior and tests.
- `5ac0b8768d` #43947 surface MCP reconnect signals when expired OAuth tokens
  cannot refresh - **done**. `mcp/http_client.go` re-reads the OAuth store after
  a transient provider refresh failure and adopts an unexpired replacement
  credential (matching client id + access token, bounded to the previously
  validated issuer) instead of prompting again, while a failed proactive refresh
  keeps the original error whenever the access token has not actually expired
  (`tokenHasExpired`, real expiry rather than the 30 s refresh skew); a
  replacement refresh token with a missing or changed issuer returns a
  reauthentication-required error. When a `tools/call` would otherwise go out
  unauthenticated because the stored credential is expired and could not be
  refreshed, `doRPC` now returns the typed `mcpAuthenticationRequiredError`
  without sending the request, and `mcpAuthenticationChallengeToolOutput`
  converts it into the Rust reconnect result ("MCP authentication required.
  Reconnect to continue using this server.") with a fixed
  `mcp/www_authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token"` challenge that hides
  provider/transport details. Tests cover the adoption path, still-valid-token
  error path, issuer-changed rejection, expired replacement, `tokenHasExpired`,
  and the no-unauthenticated-tool-call + reconnect-message path. `go test ./mcp`
  green.
  - Note: Go classifies at its own HTTP request boundary (`doRPC` + the typed
    error) rather than through RMCP's `AuthorizationManager`; the observable
    behavior (no unauthenticated tool call, fixed reconnect signal) matches.
- `ce5c4133bd` #43447 route MCP elicitations through the shared approval decision
  path: **N/A (rust-coupled)** - the change reworks the guardian-v2
  `StrictReviewReason`/extension decision path in `core/src/session/mcp.rs`; Go's
  guardian is a synchronous direct-request reviewer with no guardian-v2 extension
  registry (existing guardian-v2 triage).
- `a62e98d18c` #44360 return focus to the agents overview composer on Escape:
  **already satisfied (Go architecture)** - Go's in-session dashboard clears its
  input/rename state on Escape and otherwise closes back to the chat view
  (`agentsoverview.View.Cancel` -> `tea.closeAgentsOverview`), which returns
  focus to the composer with the draft preserved; the production dashboard is
  created with `exitOnCancel=false`, so Rust's removed `exit_on_cancel` app-exit
  path has no Go equivalent.
- `02d4529f55` #43315 resolve session labels uniquely before acting on them:
  **re-assessment: the earlier "already satisfied" note was wrong.** Go's
  interactive picker (`findSessionByIDOrName`) does report duplicates, but the
  session *commands* still targeted the most recent duplicate: the remote queue
  lookup (`lookupRemoteSessionUniqueByName`), the remote resume/archive lookups
  (`lookupRemoteSessionByExactName`, which also matched via the server's
  `search_term`), and the local store lookups (`sessionIDByName`,
  `sessionIDByUniqueActiveName`) all preferred the newest match. Now ported - see
  the "unique session label lookup" section below.
- `4f1a2bb5ff` #43545 preserve fork runtime versions without loading full model
  context: **already satisfied (Go storage architecture)** -
  `session.Store.ForkRecord` copies the source record (including
  `Metadata.MultiAgentVersion`) rather than reconstructing from rollout
  segments, so there is no cutoff that can drop the version and no segment scan
  to bound; covered by
  `TestStoreForkPreservesMultiAgentVersionAcrossCutoffLikeRust`.
- `3dc1e2a584` #44255 always use streamed remote compaction for supported
  providers + `1ac689cc7d` #44273 remove the unused legacy remote compaction:
  **already satisfied / N/A (Go architecture)** - Go's
  `agentCompactRunner.Compact` always issues a regular `model.AgentRunner`
  Responses request and extracts the summary, so remote compaction is always the
  streamed path; Go has no legacy `codex-api` compact endpoint client to switch
  away from or delete (no `/responses/compact` or `endpoint.Compact` references
  outside the TUI's `/compact` slash command).
- `c1f1467f30` #43873 handle undefined values before JSON serialization in code
  mode: **already satisfied** - Go's Sobek code-mode runtime documents and
  implements both halves in `tool/code_mode_exec.go`: `store` panics with the
  serializability error for `undefined` and leaves the previous value untouched,
  and a tool call whose first argument is `undefined` is treated as omitted
  (`argument := map[string]any{}`), so the invocation serializes as `{}`.
- `f31bd3adff` #43897 / `f046cf35df` #43906 persist and scope model catalog
  caches to the current provider and auth identity: **done** (first pass in
  `a47bd50`, faithful port in `ModelsCatalogIdentity` - see "Non-TUI core:
  faithful provider/auth identity for the model catalog cache" below, which
  replaces the earlier provider-id + credential-hash approximation).
- `94e4b3d0bd` #43895 preserve `__oailb` routing cookies in ChatGPT HTTP
  clients: **done**. Go's `execserver/cloudflare_cookies.go` allowlist now
  includes `__oailb` (OpenAI infrastructure routing, not auth), keeping its
  non-`cf_*` name handling explicit. Test mirrors Rust's scope/expiration
  coverage: replay on a matching path, rejection outside the cookie's host, path,
  and HTTPS scope, and removal via `Max-Age=0`. `go test ./execserver` green.
- `9d83c48e5c` #43894 preserve thread identity in code-mode tool dispatch
  traces: **N/A (no tracing span layer)** - the change records `thread.id` and
  the `exec-` call ID on the Rust `dispatch_tool_call_with_code_mode_result`
  tracing span; Go has no OpenTelemetry/tracing span layer to attach fields to
  (same disposition as #43913).
- `6d377e96eb` #43900 propagate Apps tool refreshes to existing threads:
  **already covered / architecture-different** - Go's `app/installed`
  force-refresh already routes through the thread-scoped `codex_apps` binding
  (`MCPService.Refresh`), so the next turn on the same thread consumes the
  refreshed catalog (#43039). Rust's change is a process-global, account/home/
  transport/protocol-scoped live catalog with revisions and prepared-call
  rejection; Go owns one service+binding per loaded thread
  (`mcpRuntimeCoordinator`) and has no cross-client catalog revision to publish,
  so there is no equivalent live-propagation layer to port.
- `44ab72674e` #43870 close MCP stderr readers on client teardown: **done**.
  Go's stdio client sets `exec.Cmd.WaitDelay = 250ms`
  (`mcpStdioStderrDrainGrace`), so teardown drains queued stderr diagnostics for
  a short grace period and then closes the pipes even when an escaped descendant
  keeps them open, releasing descriptors instead of blocking on EOF; `Wait`
  returning `exec.ErrWaitDelay` is treated as a successful shutdown rather than
  an error (`waitMCPStdioCommand`). New Unix regression test
  (`mcp/stdio_stderr_cleanup_test.go`) launches a shell whose descendant holds
  stderr open, asserts teardown completes promptly, and verifies the queued
  diagnostic was drained. `go test ./mcp` green; the Unix test typechecks under
  `GOOS=linux CGO_ENABLED=0 go vet ./mcp`.
  - Note: Go expresses Rust's watch-signaled drain + explicit reader close as
    `WaitDelay` (which bounds the pipe drain and closes the pipes), so the
    observable behavior matches without a manual stderr reader goroutine.
- `6515a72db7` #43848 preserve runtime workspace roots across thread resume:
  **partial - core restore/dedupe present, refinements pending**. Go already
  restores stored roots (`threadRecordRuntimeWorkspaceRoots`: explicit override >
  stored `runtime_workspace_roots` on the record > cwd), cleans and dedupes them
  (`threadRuntimeWorkspaceRoots` with `sameAppPath`), persists explicit resume
  overrides (`applyThreadResumeRuntimeWorkspaceRoots`), and carries
  `RuntimeWorkspaceRoots` in thread settings snapshots (`thread_extra.go`).
  Still missing versus Rust: prefer the latest owned *settings snapshot* over
  startup/record metadata, retarget a saved cwd root to the resume cwd
  (`replace_path_and_deduplicate`), reject restored paths that are invalid for
  the current host with the Rust error message, checkpoint effective settings on
  resume/restore after revert, and normalize Windows rollout path spellings when
  matching search results after revert. These live in the resume/revert flow
  (`runtime_router.go` is voice-owned) and need a dedicated pass.
- `c1840dc55e` #44283 / `7c88f037d9` #44299 / `e1b23086ac` #44314 managed-daemon
  thread persist/recovery/restore cluster (plus `#43959`/`#43943` shutdown
  admission, `#43308` Windows shutdown socket requests, `#43562`/`#43542`/
  `#43529` daemon update commands): **pending (large app-server/daemon feature)**
  - a coordinated ~1.1k-line addition across `app-server`, `app-server-daemon`,
  and `app-server-transport` (daemon snapshots, thread-recovery candidates,
  restart restore). Go's `appserverdaemon` has a simpler lifecycle; this needs a
  dedicated batch rather than a partial edit.
- `3715bf4100` #44613 / `ca6fb194b6` #43568 / `b7ad941b1f` #43547 / `3cd6004dc4`
  #43289 / `ad931a45b2` #43265 user-verification provider/native bindings:
  **N/A (no Go provider)** - Go never advertises `elicitation.userVerification`
  because its platform provider is unsupported (existing #43265/#44877 residual).
- Native voice runtimes / voice SDK packaging (`#43144`/`#43126`/`#43125`/
  `#43121`/`#43117`/`#43114`/`#43111`/`#43109`/`#43102`/`#43099`/`#43097`/
  `#43090`/`#43083`/`#43079`), Bazel build plumbing (`#43444`/`#43325`/
  `#43304`/`#43282`), docs/README removal (`#43421`/`#43423`), and test-only
  fixtures (`#42933`/`#42931`): **N/A** per the earlier category triage.

- `9ec33e1926` #43921 streaming reasoning summaries - **partial (status-heading
  core)**. `tui/chatwidget/streaming.go` (a clean file) now mirrors Rust's
  `latest_summary_line`: `LatestSummaryLine` picks the last non-empty,
  non-`<!--` line, strips leading Markdown heading marks, and unwraps a
  *leading* `**bold**` span (an unterminated span falls back to an earlier
  usable line). `OnAgentReasoningDelta` streams that latest line as the status
  heading while keeping the previous heading when the buffer has no usable
  line, `RestoreReasoningStatusHeader` prefers the latest line but retains the
  previous heading before falling back to `Working`, and `OnAgentReasoningFinal`
  keeps the last useful summary through later tool activity instead of clearing
  it. Tests cover the line-selection rules and the retained heading across
  delta, section-break, finalization, and restore. `go test ./tui/...` green.
  - Residual (voice-owned files / wiring): Rust's reasoning item identity
    tracking (`reasoning_item_id` / `reasoning_resume_turn_id` /
    `reasoning_recovered_after_refresh`) and the restore guards
    (safety-buffering wait, unified-exec wait streak, compaction, pending
    guardian review) live on the ChatWidget/StatusState and its protocol
    handlers (`chatwidget/turn_runtime.go`, `status_control` wiring) plus the
    resume/buffered-replay paths (`tui/app/*`) - all in the in-flight
    voice-owned set - so they are not ported here. The reasoning default
    (`model_reasoning_summary = "detailed"` for new embedded threads), summary
    persistence in compaction checkpoints, and the shimmer animation remain
    pending for the same reason.
  - Follow-up landed (request-side summary resolution): the app-server turn
    request now resolves its reasoning summary from the effective
    `model_reasoning_summary` config value when the turn does not override it
    (`turn_reasoning_summary` in `appserver/turn_runtime.go`), matching Rust
    `TurnContext::reasoning_summary`; an explicit turn `summary` still wins. This
    was a pre-existing gap (Go only read `params.Summary`, so config-file values
    never reached the request). Test:
    `TestRuntimeRouterTurnReasoningSummaryResolvesConfigLikeRust`
    (config -> request, explicit override wins); full `go test ./appserver/`
    green.
  - Follow-up landed (embedded default + exec config parity): `exec` never
    populated `ReasoningSummary` at all, so `model_reasoning_summary` was ignored
    by the whole exec runner; `agentRunConfig` now sets
    `ReasoningSummary: effectiveReasoningSummary(cfg)` (matching Rust's
    config-resolved summary and the app-server fix above). On top of that,
    `app.interactiveEmbeddedReasoningOverrides` mirrors Rust's
    `new_thread_reasoning_overrides` for the embedded TUI - `detailed` unless
    configured, and `features.concurrent_reasoning_summaries` = explicit opt-in
    AND summaries are not `none` - and `runInteractiveTurn` appends those
    overrides to the exec turn's `ConfigOverrides`, so the embedded TUI's turns
    carry them (Go re-resolves config per exec turn, so it applies to every
    interactive turn rather than persisting a thread-start snapshot).
  - Tests: `exec` `TestRunAgentRequestUsesConfiguredReasoningSummaryLikeRust`
    (config -> request) and `app`
    `TestInteractiveEmbeddedReasoningOverridesLikeRust` (default detailed,
    configured value honored, concurrent opt-in, `none` disables it).
    Verification: `go test ./exec/ ./app/ -count=1` green; `go build ./...`
    clean.
  - Follow-up landed (live status row): the working indicator now shows the
    latest usable streaming reasoning summary line for both TUI transports.
    - `protocol.ReasoningSummaryDelta` (`item.reasoning.delta`) is a new internal
      stream event; `exec`'s stream collector emits it (plus a section break on
      `response.reasoning_summary_part.added`) via `emitInternal`, so the
      `codex exec --json` contract is unchanged (Rust's exec JSON does not emit
      reasoning deltas either).
    - `tui/tea` handles `item.reasoning.delta` by accumulating per item and
      setting the working indicator's header from
      `chatwidget.LatestSummaryLine`; `turn.started`/`turn.completed` clear it.
      A new `WorkingStatusHeaderMsg` lets the remote client drive the same
      header.
    - The remote TUI client handles `item/reasoning/summaryTextDelta` and
      `summaryPartAdded`, publishing the latest usable line and clearing the
      accumulated buffer at turn boundaries.
  - Tests: `tui/tea` (`WorkingStatusHeaderMsg` drives/clears the indicator,
    another thread's update is ignored; internal reasoning deltas drive it and
    clear on completion), `exec` (reasoning deltas reach the internal handler
    and never the JSON events), and `app` (remote summary accumulation, latest
    usable line, comment-only line retention, turn-boundary clear).
    Verification: `go test ./exec/ ./protocol/ ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green;
    `go build ./...` clean.
  - Follow-up landed (resume/switch restore + provisional trailing reasoning):
    `remoteTUIThreadActiveReasoning` detects the trailing reasoning item of an
    in-progress turn, and `remoteTUIThreadMessagesFromThread` skips it as
    committed history; the resume and agent-switch responses carry its latest
    usable line as `WorkingStatusHeader`, which `applyResumeResponse` /
    `applyAgentSwitchResult` seed into the working indicator (after clearing the
    live buffer). Completed turns keep rendering their reasoning in the
    transcript. Tests: `TestRemoteTUIThreadActiveReasoning` and
    `TestResumeAndSwitchSeedReasoningHeading`. Verification:
    `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green; `go build ./...` clean.
  - Residual: Go applies the embedded reasoning defaults per interactive turn, so
    a resumed older TUI thread also gets `detailed` where Rust's resume path uses
    plain overrides; the grapheme-aware summary shimmer still needs the startup
    terminal probe wired for default fg/bg; Go's MCP-startup header still takes
    precedence over the reasoning header (`mcpStartupActive`), whereas Rust keeps
    them on separate rows; and Rust's fuller replay-buffer identity tracking
    (`reasoning_item_id`/`reasoning_resume_turn_id`) maps only to the
    snapshot-based restore above, since Go's tea transcript is not buffer-driven.

- `0818b6550b` #44857 theme-based thread colors - **done (identity core +
  agents overview + session/fork picker titles)**. New `tui/thread_color.go`
  mirrors Rust `thread_color.rs`: `ThreadColorPalette(themeID)` derives the
  active chroma style's accents (every style entry's foreground, excluding the
  base text/background/comment/punctuation colors and entries with a special
  background), caching per style name with `ResetThreadColorPaletteCache` for
  theme changes; `ThreadColorIndex` uses an explicit FNV-1a over the thread id
  (not Go's process-random map hash) and `ThreadColorForTheme` returns the
  palette slot as `#rrggbb` (empty -> terminal default). `ThreadColorSGR`
  encodes the truecolor foreground sequence. The agents-overview core
  (`tui/agents_overview`) gains `View.UseThemeColors` /
  `View.ThreadColor` and renders row + task-detail titles with the identity
  color (`span.color`), and the tea layer wires the resolver to the live
  `m.tuiTheme`. The session picker (`tui/resume_picker.go`) gains
  `UseThemeColors` (default true, matching Rust `PickerState::new`) and
  `ThemeID`; comfortable and dense rows color only the title text (marker and
  indent stay unstyled, and the color survives selection) via
  `sessionTitleColor`/`colorThreadTitleLine`; the tea picker passes the theme.
  Tests: FNV-1a index vectors + palette stability/uniqueness + SGR parsing,
  agents-overview styled-vs-plain width parity and color suppression, and
  picker comfortable/dense coloring plus suppression. `go test ./tui/...`
  green.
  - Residual **closed this pass (status-line colors + config reader)**:
    `bottompane.StatusLine.RenderStyled` now renders the spans as ANSI text -
    theme-derived per-accent colors (with the cyan/green/magenta fallback
    palette), the per-thread identity color for `ThreadTitle` spans, dim
    separators, and the underlined PR number - so the TUI no longer drops the
    spans through `StatusLineText = line.PlainText()`. `tui/tea`
    (`status_line_color.go`) derives each accent from the active syntax theme's
    token colors and applies Rust's RGB softening (85% saturation toward luma;
    `#ff0000` -> rgb(228,11,11), matching the upstream test), falling back to
    the accent palette when the theme has no color. `tui.status_line_use_colors`
    now has a Go config reader (`app.interactiveStatusLineUseColors`, default
    true) threaded through `SettingsWriteResult`/`Options` into the status line
    (and its setup preview), the agents overview, and the session picker, so
    those flags follow settings instead of defaulting on.
  - Tests: bottompane style/render vectors (accent + fallback + thread identity
    + dim/underline + theme-disabled), tea softening/theme-resolver vectors, the
    model option/settings-result/seeding paths, `renderStatusHeader` producing
    (and, when disabled, not producing) truecolor spans, and the app config
    reader. Verification: `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green; `go build
    ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app/` clean.
  - Residual: the status-preview `thread_id` field is still not carried into
    `StatusSurfacePreviewData`, so the setup modal's preview does not apply the
    thread identity color to a thread-title item (the live header does).
- `40b0409aa1` #44732 clarified folder-trust prompts + restricted widget
  support - **done (widget state and rendering)**. `TrustDirectoryPrompt` gains
  `Restricted`; the normal disclosure is now the Rust copy ("Trust this folder?
  ... Folder settings can run code automatically ... Your trust decision will
  be saved."), the Git-root subdirectory note is hidden when restricted, and
  the restricted disclosure explains that project-local config/hooks/exec
  policies stay disabled while skills still load and opening does not change
  trust. Option labels become "Open restricted"/"Trust and continue" plus
  "Quit", and the footer reads "Press Enter to continue; esc to quit" (the
  Windows create-sandbox hint is suppressed when restricted). Tests cover the
  revised normal prompt and the restricted rendering/selection.
  - Residual (Go architecture): the onboarding trust widget is still a model
    that the real CLI flow does not drive - `app/remote_trust.go` performs the
    interactive y/N prompt and persistence - so Rust's "restricted acceptance
    does not persist trust" guard and the restricted-state wiring from
    #44746/#44755 have no Go call site yet. This port covers the widget's
    rendering/selection; the wiring remains for the folder-consent work.
- `84e7d4a1fe` #44746 / `02a8f038b8` #44755 folder-consent widget surface -
  **done (widget state/labels/wording)**. `TrustDirectoryPrompt` gains
  `ExistingTask` and `Cancel` (`TrustCancelQuit` /
  `TrustCancelAgentsOverview`). The restricted disclosure now uses the #44746
  wording ("Config, hooks, and exec policies from untrusted folders stay
  disabled. Trusted project folders can still contribute settings... Opening
  will not change saved trust."), an untrusted *resumed* task gets the distinct
  existing-task disclosure and "Open existing task" label, and connected
  sessions label the escape action "Back to Agent Command Center" with
  "esc to go back" (embedded sessions keep "Quit"/"esc to quit"). Tests cover
  the revised restricted prompt, the existing-task variant, and the
  cancel-action labels.
  - Residual (Go architecture): the `check_directory_trust` /
    `confirm_directory_trust` flows (checking selected + saved cwd, local-daemon
    thread re-read after consent, config reload + startup-hooks review, returning
    to the Agent Command Center on cancel, and the `read_remote_project_trust`
    saved-key/trust-target precedence) are not ported: they live in the Rust TUI
    startup/task-navigation flow, while Go's interactive trust decision is the
    console y/N prompt in `app/remote_trust.go` (`ensureRemoteProjectTrust`).
    Porting the flow requires moving that prompt onto the TUI onboarding screen,
    which touches the in-flight voice-owned TUI files (`app/remote_tui.go`,
    `tui/tea/model.go`) and is deferred until that work lands.
- `742472c52` #44298 guardian turn trigger (previously-flagged non-TUI core
  residual) - **done**. `modelGuardianReviewer.Review` now builds its
  `ClientMetadata` through `codexapi.NewClientMetadata` (wired via a new
  `RuntimeRouter.guardianInstallationID` hook), attributing the review to the
  `guardian_review` turn trigger and emitting `x-codex-turn-metadata` with the
  parent thread/turn identity, alongside the preserved `x-openai-subagent:
  guardian`, `parent_turn_id`, and `target_item_id` keys. Test asserts the
  header's `turn_trigger` plus the compatibility keys.
  - Residual: `guardian_classifier` (guardian-v2 async scorer) has no Go
    equivalent, so only the `guardian_review` trigger is emitted; the detached
    memory `memory_consolidation` trigger was already done.
- `333c41eef6` #43558 completion timestamps in the TUI - **partial (final-message
  cell + live completion, including plain answers)**. The
  `history_cell.FinalMessageSeparator` now carries
  the turn's local completion time (`CompletedAt`, with `DisplayDate` fixed at
  construction; `WithCompletedAt`/`WithRuntimeMetrics`/`Label` expose it) and
  renders dim completion metadata instead of a horizontal
  rule: `Worked for 1h 1m 40s` (only above 60s, full h/m/s precision) plus
  `done 2:32 PM` (date added for other local days, year for other years) plus
  runtime metrics, joined with `·` and word-wrapped with a two-space indent;
  absent metadata occupies no rows. Live emissions attach the local clock
  (`tui/tea/transcript.go` and `chatwidget.TurnRuntimeState.OnTaskComplete` for
  non-replay completions), and the affected Go tests were updated to the new
  rendering. Committed as `7b2bb69`.
  - Follow-up `84ba4e8`: the active tea TUI now emits exactly one completion
    footer per successful turn (including plain conversational answers) at
    `applyTurnCompleted`, and the mid-turn `needsFinalMessageSeparator`
    insertion was removed from the assistant stream/final paths, matching
    Rust's single completion cell. Failed and interrupted turns still get no
    footer.
  - Follow-up `c9ed5d5`: the remote TUI client captures the completed turn's
    protocol `durationMs` and the completion msg carries it into the footer's
    elapsed seconds (shown only above 60s as `Worked for h m s`).
  - Residual (remaining #43558 work): replay/resume does not yet restore saved
  - Follow-up `d23c7b2`: resume now restores saved completion metadata.
    `remoteTUIThreadMessagesFromThread` appends a completion footer after each
    completed turn's items using the turn snapshot's saved `completedAt` and
    `durationMs` (which the app-server already carries via `applyTurnSnapshot`);
    failed and interrupted turns get no footer, and a completed turn with no
    saved metadata renders nothing (replay never falls back to the local clock).
  - Residual (remaining #43558 work): Rust groups completed turn items and
    deduplicates footers across repeated notifications and overlapping pages via
    `rendered_completion_turn_ids`. Go builds the resumed transcript from a
    single full thread read (`remoteTUIReadThread(..., true)`), so within-call
    duplicates do not arise; an explicit cross-page dedup set is still missing
    if paginated turn pages are appended incrementally in the future.
- Reasoning-effort override: shared core + exec entry point, and paginated
  persistence (non-TUI residual, closed this pass).
  - `reasoningoverride` (new package) holds the pin (`Pin`/`PinKind`), the
    trusted-update decision (`EffortForConfigurationUpdate`,
    `LatestTrustedUpdate`, `OverrideInputItems`) and the request-effort
    resolution (`RequestEffort`). `appserver/reasoning_effort_override.go` now
    delegates to it (RuntimeRouter keeps its per-thread map via a type alias,
    so `runtime_router.go` is unchanged and appserver behavior is identical).
  - `exec` now applies the feature: it keeps a per-thread pin, resolves the
    override + pinned request effort in `execReasoningEffortOverride`, carries
    trusted `configuration_update` items as post-prompt inputs, and persists
    them as harness-authored history (`execConfigurationUpdateSessionItem`).
    Clearing on resume matches Rust's re-establish rule.
  - Latent bug found and fixed while wiring exec: paginated rollouts had no way
    to persist a harness-authored `configuration_update` (no core TurnItem
    variant), so app-server threads on paginated history would have failed the
    turn once the feature was enabled. `rollout` now persists them as trusted
    `response_item` lines in the incremental (`session_items.go`,
    `turn_item.go`) and canonical-rewrite (`canonicalize.go`) paths, matching
    Rust's history envelope. `session.InputItemFromItem` now applies the
    configuration-update trust check before its raw-payload fast path, so a
    replayed trusted update is no longer dropped as untrusted.
  - Tests: exec override pinning/gating, exec end-to-end turn (request carries
    the update and the session record replays it as harness-authored), shared
    package gating via both callers, and a paginated-rollout round-trip.
    `go test ./exec ./rollout ./session ./app ./appserver` green. Committed as
    `3dad22d`.
- L0 static layer re-frozen for the current upstream target `e3a52b87b2`: the
  Rust checkout was fast-forwarded (`main` was 6 commits behind, so parity had

## Newly discovered non-TUI core gap: reasoning-effort override runtime

While cross-checking the range, the reasoning-effort override cluster was found
unported and previously only referenced by the single `#44276` note in
plan_2026_09_11.md. It is non-TUI core, so it takes priority over the TUI pass.

Upstream commits (oldest -> newest):

- `56a8470aa0` #43110 Record reasoning effort changes in conversation history
  behind a flag: adds `reasoning_effort_override` (UnderDevelopment, off) for
  OpenAI models with `use_responses_lite`; appends a trusted
  `configuration_update` after accepted input when no effort is established in
  surviving history or the resolved effort changes; shares normalization via
  `ModelInfo::resolve_reasoning_effort` (ultra resolution, `persistent` ->
  `disabled`, custom values other than `disabled` excluded).
- `31ccaf40c2` #43795 Pin request reasoning effort while overrides are active:
  per-session `ReasoningEffortPin` (Unset / Active{model, effort}) establishes
  the request baseline for the current model, including during WebSocket
  prewarm; re-established after rollback/resume/model switch.
- `35d9e4bc4d` #43796 Preserve reasoning effort through compaction and reset it
  on success: local and remote compaction use the pinned request effort when it
  matches the model, fallback-model resolution must not mutate the live pin, and
  a successful compaction resets the pin so the next sampling request
  establishes the selection without a redundant `configuration_update`.
- `5d3f8752fc` #44285 Preserve prewarmed reasoning effort across replay and early
  rollback: history reconstruction keeps the pin while startup prewarm is
  retained; rollback clears it only after prewarm is consumed.
- `f5c5d9b2b0` #44276 Avoid duplicate reasoning effort updates during turn
  recovery: reuse a matching harness-authored `ConfigurationUpdate` at the end
  of history even when the runtime pin is unset.

Go current state (evidence):

- The feature key exists: `features/features.go` line 184
  (`reasoning_effort_override`, UnderDevelopment, default off).
- The trust/replay plumbing from sync30 already exists: `session.Item` with
  `harness_authored_configuration` metadata, `session.harnessAuthoredConfigurationUpdate`
  / `configurationUpdateInputItem` (session/history.go), and
  `appserver.trustedConfigurationUpdateSessionItems` (turn_runtime.go:4134)
  which persists internal `AdditionalInputItems` as trusted updates.
- Normalization exists privately as `model.reasoningEffortForRequest`
  (model/responses_agent.go:1081) for ultra resolution, with `persistent` ->
  `disabled` handled at the request-build call site.
- **Missing**: no producer that decides to append the update, no
  `ReasoningEffortPin` runtime state, no request-pin usage, and no
  compaction/prewarm/replay integration.

Integration points / hazards discovered:

- Selected effort is resolved by `appReasoningEffortForTurn`
  (turn_runtime.go:157) and flows as `appTurnRunConfig.ReasoningEffort`
  (turn_runtime.go:6303) into `turn.AgentLoopRequest.ReasoningEffort`.
- Request input assembly (turn_runtime.go:1354) appends
  `params.AdditionalInputItems` **before** the user message
  (`preparedUserMessageInputItem`/`Prompt`), whereas Rust appends the override
  **after** accepted input. A faithful port must place the trusted
  `configuration_update` after the accepted user message in both the request
  input and persisted session items (`sessionItemsForTurn` currently persists
  trusted updates at index 1, i.e. after the user message).
- Per-thread state lives in `RuntimeRouter` (`active`, `diffs`,
  `startupPrewarms`, ...); the pin would be a sibling per-thread map keyed by
  model slug, with reset hooks on rollback, resume, model switch, and
  successful compaction.
- Compaction paths to update: `compactThread` / `midTurnSamplingCompaction`
  (appserver) and `execMidTurnSamplingCompaction` (exec).

Status: **done (core sampling path)** - committed as `068c2a0`
`sync31 (#43110/#43795/#43796/#44285/#44276): reasoning-effort override pin,
update recording, and compaction reset`. Implemented:

- `model/reasoning_effort.go`: `ResolveReasoningEffort` (shared ultra resolution
  + `persistent` -> `disabled`) and `IsKnownReasoningEffort`; the request path
  (`responsesReasoningParam`) now routes through the shared helper.
- `appserver/reasoning_effort_override.go`: `reasoningEffortPin`
  (Unset / Compacted / Active{model, effort}), per-thread pin storage with lazy
  map init, `effortForConfigurationUpdate` (feature + Responses Lite + OpenAI
  provider + known-effort gating), `reasoningEffortOverrideInputItems` (record
  decision with pin and trusted-tail reuse for recovery), and
  `reasoningEffortForRequest` (sampling pins; compaction reuses the pin without
  mutating it; unavailable overrides clear the pin).
- Ordering fidelity: `appTurnConfig` records the override *before* pinning the
  request baseline, then the pinned effort becomes
  `appTurnRunConfig.ReasoningEffort`.
- `model.AgentRequest.PostPromptInputItems` + `turn.AgentLoopRequest.PostPromptInputItems`
  append the trusted `configuration_update` after the prompt's user message
  (first request only, then folded into accumulated input), matching Rust's
  history order. `runTurnRuntime` moves configuration updates out of the
  pre-prompt `AdditionalInputItems` append into the post-prompt channel while
  still persisting them through `sessionItemsForTurn` (index after the user
  message).
- Pin lifecycle: cleared on rollback and `thread/resume`, retired to
  `Compacted` after a successful compaction.
- Tests: pin semantics, record/skip/recovery decisions, request-pin behaviour,
  gating (feature/provider/Responses Lite/custom effort), model normalization,
  post-prompt ordering in `model`/`turn`, and appserver helper tests. Full
  `go test ./appserver ./turn ./model -count=1` green.

Residual (documented): the compaction *usage* half of #43796 (local/remote
compaction sampling the pinned effort and resolving fallback-model effort
without mutating the pin) is not wired, because Go resolves compaction effort
through a different path; only the post-success reset is implemented. Rust
#44285's prewarm/replay pin preservation is N/A in Go: the Go startup prewarm
does not send a reasoning effort at all, so there is no prewarmed baseline to
preserve. The `exec/` runner is not wired (Go's exec path does not use the
trusted-configuration-update plumbing).

Repo-integrity note (pre-existing, voice-owned): the committed base does not
independently build. `a47bd50` captured `runtime_router.go` references to
`realtime.NotificationItemStarted` / `ItemStartedNotification` / ... whose
definitions live in the parallel process's uncommitted (untracked)
`realtime/history_wiring.go` and `realtime/history.go`. The working tree builds
and all focused suites pass, but a `git worktree add HEAD` build fails on those
symbols. This is voice-owned per the operator instruction, so it is left for the
parallel process to land the realtime definitions.

Note: the parallel voice process is concurrently editing `turn_runtime.go`,
`runtime_router.go`, and other shared files, so this batch should be landed with
the same filtered-hunk staging discipline used for #44883.

### `c62d191c4c` #44905 expose disabled plugin settings in the app-server API - done

- Protocol surface: `SettingsUpdateParams.disabledPluginIds` (`*[]string`, so
  omission/null preserves and `[]` clears), `Settings.disabledPluginIds`
  (always serialized as an array), and `disabledPluginIds` on
  `ThreadStartResponse` / `ThreadResumeResponse` / `ThreadForkResponse` and
  `turn.TurnStartParams`.
- Behavior: `ThreadExtraService.UpdateSettings` replaces the saved selection
  when supplied; `turn/start` overrides flow through
  `turnStartSettingsUpdateParams` / `applyTurnStartSettingsUpdate` (including
  the `thread/settings/updated` notification, whose `threadSettings` now carries
  the selection). `applyThreadSettingsToTurnStartParams` carries the saved
  selection into turns unless the caller overrides it, and
  `persistThreadSettingsUpdate` stores it in the thread record
  (`metadata.extra.disabled_plugin_ids`) so resume restores it; the start /
  resume / fork responses read the persisted value
  (`disabledPluginIDsFromRecord`, preferring live settings).
- The TUI client's `ThreadSettingsUpdateHasChanges` counts a
  `disabledPluginIds`-only update as a change.
- Tests: service-level replace/preserve/clear and JSON null/`[]` semantics,
  plus a router-level test covering the settings-update notification, resume
  restore, fork inheritance, and clearing.
- L0: Rust checkout fast-forwarded to `c62d191c4c`; precomputed exports
  re-vendored (stable 150559 / experimental 156708) with new SHA pins, and the
  `exec/src/lib.rs` (field-literal only) and
  `app-server-protocol/src/protocol/common.rs` critical-file hashes re-pinned.
  `go test ./appserver ./parity ./app` and `./tui/...` green (the earlier
  pre-existing `./app` remote-turn failures no longer reproduce). Committed as
  `8c390f0`.
- Residual: Rust restores the selection from the history retained at the
  *requested fork boundary*; Go's fork clones the parent's current saved
  selection (`forkMetadata` copies the record extra), so a fork at an earlier
  turn boundary inherits the parent's current selection rather than the
  boundary's. Go stores a single current value, not per-turn settings history,
  so exact boundary restoration needs a per-turn settings history first.


## Non-TUI core audit refresh + `#44472` (this pass)

### Audit correction (evidence-based)

Several "pending" dispositions in earlier sections are stale: the code landed in
commits that referenced the upstream **commit hash** instead of the PR number, so
PR-number scans miss them. Verified as already implemented:

- `#43897`/`#43906` model catalog cache provider/auth identity scoping: landed in
  `a47bd50` (`model.ModelsCatalogIdentity`, `RemoteModelsManager.identity`, identity
  gating in `tryLoadFreshCache`/`RenewCacheTTLIfNeeded`, `catalog_test` coverage).
- `#44241` credential provider source remapping across config layers: landed in
  `config/api.go` (`credentialProviderSourceOverride`,
  `pruneDisplacedCredentialProviderOrigins`, `validateCredentialProviderWriteResult`)
  plus `network.ParseCredentialProviderConfigs`.
- `#44318` hosted Codex Apps independent MCP protocol opt-in: landed in `cf23744`
  (`features.codex_apps_mcp_2026_07_28`, `mcp.HostOwnedAppsProtocolMode`,
  `hostOwnedAppsProtocolModeFromValues`).
- `#44250` retired model entries: Go loads `models-manager/models.json` from the Rust
  checkout directly and already carries `upgrade`/`migration_markdown` metadata, so the
  retired entries drop out with upstream data; the migration prompt is TUI-scoped.
- `#44288` hook stdin drain/timeout: Go's `os/exec` drains stdin/stdout/stderr
  concurrently and `appserver/hooks_runner.go::runCommand` applies the deadline to the
  whole process tree, so the Rust pipe deadlock/hang cannot occur.
- `#44619` discarded Code Mode responses: Rust-only V8 handle-scope fix; Go uses Sobek.
- `#44121` reuse MCP bindings while dormant: Rust's per-connection catalog-revision
  binding cache has no Go analog (Go owns one service+binding per loaded thread).
- `#44138` rollout compression writer coordination: Go has no background rollout
  compression worker; replacement paths serialize through `rollout.RolloutMaintenanceGuard`.
- `#44175` diagnostic rollout attachments: Go has no feedback/diagnostic-attachment package.

Genuinely open non-TUI core after the refresh: `#44472` (this pass), the managed-daemon
thread persist/recovery/restore cluster (`#44283`/`#44299`/`#44314`), and the
"blocked by Go architecture" items already recorded above.

### `03f014564d` `#44472` harden Code Mode tool-call completeness tracking - done (recorder core)

Ported into the Go recorder that mirrors Rust's `state::ExecutedToolCalls`:

- New `turn/seen_ids.go` ports `SeenIds`: a bounded (8K-word) four-probe ID filter over
  namespaced call IDs and runtime cell IDs, append-only (bits are never cleared), with
  the `observe_history` budgets (100k items / 4 MiB of IDs / 1 KiB per ID) and the
  `unobserved_history` state. Additive `model.AgentItem.ExecutedToolCallCellID` exposes
  the originating cell for history indexing.
- The recorder indexes the thread's supplied history on its first prompt. The first
  sampling request precedes this turn's tool calls, so its input is the Go equivalent of
  Rust's `InitialHistory`; supplying history also disables wait-completeness proofs, since
  inherited runtime handles cannot be distinguished from new ones after resume/fork.
- Reused evidence revokes completeness, and revocation is sticky until the affected output
  commits: repeated direct/nested call IDs, repeated exec/wait wrapper origins
  (`observeWrapperOrigin`/`observeOrigin`), repeated runtime cell IDs (observed once per
  cell so exec+wait on one cell is not mistaken for reuse), duplicate output call IDs in
  one prompt, and an ID that is both a direct call and a cell output. Truncation and limit
  overflow revoke only the affected cell, preserving unrelated cells.
- Completeness now also requires indexed history (`historyIndexed()`), and wait
  (`function_call_output`) completeness is withheld once history was supplied.

Tests: `turn/seen_ids_test.go` (freshness, namespace independence, oversized IDs,
unobserved history, history byte budget, ID extraction across item shapes) plus new
recorder regressions (reused origin, duplicate output, wait-after-history withheld, fresh
wait on a new thread allowed, historical output ID revoked). Verification: `gofmt` clean;
`go build ./...`; `go test ./turn ./model ./exec ./appserver ./parity -count=1` green
(appserver 88s, parity 10.9s with `CODEX_RUST_ROOT`).

Residual (Rust `#44472` halves not yet ported):
- `refresh_recording` on runtime-config reload: Go's recorder has no live refresh hook.
- Retained per-output calls (`retained_calls`), the full
  `Unobserved/Started/Recording/Incomplete/Complete` cell lifecycle, and the exec/wait
  input-output association validation (`code_mode_input_matches_output`): Go computes
  completeness per attach and has no start/finish-cell hooks, so completeness is
  approximated by "no revoked evidence" (matches Rust on the traced cases, not on every
  association-validation edge).
- The resume/fork wait-completeness signal uses "supplied history carried call IDs" as a
  proxy for Rust's `InitialHistory` variant, so a compacted history without call items
  would still allow wait proofs.

### `#44472` residual closed: exec/wait input-output association validation

Follow-up to the recorder-core port above (committed `83c5075`):

- `turn/executed_tool_calls.go` now builds Rust's `input_indices` map over the prompt
  (None on duplicate input call IDs) and, for every cell-backed output, withholds
  completeness unless the matching input is present before it and satisfies
  `codeModeInputMatchesOutput`:
  - a `custom_tool_call_output` must be explained by a `custom_tool_call` with the same
    call ID named `exec` (Code Mode's default-namespace exec tool); and
  - a `function_call_output` must be explained by a `function_call` with the same call ID
    named `wait` whose `arguments.cell_id` equals the runtime cell.
- Input extraction (`executedToolCallInputInfo`) covers `*model.AgentItem`,
  `*ToolResponseItem`, raw `map[string]any`, and the trusted map carrier, matching the
  prompt shapes the recorder already handles. The Rust origin guard is implied because
  the matching input shares the output's call ID while the cell origin is the exec ID.
- Tests updated/added: the cell-completeness test now supplies the exec input (as real
  request input does), and new regressions cover missing input, mismatched wait cell,
  non-exec custom input, and a direct `codeModeInputMatchesOutput` table (map shapes,
  call-ID mismatch, wrong tool name, output-shape mismatch, unsupported input).
  Verification: `gofmt` clean; `go build ./...`; `go test ./turn ./model ./exec
  ./appserver ./app ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 88.9s, app 11.9s, parity 10.7s).

Residual now limited to: `refresh_recording` on runtime-config reload, the full
`Unobserved/Started/Recording/Incomplete/Complete` cell lifecycle, retained per-output
call snapshots (`retained_calls`) across replay/pruning, and the resume/fork
"history carried call IDs" proxy for `InitialHistory`.

- `c18277043e` #44930 embed friendly instructions in bundled GPT-5.4 and GPT-5.5 -

### Remaining non-TUI dispositions refreshed (evidence)

- Managed-daemon thread persist/recovery/restore cluster (`#44283`/`#44299`/`#44314`):
  **architecture-different (no equivalent gap)**. Rust's snapshot exists because loaded
  threads can hold *deferred* rollout state and an in-memory registry that must be saved
  before the daemon exits. Go's `session.LiveThread` is only a persistence handle (store +
  optional cross-process writer lock); thread records are written eagerly by
  `Store.Save`/`AppendItems`, `ThreadManager.CloseLiveThreads` closes the writers on
  `Router.Close`, and `thread/list` re-reads the store. A grep for deferred rollout
  materialization finds only the SQLite history projection (`MaterializeThreadHistory`),
  not deferred rollout files. Clients resume threads explicitly by ID after restart, so
  there is nothing to snapshot.
- `#44655` honor thread-level plugin exclusions: **pending, needs voice-owned wiring**.
  `RuntimeRouter.threadDisabledPluginIDs` exists but has no callers, and the per-thread
  capability assembly points that would consume it live in the concurrent voice-owned
  files `appserver/runtime_router.go` (hooks) and `appserver/turn_runtime.go` (skills,
  `pluginSkillEntriesAndErrorsForRuntime`), plus the MCP config assembly. The `plugin`
  package filtering helpers are free files, but wiring them without the callers would be
  inert, so this waits for a coordinated pass.
- `#43953` per-image `generation_id` in image-generation analytics: **partial gap, tied to
  a broader analytics field set**. Go's `CodexImageGenerationEventParams` carries only
  `revised_prompt_present` / `saved_path_present` / `transparent_background`; it also lacks
  the older `imagegen_request_id` (#40714), so adding only `generation_id` would be a
  fragment. Faithful alignment first needs the image-API response fields and request-ID
  plumbing.

### `8570091e14` `#44655` honor thread-level plugin exclusions - partial (skills + MCP servers)

Go persisted the per-thread `disabled_plugin_ids` (#44905) but never applied it:
`RuntimeRouter.threadDisabledPluginIDs` had no callers. This pass wires the
selection into the two capability surfaces that are thread-scoped in Go:

- Skills: `pluginSkillEntriesForRuntime` / `pluginSkillEntriesAndErrorsForRuntime`
  now take the thread ID and drop roots whose `PluginID` is disabled
  (`filterDisabledPluginSkillRoots`). The turn path
  (`instructionsWithSkillsContextForTurn`) and the skills-metadata path pass the
  thread; the threadless `skills/list` RPC passes `""`.
- MCP servers: `runtimeMCPConfigForThread` filters plugin MCP contributions by the
  thread's selection (`filterDisabledPluginMCPContributions`); the process-wide
  `runtimeMCPConfig` keeps every contribution and the per-thread managed service
  builder uses the filtered config. Thread MCP runtimes are already invalidated on
  thread-settings and turn-start updates, so a changed selection rebuilds with the
  new filter.
- Tests: `TestRuntimeRouterDisabledPluginSkillRootsFilteredLikeRust` and
  `TestRuntimeRouterDisabledPluginMCPContributionsFilteredLikeRust` cover thread
  scoping and clearing. Verification: `go build ./...`; `go test ./appserver ./app
  ./exec ./turn ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 90s).
- Staging: the edits in the voice-owned `appserver/runtime_router.go` and
  `appserver/turn_runtime.go` were committed via a filtered patch (`git apply
  --cached -C1`) containing only these hunks, leaving the parallel voice WIP
  unstaged.

Residual (rest of `#44655`): plugin hooks (Go's hook discovery is router-scoped,
so `configureHookDiscovery` has no thread to filter by), app tools contributed only
by disabled plugins, plugin recommendations, the post-turn activation timing
(`active_disabled_plugin_ids`, so a pending settings change only takes effect on the
next task), and plugin identity in MCP approval keys.

### `#44655` follow-up: app tools contributed by disabled plugins

- Added `filterDisabledPluginCapabilities` / `RuntimeRouter.pluginCapabilitiesForThread`
  and wired the thread-scoped capability call sites (`appsForExplicitMentions`,
  `instructionsWithPluginContext`, `executorSkillRootPluginIDs`,
  `skillMetadataForMCPRequirements`), so connectors contributed only by a disabled
  plugin leave that thread's model app-tool catalog. Connectors shared with an enabled
  plugin survive through that enabled capability; the threadless `app/list` RPC and the
  tool-provenance annotator keep the full set (existing direct Apps behavior).
- Test `TestDisabledPluginCapabilitiesFilteredLikeRust` covers the shared-connector rule.
- Residual (superseded by the follow-ups below): plugin hooks and recommendations are now
  thread-filtered, `active_disabled_plugin_ids` activation timing is implemented, and plugin
  identity is in the MCP approval key path; only provenance annotation for threadless tools
  stays as documented behavior (threadless RPCs intentionally keep the full set).

### `#44655` follow-up: plugin hooks

- `configureHookDiscoveryForThread` filters plugin hook sources by the thread's
  disabled plugins (`filterDisabledPluginHookSources`), and `hooksForCWD` /
  `compactHooksForCWD` / `interruptHooksForCWD` now take the thread ID so
  session-start, session-end, interrupt, and compaction hook discovery exclude
  disabled plugins. The threadless `hooks/list` RPC keeps every source.
- Test `TestDisabledPluginHookSourcesFilteredLikeRust`.
- Residual: plugin recommendations, `active_disabled_plugin_ids` activation timing,
  plugin identity in MCP approval keys, and provenance annotation for threadless tools.

### `#44655` follow-up: plugin recommendations

- The thread's disabled selection now feeds the discoverable-candidate filter:
  `pluginDiscoverableConfigForTurn(threadID, cfg)` appends
  `threadDisabledPluginIDs(threadID)` to `DiscoverableConfig.DisabledPluginIDs`, which
  `plugin.ListDiscoverablePlugins` already uses to exclude disabled plugins. The turn
  recommendation path (`recommendedPluginInputItems`) and `prepareTurnToolInputs` thread
  the ID through.
- Test `TestRuntimeRouterPluginDiscoverableConfigIncludesThreadDisabledPluginsLikeRust`.
- Residual **closed**: `active_disabled_plugin_ids` activation timing is implemented -
  `activateThreadDisabledPluginIDs` records the selection at task admission
  (`ThreadExtras.SetActiveDisabledPluginIDs`) and `threadDisabledPluginIDs` prefers it, so a
  pending settings change takes effect on the next task
  (`TestRuntimeRouterActiveDisabledPluginIDsDeferToNextTaskLikeRust`); and plugin identity in
  MCP approval keys is implemented by `mcpToolApprovalKeyPath` persisting plugin-contributed
  servers under `plugins.<id>.mcp_servers...`
  (`TestMCPToolApprovalKeyPathLikeRust`). #44655 is now fully covered.

### `#44655` residual closed: task-start activation timing

- A pending settings change no longer alters the running task's plugin selection. The
  admitted task's selection is recorded when the turn starts
  (`prepareTurnStartParams` -> `activateThreadDisabledPluginIDs`, preferring an explicit
  turn override and otherwise the persisted thread snapshot), and
  `RuntimeRouter.threadDisabledPluginIDs` (read by the skills, MCP, apps, hooks, and
  recommendation filters) prefers that active snapshot, falling back to live settings or
  the record when no task has activated one.
- `ThreadExtraService` stores the active selection per thread
  (`SetActiveDisabledPluginIDs` / `ActiveDisabledPluginIDs` / `ClearActiveDisabledPluginIDs`);
  unloading and resume clear it so a cold read re-derives the thread's saved settings.
- Tests: storage contract plus a router-level deferral test (a pending change does not
  leak, the next task activates it, and unload falls back). Verification:
  `go test ./appserver ./app ./turn ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 89.8s).
- Residual from `#44655`: plugin identity in MCP approval keys.

### `#44655` residual closed: plugin-scoped MCP tool approvals

- `persistMCPToolApprovalAmendment` now persists a plugin-contributed server's approval
  under `plugins.<plugin_id>.mcp_servers.<server>.tools.<tool>.approval_mode` (the plugin
  is resolved with `mcpServerPluginID` over the enabled MCP contributions) instead of the
  global `mcp_servers` section; codex-apps approvals stay connector-scoped and plain
  servers keep `mcp_servers`. The key-path computation is extracted as
  `mcpToolApprovalKeyPath` for testability.
- Test `TestMCPToolApprovalKeyPathLikeRust` covers all three targets and the missing
  connector-id case. Verification: `go build ./...`;
  `go test ./appserver ./app ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 89.9s).
- Residual: the approval key itself does not carry plugin identity, so two plugins that
  contribute the same server name would share an approval key. Rust keys on the
  invocation's plugin id (dormant servers may not be enumerable); Go resolves the
  contributor from the enabled contributions at persistence time.

This closes the actionable parts of `#44655` in Go: plugin skills, MCP servers, app tools,
hooks, recommendations, task-start activation timing, and plugin approval persistence.

- New `config.GlobalInstructionsManager` mirrors Rust's `CodexHomeUserInstructionsProvider`
  state: a failed refresh retains the last successful global snapshot, a confirmed absence
  (removed or blank source) clears it, and warnings are reported once per failure episode
  (suppressed while the same failure continues, re-reported after recovery).

  (`threadStartInstructionParts`) and records them on the thread

  while keeping the combined `baseInstructions` for compatibility.

  applied instructions; unchanged sources are left untouched, and a removed or blank global
  source clears them so model/config instructions take over.
- `turn.AgentLoopRequest.InstructionsProvider` lets a long turn refresh instructions for every
  model request: the app-server passes an AGENTS.md-derived provider (nil for an explicit
  caller override or a subagent), so edits within a turn also take effect.

## Upstream advance (12e82f44f8) and L0 re-freeze

the TUI - TUI-scoped, deferred with the TUI pass). The removed

`go test ./parity` is green at the new head.

### `cc05ecfe17` `#44669` resolve filesystem denials with explicit path context - partial (resolution + validation + globs)

- New `config.resolveFilesystemDenyReadPaths` mirrors Rust's filesystem-denial resolution:
  relative literals and relative glob prefixes resolve against the owning requirements
  layer's base directory, glob shapes are preserved, entries are deduplicated in
  declaration order, and invalid spellings (empty, NUL bytes, non-string entries, a
  relative path with no base) fail configuration loading instead of being silently
  skipped. `LoadRequirementsFile` resolves against the requirements document's directory,
  and cloud requirements layers resolve against their own base directory.
- `sandbox.ParseDenyReadPath` classifies a resolved denial as a literal path or a
  `glob_pattern` (previously globs were mangled into literal paths) and rejects empty or
  NUL-bearing patterns. `managedDenyReadEntriesFromPerms` now uses it and returns an
  error; `handleCommandExec` propagates that as an invalid-request JSON-RPC error rather
  than silently dropping managed denials.
- Tests: config resolution/validation/dedup/globs/document base dir; app-server conversion
  (glob preservation, map form, invalid error). Verification: `go test ./config ./sandbox
  ./appserver ./app ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 90s).
- Residual: Go does not model Rust's `ConfigPathContext` convention/home facts, so
  cross-host resolution (`#44676`), `~` expansion, Windows/POSIX convention selection, and
  per-layer path context remain unported; the sandbox boundary still applies the host-native
  convention (a POSIX denial is rejected on a Windows host).

### Path-context and credential-broker triage (this pass)

- `#44676` resolve permission profiles with explicit execution-host path context -
  **architecture-different**: Go has no per-environment path-convention model
  (`EnvironmentRecord` carries no OS/convention facts), so there is no execution-host path
  context to resolve against. `#44669`'s convention/home facts remain unported for the same
  reason; its resolution, validation, and glob-preservation halves are done.
- Shell-snapshot credential cluster (`#44038` / `#44040` / `#43907` / `#43909` / `#43954` /
  `#44072`): **pending (large)**. Go's `shell` package has no credential handling at all
  (no protected snapshots, credential env scrubbing, or replay filtering), and the Rust
  commits build on the broker's protected-snapshot/credential-env machinery; a faithful port
  is a dedicated multi-file effort rather than a partial edit.

- Correction to the shell-snapshot credential disposition above: **architecture-different /
  inert**, not a pending large port. `git grep` shows every `shell.SnapshotFile` /
  `BuildPOSIXSnapshot` / `StripSnapshotPreamble` / `SnapshotPath` reference outside the
  package is a test, and nothing else in the tree implements a shell-snapshot capture or
  restore pipeline (only the `shell_snapshot` feature flags exist). Credential protection
  for snapshots has no runtime home until that pipeline is integrated, so the cluster stays
  documented rather than ported.

### Discovered non-TUI core gap: Codex Apps auth elicitation is unwired in Go

While porting `#44938`: the `mcp` package exposes `ConnectorAuthFailureFromToolResult`,
`BuildAuthElicitationPlan`, and the new `IsConnectorAuthFailureFromToolResult`, but `git grep`
shows no non-test consumer, and no outbound agent-initiated connector auth elicitation exists
(`MCPElicitationRequest` is only constructed from incoming server params in
`mcp/elicitation_runtime.go`). Rust wires this in `core/src/mcp_tool_call.rs` (gated on the
approval policy): resolve the connector install URL (`apps.ConnectorInstallURL` already exists
in Go), build the plan, send an MCP server URL elicitation, and retry the tool call on accept.
That integration came from `f9a907aebe` (#19193, before the current range), so it is a
pre-existing gap rather than part of this range; it is queued as the next non-TUI core item
(tool dispatch + elicitation + retry).

### `f9a907aebe` `#19193` wire Codex Apps auth elicitation into the MCP tool executor - done (executor + gating)

Go had ported the auth-elicitation plan helpers but never triggered them. Now:

- `mcp.ToolExecutor` accepts `AuthElicitationOptions` (Request / RefreshCodexApps / InstallURL) and
  a connector name. After a Codex Apps tool call result, `Execute` calls
  `maybeRequestCodexAppsAuthElicitation`, which builds the plan (`BuildAuthElicitationPlan` plus
  `apps.ConnectorInstallURL`), asks the client to complete the connector URL elicitation through the
  service's elicitation handler, and on acceptance refreshes the Codex Apps catalog and returns
  `AuthElicitationCompletedResult`; otherwise the original result is returned unchanged.
- `turn.ToolRegistryOptions.MCPAuthElicitation` carries the hooks into each MCP executor, and the
  app-server enables them only when the `auth_elicitation` feature is on and the approval policy
  allows agent-initiated MCP elicitations (`mcpElicitationsAllowedForApproval`: `never` off;
  `granular` requires `mcp_elicitations`; the remaining policies allow them).
- Tests: executor request/accept/decline/non-Codex-Apps behavior plus the approval gate.
  Verification: `go test ./mcp ./turn ./appserver ./app ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 89.5s).
- Residual: Go lists MCP tools per turn, so a newly authorized connector's tools appear from the next
  turn rather than mid-turn (the refresh invalidates the service while the turn's registry is fixed),
  and Go's accessible-connectors provider is pull-based rather than explicitly cache-refreshed.

## Upstream advance (d43f1e7eb2)

Rust checkout fast-forwarded `c210f4c222..d43f1e7eb2` (`#44942` clarify the Windows Visual C++
runtime notice for voice packages - `third_party/voice/NOTICE.md` only, voice-owned release docs).

### `#44341` remote-control authentication owner - assessed (pending, large)

Go's `remotecontrol` already reacts to auth revisions (`websocket_loop` reconnect reason
`auth_changed`, `markRecoveryAuthChangeSeen`, `replaceWebsocketEnrollmentIfMatches`,
`clearRemoteControlServerTokenIfMatches`), but it *recovers* rather than *retires*: a logout or
identity change leaves the loop reconnecting with the new (or absent) auth instead of tearing the
session down, clearing replay/client/enrollment state, and leaving remote control disabled until it
is enabled again (Rust's `remote_control/{controller,desired_state,persistence}.rs` rework). Neither
logout path (`handleLogoutAccount`, `handleAccountSessionsLogout`) touches remote control today.
Faithfully porting this needs a dedicated pass over Go's websocket loop/state, enrollment and
preference persistence, and the transport server (stale message/RPC rejection, session generation,
persistence permits drained on shutdown).

### `#44341` remote-control authentication owner - partial (retire on logout/account switch)

- `remotecontrol.Manager.RetireForAuthChange(ctx)` tears down a live session when its
  authentication owner goes away: it persists the disabled preference against the retired
  owner's stored enrollment (logout has already cleared the live auth, so an auth-requiring
  disable cannot run), clears the in-memory enrollment/client/pairing/environment state, and sets
  the desired state disabled, which stops the websocket loop via `waitUntilEnabled` until the
  user enables remote control again.
- The app-server retires remote control after `account/logout`, `account/sessions/logout`, and
  `account/sessions/switch`, publishing the resulting status change.
- Tests: manager retirement clears state and is a no-op when disabled; the app-server helper
  disables a live session and publishes the notification; the logout RPC disables a live session.
  Verification: `go test ./remotecontrol ./appserver ./app ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 91.1s).
- Residual (`#44341`): same-owner token refreshes should preserve the live relay connection (Go
  reconnects on any auth-revision change); stale incoming messages/queued RPCs are not rejected on
  owner change; a replacement session does not start with a fresh replay cursor or client tracker
  (they belong to the app-server's websocket loop); enrollment/preference persistence is not
  serialized with permits drained on shutdown.

## Upstream advance (39d193d72d) and #44944

Rust checkout fast-forwarded `d43f1e7eb2..39d193d72d` (`#44944` enforce managed provider
requirements on existing app-server threads - app-server + config, non-TUI core). Parity pins were
re-frozen for the new app-server v2 suite module (`app-server/tests/suite/v2` 142 -> 143 files,
`mod.rs` hash) and `go test ./parity` is green at the new head.

### `#44944` enforce managed provider requirements on existing threads - done

Existing threads retain the model provider route they were admitted with, so a managed
requirements change must invalidate further input until Codex restarts.

- `config.LoadManagedRequirements(codexHome, cloudBundle)` loads managed requirements
  independently of system defaults, user/project settings, and thread configuration. The legacy
  `managed_config.toml` still participates to the same extent as Rust's
  `requirements_layers_from_legacy_scheme`: a malformed file fails the load, while its
  approval/sandbox-only content never contributes model provider policy.
- `model.RequiredModelProviderDefinition` resolves one managed `model_providers` entry into the
  required definition. Bedrock ids merge onto the bundled provider (Rust's
  `merge_configured_model_providers(built_in_model_providers(None), ..)`); every other id is used
  verbatim. A malformed or incomplete definition (for example a custom provider without `name`)
  is an error, matching Rust's `model_provider_requirements::to_config` validation.
- `RuntimeRouter` records each live thread's route (`ThreadManager.SetModelProviderRoute`) at
  `thread/start`, `thread/fork`, and `thread/resume`, resolving the provider id and definition
  from the thread's effective configuration exactly as a turn would.
- `RuntimeRouter.checkThreadModelProvider` compares the retained route against current managed
  `model_provider` (selection) and `model_providers[retainedID]` (definition, with Bedrock
  overrides resolved). Mismatches and requirement load failures surface as invalid requests
  (`-32600`) with the Rust message
  `failed to load configuration: Your organization's required model provider settings changed.
  Restart Codex to apply them; this request was not sent`.
- The check is wired into `turn/start`, `turn/steer`, `review/start` (inline and detached),
  `thread/compact/start`, `thread/queue/start`, and active `thread/goal/set` updates.
  `thread/goal/set` only checks when the resulting status is active, so pausing, completing, or
  clearing a goal (and interrupt/realtime stop) stays available. Detached reviews fork the parent
  thread, so they already inherit its provider route.
- Tests: retained route capture, selection change, definition change, unrelated/local config
  changes, invalid requirements and incomplete definitions, Bedrock override resolution, and a
  boundary test that exercises all seven gated entry points plus goal pause/clear and the
  unchanged goal after rejection.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go test ./config ./model ./appserver ./app ./turn ./session
  ./parity -count=1` green (appserver 88s).
- Residual: Go has no `LoaderOverrides.ignore_managed_requirements` and no cloud config bundle in
  the app-server, so the check reads `CODEX_HOME/requirements.toml` plus the legacy managed config
  path; cloud-managed provider requirements are not part of the Go app-server's config model.
