`tui/system_motion.go` mirrors Rust's `tui/src/system_motion.rs`: the host motion preference is read
once per process and cached (`SystemMotionMode`), and `EffectiveAnimations(configured)` applies it
to a configured `tui.animations` value without changing saved configuration (Rust's
`LocalSettings::with_system_motion`). `tui/system_motion_windows.go` queries
`SystemParametersInfoW(SPI_GETCLIENTAREAANIMATION)` like Rust's windows-sys path;
`tui/system_motion_other.go` reports the preference as unavailable on other hosts, which preserves
the configured behavior per Rust's stated contract. Tests cover reduced/animated/unavailable
resolution and the read-once cache.

### `095ac4f131` #42369 keep SQLite history projection moving past invalid records - done

Rust's SQLite history projection used to defer or reject malformed/unknown/ordinal-anomalous
rollout records, which could block later valid history from materializing. Go's
`rollout.readProjectionStepsData` mirrored the old behavior (a `pendingRejected` counter plus hard
errors for missing ordinals, regressed ordinals, and unexplained gaps). It now matches Rust:

- Malformed JSON, unrecognized records, and lines without an ordinal are skipped while the byte
  checkpoint advances; unknown records leave their ordinal available so a later valid retry can
  reuse it.
- A duplicate or regressed ordinal is skipped; a forward ordinal gap becomes a
  `SkippedOrdinalRange` step without the old "rejected lines must cover the gap" requirement.
- A `validate` failure (for example an invalid fallback timestamp) skips that ordinal range and
  keeps projecting; blank lines always advance the offset.
- Rust's OpenTelemetry anomaly counters are not ported (Go has no metrics exporter, matching the
  earlier metrics dispositions).
- Tests updated to the new semantics: `TestReadProjectionStepsSkipsUnknownRecordsAndKeepsOrdinalLikeRust`,
  `TestReadProjectionStepsSkipsUnexplainedOrdinalGapLikeRust`,
  `TestReadProjectionStepsAdvancesPastBlankAndMalformedLinesLikeRust`,
  `TestMaterializeThreadHistorySkipsUnknownRecordsAndKeepsOrdinalLikeRust`, and
  `TestMaterializeThreadHistorySkipsInvalidFallbackTimestampLikeRust`.
  Verification: `go test ./rollout ./state -count=1` green; a focused app-server
  history/resume subset is green; `go build ./...` clean.

### `637c3227b3` #42324 avoid executing PATH helpers before workspace trust - partial (trusted helper discovery + automatic plugin git)

Automatic startup work and `codex doctor` can run before a workspace is trusted, so a
repository-controlled `PATH` must not be able to make those flows execute workspace-provided
helpers. This pass ports the reusable core and its highest-risk caller:

- `envutil/trusted_executable.go` mirrors Rust `codex-utils-path::system_executable` /
  `system_path`: `TrustedExecutable(name)` searches only conventional OS/package-manager
  directories (per-OS lists, Windows `ProgramFiles`/`System32` Git layouts, WSL `/mnt/c`), never
  PATH/PATHEXT/cwd; the canonicalized result must live inside the trusted directories or
  installation roots and be an executable file, names containing a path separator are rejected,
  and `TrustedSystemPath()` returns a PATH built only from those directories.
- `plugin/marketplace_install.go` automatic marketplace Git now resolves `git` through
  `TrustedExecutable` (failing with "no Git executable found in trusted installation
  directories") and runs it with a hardened environment: `PATH` rebuilt from trusted
  directories, `GIT_EXEC_PATH`/`GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR`/`DEVELOPER_DIR` removed, plus the existing
  isolated git config and `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath=1` (Rust #42324's Git command
  hardening). User-requested (non-automatic) marketplace git keeps the ambient PATH.
- Tests: `envutil` trusted-directory resolution, path-containing-name rejection, symlink escape
  rejection, and trusted-PATH membership; `plugin` isolated-env test extended for the trusted
  PATH and removed Git/developer overrides. Verification: `go test ./envutil ./plugin ./app
  ./appserver -count=1` green; `go build ./...` clean.
- Residual (#42324): Rust also makes terminal detection environment-only, resolves tmux/terminal
  helpers from trusted directories (falling back conservatively), has `codex doctor` inspect
  executable locations without running them, and fetches update metadata over HTTP instead of
  `curl`. Those doctor/terminal/update halves remain queued.

- Follow-up pass closed the terminal-detection and most of the `codex doctor` halves:
  - `shell/terminal_detection.go` is environment-only (Rust `terminal-detection` crate): the
    `Environment` interface lost `TmuxClientInfo`/`ZellijVersion`, so detection no longer runs
    `tmux display-message` or `zellij --version` from PATH. A `TERM_PROGRAM=tmux` value is
    skipped so the underlying terminal is recovered from environment probes (new
    `GHOSTTY_RESOURCES_DIR` -> Ghostty check before WezTerm), and the zellij version comes only
    from `ZELLIJ_VERSION`. Tests mirror Rust's `tmux_preserves_safe_underlying_terminal_identifiers`
    and the reworked tmux-multiplexer expectations; the client-termtype/termname and
    command-version tests were removed with the helpers.
  - `doctor/doctor.go` no longer executes PATH-selected helpers: the npm installation/update
    checks report `npm update target: not inspected (PATH helpers are not executed)` (the
    installation check still warns when `CODEX_MANAGED_PACKAGE_ROOT` provenance is missing), tmux
    diagnostics report `tmux options: not inspected (PATH helpers are not executed)`, and
    `codexPathEntriesForDoctor` lists PATH candidates in-process
    (`codexPathCandidatesForDoctor`, deduplicated, PATHEXT-aware on Windows) instead of running
    `which -a`/`where`. Update metadata was already fetched through the HTTP client, not `curl`.
  - Verification: `go test ./shell/ ./doctor/ -count=1` green; `go build ./...` clean;
    `gofmt -l` clean.
  - `doctor`'s git check (`gitCheck` / `gitCheckFromInputs`) now inspects executable locations and
    repository metadata without running Git: it drops the `git --version` / `--exec-path` /
    `version --build-options` / `rev-parse` / `config` probes and the old-Git-for-Windows warning,
    adds `git execution: not inspected (PATH helpers are not executed)`, and reports the summary
    `git executable found; execution not verified` (or `git executable not found`). The
    `GitVersion`/`GitExecPath`/`GitBuildOptions`/`Branch`/`CoreFSMonitor` inputs and their
    parsing/normalization helpers are removed; the no-git-executable warning is kept. Tests
    updated accordingly. Verification: `go test ./doctor/ -count=1` green.
  - Residual (#42324 after this pass): the TUI tmux keyboard-mode conservative fallback
    (`tui/src/tui/keyboard_modes.rs`) is TUI-scoped and deferred with the TUI pass.

- `781c183c3b` #42623 bound Noise handshakes by the exec-server initialization timeout -
  **ordering already satisfied; diagnostics-only residual**. Go's `dialNoiseHarnessConnection`
  completes the authenticated Noise hybrid-IK handshake synchronously before returning the
  connection, and `initializeClientConnection` only then sends the JSON-RPC `initialize`, so the
  Rust fix's primary ordering guarantee already holds; a handshake failure closes the websocket
  via `closeOnError`, matching Rust's transport termination. Every production caller bounds the
  dial with a context deadline (`appserver.environmentConnectTimeout`, default 10s = Rust's
  `DEFAULT_REMOTE_EXEC_SERVER_INITIALIZE_TIMEOUT`; `tool/unified_exec.go` 10s; the recovery loop's
  `unifiedExecRemoteRecoveryTimeout`), so a stalled handshake cannot hang. Not ported: Rust's
  dedicated `InitializeTimedOut` diagnostic that keeps reporting the caller's configured duration
  after the handshake consumed part of the budget and splits the handshake/initialize RPC into
  child spans - Go maps connect+initialize into a single context timeout and surfaces the context
  error, so the diagnostic text/span split is an architecture difference rather than missing
  behavior.
- `801ca0d0d1` #42606 support trusted headers for remote exec WebSockets - **N/A (no Go
  consumer/option surface)**. In the pinned upstream `RemoteEnvironmentOptions` is only
  constructed with headers by tests; the sole production upsert passes an empty
  `http_headers`, and the loopback-direct dial plus the `wss://` requirement only activate when
  headers are non-empty. Go registers remote environments through `EnvironmentAddParams`,
  `AddNoise`, and `RegisterDeferredNoiseEnvironment`, none of which expose embedding-host HTTP
  headers, and `dialNoiseHarnessConnection`/`dialInitializedClientConnection` dial the
  registry-issued URL. The API (`RemoteEnvironmentOptions`, header validation/redaction,
  loopback-only DNS) should be ported only alongside a Go embedding-host header surface.

### Additional triage (this pass)

- `ea2046f36d` #42668 cancel remote control enrollment on stdio shutdown - **architecture-
  different / largely covered**: Go keeps no Rust-style child shutdown token; on stdio EOF the
  server closes the remote-control backend before/around draining in-flight RPCs, and
  `TestStdioServerEOFClosesRemoteControlBackendLikeRust` verifies the backend store is released.
  Go's enrollment is not a pending network RPC inside the stdio drain path, so the Rust
  interruption mechanism has no direct counterpart.
- `312709252d` #42384 add an RMCP OAuth credential store adapter - **N/A (test-only,
  RMCP-internal)**: the adapter and its tests are `#[cfg(test)]` scaffolding for RMCP-owned OAuth
  refreshes; Go's hand-written OAuth has no RMCP `CredentialStore` seam to adapt.

- The question text is bounded to 512 bytes at a UTF-8 character boundary (`floorCharBoundary`,
  Rust `str::floor_char_boundary`).
- Line breaks and carriage returns are flattened to spaces; `Body` renders `"> <question>\n\n"`.
- Markers are empty (unmarked fragment), role is `user`, and the content kind is
  `user.answered_question`, matching `ContextualUserFragment::type_markers` and `content_kind`.
- Tests: bounded/Unicode-safe rendering against Rust's expected string and line-break flattening.
- Residual (wiring): Rust renders the framing when an async question is answered
  (`tui/src/bottom_pane/async_questions/state.rs`). Go's `tui/request_user_input.go` still returns
  structured per-question answers, so the framing has no consumer until the async-question TUI
  redesign is ported. `go test ./context` green.

### `12e82f44f8` #44935 personality selection removal from the TUI - done

Rust removed the `/personality` command, selection popup, tooltip, and the associated settings
update/persistence handlers. Go's TUI removed the same surface (net -393 lines, 23 files):

- Command surface: `tui/slash_command.go` drops the `personality` frame, `tui/state.go` drops the
  `CommandPersonality` constant and the `/personality` parse arm, and `tui/tea/model.go` drops the
  dispatch case, so the command is neither offered nor recognized. `app/interactive.go` loses the
  plain-shell `/personality` handler and parser.
- Flag plumbing: `BuiltinCommandFlags` / `CommandPopupFlags` lose `PersonalityCommandEnabled` (and the
  Rust-order/description tables lose `personality`), with the `slash_input`, `slash_popup`, and
  `command_popup` mappings removed.
- Selection popup + handlers: `chatwidget.NewPersonalityPopup` (+ its view/option types,
  `PersonalityLabel`, `PersonalityDescription`, `normalizedPersonality`),
  `NewPersonalitySelectionPopup`, `SettingsFeaturePersonality` / `SyncPersonalityCommand`, and the
  `ModalKindPersonality` branch are gone. `tui/tea/settings_commands.go` loses
  `openPersonalityMenu`, `applyPersonalityCommand`, `parsePersonalityArgument`,
  `applyPersonalityModalOption`, `setPersonality`, and the personality settings-write kind/result
  handling; `tui/app/thread_settings.go` loses `ThreadPersonalitySettingUpdateParams`.
- `initialPersonality` no longer invents a Friendly default: the model carries the configured
  personality (or nothing), matching Rust's removal of the implicit Pragmatic default in #44946.
- Kept deliberately: the `personality` protocol/config field, `State.Personality`, and the
  config-derived turn override, since Rust keeps `config.personality` and the app-server protocol
  surface; only TUI-initiated selection is retired.
- Verified: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./...`; `go test ./app ./tui/... ./context ./model -count=1`
  green (all 25 TUI packages). The four voice-held files (`tui/slash_command.go`, `tui/state.go`,
  `tui/tea/model.go`, `tui/tea/modal.go`) were committed with the same filtered-hunk staging
  discipline so the parallel process's `voice` command WIP stays unstaged.

### `d30f9cc72a` #43279 linked worktrees in session discovery - partial (git-utils fix + picker wiring)

### `973dcd80fc` #43942 safe managed-worktree removal - core done (TUI browser still queued)

Rust's worktree crate gained a user-facing `WorktreeManager::remove(source_cwd, root)` that refuses
the current checkout (including path aliases) and any checkout with ignored local files before
running a non-force `git worktree remove`. Go only had the internal force cleanup used by `exec`,
which could delete a dirty or even current checkout:

- `worktree/manager.go` adds `RemoveManaged(sourceCWD, root)`: it resolves the checkout through
  `List` (so unrelated paths are rejected), canonicalizes the source cwd and checkout root and
  refuses to delete the checkout the source lives in (`pathWithin`), rejects a checkout containing
  ignored files (`git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard -z`), then runs
  `git worktree remove` without `--force` (so dirty/untracked files are refused) and cleans up the
  empty allocation bucket. `Remove` remains the force cleanup for `exec`-owned worktrees.
- Test `TestWorktreeManagerRemoveManagedSafety` covers the unrelated-root, current-checkout,
  symlink-alias (skipped when symlinks are unavailable), ignored-file, and clean-removal paths.
  Verification: `go test ./worktree/ -count=1` green.
- Residual (TUI half of `#43942`/`#43069`): Rust's managed worktree browser/picker (owner titles,
  relative update times, archived/unavailable owner states with resume-only-when-resumable, and the
  delete confirmation defaulting to Cancel) is not ported, so nothing calls `RemoveManaged` from the
  TUI yet; managed-worktree creation for interactive sessions/forks on a blocking worker with
  source-session revalidation also remains queued.

The parallel process's bulk commit `b7d2366` landed the `git-utils` portable core
(`gitutil/worktree.go`: `RepositoryIdentity`, `RepositoryIdentityForCWD`, `LinkedWorktreeCWDs`) along
with in-progress picker wiring. This pass fixed a real bug in that core and closed it with tests:

- `gitutil.readGitPath` now resolves an absolute `gitdir:`/`commondir` value correctly. Git writes
  those as absolute paths (with forward slashes on Windows), and Rust's `Path::join` replaces the
  base for an absolute argument while Go's `filepath.Join` concatenates. Without the fix, every
  linked checkout failed identity validation, so `LinkedWorktreeCWDs` only ever returned the input
  directory on Windows.
- Wiring verified in the tree: `SessionSourceOptions.WorktreesEnabled` (store + app-server thread
  list paths), `tui.SessionPickerWorktreeCWDs` (feature-gated, falls back to the requested directory
  when the checkout cannot be validated), the multi-directory picker filter
  (`SessionPickerState.FilterCWDs`), `tui/tea/session_picker.go`, `app/interactive.go`, and
  `app/remote_tui.go` (the remote resume/fork picker list params).
- Tests: linked-worktree discovery with a preserved relative directory (`pkg/` in both checkouts),
  shared-identity assertions, unregistered-`gitdir` rejection, the feature-gating fallbacks, and the
  expanded multi-directory picker filter.
- Verification: `go test ./gitutil ./tui/ ./app -count=1` green.
- Residual (`#43279`): the TUI presentation half is not ported - agents-overview grouping of linked
  checkouts and showing another worktree's checkout path on picker entries, plus managed-worktree
  creation on a blocking worker with source-session revalidation. The legacy-daemon fallback to the
  originally requested directory when an array-valued `cwd` filter is rejected is also unported
  (Go never inspects the rejection), and Go's `--last` remote lookup does not scope by directory at
  all.

### `cebdb732ea` #44945 Windows sandbox setup through the app server - partial (completion matching)

Go already routes both TUI sandbox-setup prompts through the app-server
`windowsSandbox/setupStart` RPC (`app/windows_sandbox_tui.go`, the remote path in
`app/remote_tui.go`, and `tui/tea/windows_sandbox.go`), locks composer input while a setup is
pending, and consumes `windowsSandbox/setupCompleted`. This pass closes the completion-matching gap
from the Rust commit:

- The model now records the mode of the pending setup (`windowsSandboxSetupMode`) when the setup
  starts and when an inline start response carries its completion, and
  `applyWindowsSandboxSetupCompleted` ignores a completion whose mode differs from the pending one.
  A setup therefore keeps its locked input and status until its own completion arrives, matching
  Rust's "ignore completion notifications for a different mode".
- Test: `TestModelWindowsSandboxSetupIgnoresCompletionForDifferentMode` starts an elevated setup
  that stays pending, delivers an unelevated completion (must be ignored), then the elevated one
  (must clear).
- Verification: `go test ./tui/tea/ -run WindowsSandbox` and `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1`
  green.
- Residual (`#44945`): the response-lost/timeout retention is structural rather than explicit (Go's
  start call is synchronous and blocked until a completion arrives, so the pending state is kept
  without a timeout), the "clear interrupted setup state with a restart message after reconnection"
  half is not ported (no reconnect-time invalidation of a pending setup), and the TUI-side removal
  of the direct sandbox helpers/`config_persistence` writes from the Rust commit is not mirrored.

## Upstream advance (944d6fd1ba)

Rust checkout fast-forwarded `89c8bcf37d..944d6fd1ba` (`944d6fd1ba` #44952 keep voice captions
visible across speaker updates and history handoff). The commit touches only
`codex-rs/tui/src/{app_event.rs, app/event_dispatch.rs, chatwidget.rs, chatwidget/realtime*.rs,
chatwidget/rendering.rs}` - voice/realtime captions, which remain owned by the parallel voice
process and are excluded per the operator instruction. No non-TUI work to port; parity and
app-server schema gates are unaffected (no pinned file changed).

### `#43279` residual closed: legacy-daemon single-CWD fallback

Rust's `app_server_session/thread_list.rs` retries `thread/list` with a single `cwd` when an older
daemon rejects the array-valued filter. Go now mirrors that:

- `remoteAppServerTUIClient.waitResponse` returns a typed `remoteRPCError` (code + message) instead
  of a bare `errors.New(message)`, so callers can branch on the JSON-RPC code without losing the
  existing message-only behavior.
- `remoteThreadListWithCwdFallback` retries once with the first directory (discovery orders the
  requested directory first) only when the server error is `-32600`/`-32602`, the message contains
  `invalid type: sequence` and `expected a string`, and the filter actually has more than one
  directory - matching Rust's `ThreadListCwdFilter::Many` match.
- The remote resume/fork picker (`interactiveRemoteSessionPickerItems`, the only producer of an
  array-valued `cwd` filter) routes through the helper.
- Tests: a scripted transport proves the retry sends a single-directory `cwd` string and that a
  single-directory request does not retry (the error surfaces).
- Verification: `go test ./app -count=1` green.
- Residual (`#43279`): agents-overview grouping of linked checkouts and managed-worktree creation on
  a blocking worker with source-session revalidation are still not ported.

### `c210f4c222` #44939 respect execution hosts in Windows sandbox setup - partial (readiness gate)

- Go already restricts the startup sandbox NUX to local app-server endpoints
  (`interactiveRemoteWindowsSandboxStartupPrompt` returns nil for remote endpoints) and locks
  composer input while setup is pending. This pass adds the readiness half from the Rust commit.
- `windowsSandboxReadyViaClient` queries `windowsSandbox/readiness` over the connected app server and
  reports ready only for `WindowsReadinessReady`; `interactiveRemoteWindowsSandboxReady` bounds the
  query with Rust's 5-second timeout and reports not-ready when the server is unavailable or slow
  (preserving the configured behavior). `interactiveRemoteWindowsSandboxStartupPrompt` now consults
  it before falling back to the TUI's own setup files, so a local app server that already has
  elevated setup no longer prompts.
- Test: `TestWindowsSandboxReadyViaClient` covers ready / not-configured / missing-client using a
  scripted transport and asserts the readiness RPC method.
- Verification: `go test ./app -count=1` green.
- Residual (`#44939`): the `WindowsSandboxHost` Local/Mixed/Remote classification (and its
  "local and remote executors are configured together" startup warning), pending-input restoration
  for mixed executors or a dropped local connection, and setup-state preservation across chat
  widget replacement/reconnects still depend on Rust's `AppServerTarget` + `EnvironmentManager`
  model, which Go's TUI does not carry; remote servers owning Agent permission selection is
  already Go's behavior because the remote TUI only prompts for local endpoints.

### `#43279` residual closed: linked-checkout grouping in the agents overview

- `tui/agents_overview` resolves a project group per row (`projectGroupForRow`): with worktrees
  enabled and a validated checkout, the key is the shared administrative directory plus the
  directory within the checkout and the heading points at the primary checkout's corresponding
  directory; otherwise the key/heading is the row's cwd. `SetWorktreesEnabled` computes the groups,
  `ApplyRefresh` preserves the flag, and project sorting, the group-changed boundary, the rendered
  heading, and the group count all use the resolved group (Rust `AgentsOverviewProjectGroup`).
- Wiring mirrors Rust's `features.enabled(Worktrees) && !remote workspace/environment` gate:
  `codextea.Options.LocalSession` (set true by the local in-process TUI and by the remote TUI only
  for local endpoints) combined with the worktrees feature, and `newAgentsDashboardModel` /
  `runAgentsDashboard` take the flag for the standalone `codex agents` dashboard (local branch from
  the effective config, remote branch hard false).
- Tests: `tui/agents_overview` proves two linked checkouts group together with the primary heading
  when enabled, stay separate when disabled, and that `ApplyRefresh` keeps the grouping.
- Verification: `go test ./tui/agents_overview ./tui/tea ./tui/ ./app -count=1` green.
- Residual (`#43279`): managed-worktree creation on a blocking worker with transition-blocker
  checks and source-session revalidation is still not ported, and picker entries from another
  worktree continue to show their cwd through the existing "Directory" detail rather than a
  dedicated checkout-path annotation.

### `#44666` residual closed: `tui.animations` reaches the TUI consumers

The earlier pass landed the system-motion core but noted Go never read `tui.animations`. That is now
wired end to end:

- `app/interactive.go` gains `interactiveAnimationsEnabled`, which reads `[tui].animations`
  (default enabled) and applies the host motion preference through `codextui.EffectiveAnimations`,
  and `interactiveSettingsFromConfig` carries the result as `SettingsWriteResult.AnimationsEnabled`.
- `codextea.SettingsWriteResult` / `codextea.Options` gain `AnimationsEnabled *bool`; the model
  stores the effective value (nil defaults to enabled) and `SettingsWriteResultMsg` updates it when
  settings are reloaded, so a live settings write re-resolves the preference.
- Consumers now use it instead of hardcoded constants: `tui/tea/pet_runtime.go` passed `true` to
  `pets.LoadAmbientPet`, and the three `execcell.NewExecCell(call, false)` sites in `tui/tea/model.go`
  passed `false`. Both mirrors Rust, which passes `self.local_settings.tui.animations` to the exec
  cell constructor and `config.tui.animations` to the ambient pet (and `system_motion::mode()` to the
  startup composer).
- Tests: model default/override and the settings-result update (`tui/tea/animations_test.go`), and
  the app-level config-plus-host-motion resolution (`app/animations_test.go`).
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go test ./tui/tea ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green.
- Residual (`#44666`): the composer sparkle/effort-ignition and hook-status animations have no Go
  counterpart, and `tui/onboarding.ShouldShowWelcomeAnimation` still has no runtime caller, so the
  startup-composer motion handling has nothing to gate yet.

Rust removed the TUI command that granted a sandbox read root, its event handlers, and the

### `eaa8b6d917` #44744 archive confirmation number shortcuts - done

Rust made the agents-overview archive confirmation act on its first activation while permanent
deletion keeps an explicit second confirmation (`require_explicit_confirmation: action == Delete`).
Go had no explicit-confirmation concept, so both acted immediately:

- `tui/chatwidget.SelectionItem` gains `RequireExplicitConfirmation`, carried through
  `openSelectionViewModal` into the new `ModalOption.RequireConfirmation`.
- `tui/tea` modals track an armed option: `activateModalOption` arms a `RequireConfirmation` option
  on its first activation (setting the "Press enter again to confirm." footer note) and runs it on
  the second; moving the selection or cancelling disarms. `openModal` normalization preserves the
  new flag.
- `openAgentsOverviewLifecycleConfirmation` marks the confirm option with
  `RequireConfirmation: action == delete`, so archiving acts on the first key and permanent deletion
  needs two - matching Rust.
- The standalone `codex agents` dashboard's own y/n confirmation gained the same split: the first
  `y`/Enter arms permanent deletion and the view asks for a second confirmation, while archiving
  still confirms in one step.
- Tests: `TestModelAgentsConfirmationNumberKeysLikeRust` drives the `2` shortcut for both actions,
  the existing delete test now asserts the first activation does nothing, and the dashboard
  lifecycle test asserts the armed state and second confirmation.
- Verification: `go test ./app ./tui/... -count=1` green.

### `eabb7c91d1` #44742 preserve editor yanks across sessions - already satisfied (test added)

Rust transfers the composer kill-buffer snapshot when it replaces the chat widget on `/new` and
thread switches. Go already satisfies this by construction: the kill buffer lives on the model
(`composerKillBuffer`), while only the composer/session state is reset, so no snapshot hand-off is
needed.

- Regression test `TestEditorKillBufferSurvivesNewSessionLikeRust` kills a line with ctrl-u, starts
  a new session, and yanks it back into the replacement composer.
- Residual (pre-existing, not #44742): Go's kill buffer stores only the text, while Rust also tracks
  whether the kill was characterwise or linewise and its Vim `p` path uses a separate `vimYank`
  buffer, so linewise paste positioning differs from Rust.
- Verification: `go test ./tui/tea/ -run 'KillBuffer|EditorKill' -count=1` green.

### `51c97f3a67` #43074 retryable apps popup failure - done

Rust replaces the `/apps` loading popup with a generic failure popup plus a Retry action when the
directory request fails with no cached or partial list, and never shows the raw request error. Go
showed the raw error with a Close item, so this is now ported:

- `tui/chatwidget` gains `AppsRetryView` (Title "Apps", Subtitle "Failed to load apps.", a disabled
  "App directory unavailable" item with the Rust description, and a "Retry" item carrying
  `AppsActionRetry` + "Reload installed and available apps.") matching Rust's
  `connectors_error_popup_params`. The raw-error `AppsErrorView` is removed since it has no
  production caller.
- `tui/tea/apps.go` shows the retry popup on request failure instead of the raw error, and the tea
  modal handles the Retry option by re-running `applyAppsCommand`, which restores the loading view
  and forces a refresh - Rust's "retrying forces a refresh and restores the loading state".
- Tests: `TestAppsLoadingAndRetryViews` pins the popup shape, and
  `TestAppListFailureShowsRetryablePopupLikeRust` drives the failure -> retry -> loading -> catalog
  flow, asserting the raw error never reaches the view.
- Verification: `go vet ./tui/tea/ ./tui/chatwidget/ ./app/`; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1`
  green.
- Residual: Go's unused `tui/chatwidget.ConnectorsState` core (ported earlier) still returns
  `ConnectorsOutputFailed` with the raw error rather than a view; the live `/apps` path in
  `tui/tea/apps.go` is the one that matters today, so the state machine was left untouched.

## Upstream advance (53ff712a48) and #44957

Rust checkout fast-forwarded `944d6fd1ba..53ff712a48` (`53ff712a48` #44957 add model grouping to
the agent command center - TUI-only). Landed:

  project -> status -> model -> project and the footer reports `group: project|status|model`.
- `Row.Model` carries the task's model; `ModelName` reports `Unknown` for missing/empty names. Model
  grouping sorts rows by model name (byte-wise, so `Unknown` precedes lowercase model ids) while
  keeping the host's recency order within a group, and the group headers, per-group counts,
  selection navigation, and status-marker visibility all follow the active grouping through shared
  `sameGroup` / `groupHeading` helpers.

  task's cwd in project grouping (both the in-session tea dashboard and the standalone
  `codex agents` dashboard). Row builders populate the model from the app-server thread or the local
  session record.
- Tests: `TestModelGroupingLikeRust` covers the cycle, model ordering (including `Unknown`), group
  headers/counts, the footer hint, and the details row; the tea dashboard test asserts Ctrl+S
  cycling to model grouping.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app/`; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green.
- Residual (`#44957`): a `thread/settings/updated` model change is picked up on the dashboard's next
  refresh (Go re-lists threads per refresh) rather than patching the retained row immediately as

- L0: the added `codex_tui__app__agents_overview__tests__agents_overview_model_grouping.snap` moved

- The details pane now labels the preview `Prompt` (Rust's label) and renders `No prompt available.`
  for an empty preview, keeps explicit line breaks (per-line wrapping instead of `strings.Fields`),
  and limits the prompt to two rendered lines with an ellipsis marker - matching Rust's
  `render_markdown_text_with_width_and_cwd` plus its two-line truncation.
- `Row.Title()` now takes only the first line of a name/preview, mirroring Rust's `display_title`.
- Tests: `TestPreviewMarkdownLikeRust` covers control-character filtering, the 512-character cap
  (including that filtered characters do not consume it), and the two-line prompt limit; the
  existing details tests were updated for the new label/placeholder.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green.
  - Residual closed this pass (markdown styling): the terminal-agnostic core now accepts an
    injected `View.RenderMarkdown` renderer. The core adds a `raw` span kind whose width is
    measured with SGR escapes stripped, emits it verbatim for `RenderStyled`, and strips it
    for plain `Render`, so pre-rendered ANSI markdown lines do not corrupt width math. The
    task-details prompt preview uses the renderer when set (falling back to the plain
    two-line preview otherwise), and `tui/tea` wires it through
    `markdown.RenderWithThemeCwd` with the live theme/cwd. Tests:
    `TestTaskDetailsUsesInjectedMarkdownRenderer` (styled verbatim, plain stripped),
    `TestTaskDetailsMarkdownFallsBackToPlainPreview`, and
    `TestWireAgentsOverviewSetsMarkdownRenderer`. Verification:
    `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green; `go build ./...` clean.
- Residual (`#44752`): the "Last message" section is not ported - it needs the dashboard to
  observe delivered agent messages, since Go's `/agents` refresh only lists threads. Rust also
  renders the preview through its full markdown engine (tables/links) whereas Go uses the
  injected renderer's lines as-is; both share the two-line truncation.

### `0344625ccf` #41921 fresh Vim drafts start in Insert mode - done (mode transitions)

Rust now starts the composer in Vim Insert mode when Vim is enabled and returns to Insert after
submissions and slash-command dispatch (previously it reset to Normal):

- `Model.enterVimInsertAfterSubmission` sets Insert mode and clears pending operators, and is called
  after the composer is reset by `submitComposer`, `submitRunningSlashCommand`, `queueComposer`,
  `steerComposer`, and the slash-popup dispatch (`tui/tea/slash_popup.go`).
- `toggleVimMode` starts Insert when Vim is enabled (and Normal when it is disabled).
- Vim search: Backspace on an empty query cancels the search and any pending operator without
  changing the draft; on a non-empty query it removes the last query character (Rust #41921).
  Go already kept `/`/`?` bound to Normal-mode search rather than slash-command insertion.
- Tests: `TestVimModeToggleStartsInInsertModeAndNormalMotions`,
  `TestVimSubmittedDraftStartsInInsertModeLikeRust` (submission / queued / slash dispatch), and
  `TestVimSearchBackspaceCancelsEmptyQueryLikeRust`; the queued-message history-up tests now return
  to Normal mode explicitly, and the long-tail slash-command test no longer types `i` to enter
  Insert.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green.
- Residual (`#41921`): Rust also records the fresh Insert session as a Vim edit so `.` can repeat
  text typed into a new draft; Go's dot-repeat only replays recorded deletes, so repeating an
  inserted draft remains unported.

## Audit-backlog triage (non-TUI core first, TUI last)

The audit finding above queued 18 previously-unenumerated non-voice commits. Working the
non-TUI core candidates first per the operator priority produced these verified statuses:

- `3436cad5ab` #44238 Fix MCP elicitation cancellation and reset state on reconnect - **done**.
  Go's stdio client answered elicitations inline on the read loop, so server
  `notifications/cancelled` frames could never reach a pending form/URL elicitation, and a
  cancelled request context was reported as `decline` instead of `cancel`.
  - New `mcp/elicitation_cancellation.go` adds `mcpElicitationCancellationMemory`, mirroring
    Rust's `ElicitationClientService` map: cancellations remembered before or during an
    elicitation, never evicted, saturated at 1024 (after which every new elicitation on the
    connection is cancelled), consumed on use so a reused request id starts fresh, and reset
    for each connection attempt (`startAndInitialize`), which scopes state to its connection
    including initialization retries/recovery.
  - `stdioClient.handleReadFrame` now answers elicitations on a dedicated goroutine and routes
    `notifications/cancelled` (camel- and snake-case `requestId`) into the memory, so the read
    loop keeps observing cancellation while a prompt is pending. `elicitationRequestResult`
    returns `cancel` for a cancelled request context or a remembered cancellation, for every
    elicitation kind (previously only user verification honored cancellation).
  - `mcpElicitationResult` now returns `cancel` when the request context is already cancelled
    and when it cancels while the handler runs, so a late accept/decline is not reported as if
    the user still wanted it.
  - Tests: `mcp/elicitation_cancellation_test.go` covers early/late cancels, consume-on-use,
    saturation-without-eviction, per-connection reset, camel/snake `requestId` parsing, the
    cancelled-context short-circuit, and an end-to-end stdio subprocess test where the server
    sends an elicitation then cancels it and the client must answer `cancel`.
  - Verification: `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green; `go build ./...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean.
  - Residual: Go elicitation handlers run synchronously inside their goroutine, so a late
    server cancellation is applied when the handler returns rather than preempting it
    mid-callback as Rust's `tokio::select!` does; there is no `pause_state` equivalent to
    release, so that half of the Rust change is N/A.
- `3dc1e2a584` #44255 Always use streamed remote compaction for supported providers -
  **already satisfied**. Go has no v1 `/responses/compact` path or `remote_compaction_v2`
  feature gate: `agentCompactRunner`/`execAgentCompactRunner` always drive the streamed
  `/responses` runner, and `features.ModelClientBetaFeaturesHeader` already advertises
  `remote_compaction_v2` unconditionally, matching Rust's retired-toggle behavior.
- `935ac7710d` #44675 Refresh global instructions at model-request boundaries -
  **already satisfied**. `config.GlobalInstructionsManager` retains the last successful global
  snapshot with one-warning-per-episode behavior, and `RuntimeRouter`
  `refreshThreadGlobalInstructions`/`turnInstructionsProvider`/`refreshedThreadInstructionText`
  refresh global AGENTS.md at turn and step boundaries while reusing the captured repository
  snapshot and re-discovering it on cwd/trust change.
- `8570091e14` #44655 Honor thread-level plugin exclusions across runtime capabilities -
  **already satisfied**. `appserver/disabled_plugin_ids_test.go` covers the skills, MCP
  contributions, app-tool capabilities, plugin-hook sources, and discoverable-config filtering
  of thread-disabled plugins.
- `6515a72db7` #43848 Preserve runtime workspace roots across thread resume - **mostly
  satisfied**. Go persists `runtime_workspace_roots` in the thread record's startup metadata,
  restores them on resume (`threadRecordRuntimeWorkspaceRoots`), honors explicit overrides,
  retargets/dedupes against `cwd`, and round-trips them through the app-server thread/resume
  result; `router_test.go` covers stored-root restoration and explicit overrides. Remaining
  Rust-only details to confirm before closing: resume-time settings checkpoint when no turn
  follows, and Windows rollout path-spelling normalization for search/revert matching.
- `537278c65f` #44489 Reset cached WebSocket state when auth ownership changes - **queued
  (blocked on prerequisite)**. Go `model.ResponsesAgentRunner` caches websocket sessions by
  thread/turn key but has no auth-change state; Rust's fix reads
  `AuthChangeState.owner_generation` from `codex-rs/login/src/auth/change_state.rs`, which Go's
  `auth` package does not port. This needs the auth owner-generation machinery (generation vs
  owner_generation, same_owner semantics, watch-style change notifications) before the
  websocket/session reset can be ported faithfully.
- `fc948f8c47` #44701 Add a provider for thread-scoped instructions - **queued**. Go has the
  global instructions provider (`config.UserInstructionsProvider`,
  `config.GlobalInstructionsManager`) but no `ThreadInstructionsProvider`, no optional
  `Instructions.Source`, and no thread-instruction composition/10k-token budget. Substantial
  but core (model-request instructions), so it is the next non-TUI core item after the smaller
  ones.
  - **Reclassified N/A (provider surface)**: `ThreadInstructionsProvider` is exposed only
    through the Rust core-library `StartThreadOptions` (an embedded-host API); it has no
    app-server protocol field and no non-test host besides Rust's own core, and Go has no
    embedded-host/extensibility layer to register such a provider. Go's app-server already
    carries thread-scoped instructions directly via `ThreadStartParams.BaseInstructions` /
    `DeveloperInstructions`, which it composes and persists. The independent 10k-token bound
    applies to the provider snapshot, so it has no Go target without that provider surface.
- `94697375cb` #44548 MIME-filtered Codex Apps resource listing - **queued (no Go consumer)**.
  Rust adds `McpResourceClient::list_codex_apps_resources`; in the pinned upstream it has no
  production caller (only `core/tests/suite/mcp_tool_exposure.rs`). Go's `apps` app-server path
  lists accessible apps through the `codex_apps` connector tool, not MCP resource listing, and
  its resource client has no `mimeType` parameter. Port the API only alongside a Go consumer.
- `b090e901f8` #43844 staged enterprise OIDC login + coordinated logout, `9e22e74e8d`
  #44676 / `cc05ecfe17` #44669 permission-path context, and the `442xx` captured-step-settings
  cluster remain queued non-TUI core items for follow-up passes.
  - `#44669` is already partially ported (resolution/validation/glob preservation, documented
    above); `#44676` and the `442xx` step-settings cluster lack the Go execution-host
    path-convention and `TurnContext` step-settings models, so they remain
    architecture-different.
  - `#43844` is the main remaining portable core item (Go has `mcp/oauth_*.go`); it needs a
    dedicated pass.
- `ce2c2759eb` #44183 release persistent writers when session startup is cancelled - **N/A
  (Go synchronous startup)**. Go's `ThreadManager.RetainLiveThread` opens the guard and
  commits it immediately under `defer guard.Discard()`, and start/fork error paths call
  `releaseLiveThreads`; there is no concurrent `join!` that can drop a persistence future and
  leave a writer held with no session, so the Rust failure mode does not arise.
- `9688359977` #44493 and the other Guardian-scoped commits stay N/A on Go architecture, per
  the earlier `plan_2026_09_11.md` analysis.

### Next

- Continue the non-TUI core queue: `#44701` (thread-scoped instructions provider), then
  `#44669`/`#44676` permission-path context, then the `442xx` captured-step-settings cluster.
- `#43428` and `#44489` are now landed (auth change state, opted-in MCP notifications, and
  websocket/routing-state reset on ownership change).
- `#44548` awaits a Go consumer.

### `e6249b5296` #42388 recover deferred environments after provisioning failure - core done

Rust's provisioning state machine changed so a valid Ready report can recover a
previously failed provisioned environment while preserving the same environment
instance, and a late failure can no longer replace Ready. Go's
`appserver/environment.go` mirrored the old "Ready after Failed is
contradictory" rule:

- `ProvisioningState.applyReady` now validates ready info first: invalid info
  fails a Pending environment but leaves an already Ready/Failed environment
  unchanged; valid info moves any state to Ready, clears the retained failure,
  and refreshes the capability roots, so a corrected report recovers the same
  `ProvisioningState`.
- `applyFailure` is unchanged (keeps the first error; a late failure on Ready is
  still rejected), matching Rust's `apply_error_report`.
- `EnvironmentManager.StatusContext` reports a provisioning failure without
  starting a connection and now uses Rust's wrapped
  `ExecServerError::ProvisioningFailed` display, `environment unavailable:
  <error>` (pinned by Rust's `deferred_environment.rs`).
- Tests: `TestRepeatedFailurePreservesTheFirstErrorUntilReady` (first error kept
  through repeated failures, invalid ready keeps it, valid ready recovers the
  same instance and re-enables the connection),
  `TestInvalidReadyInfoFailsTheProvisioningGate` (corrected report recovers),
  and the exact wrapped status string in
  `TestFailureAndDroppedRegistrationAreTerminal`. Verification:
  `go test ./appserver -count=1` green (131.7s); `go build ./...`; `gofmt -l`
  clean.
- Residual (#42388): the Rust `LazyRemoteExecServerClient` half - treating a
  stale provisioning failure as retryable and reconnecting when a completed or
  still-publishing startup attempt belongs to a now-Ready environment - has no
  Go counterpart; Go's `EnvironmentManager` re-dials per request and keeps no
  cached startup result, so the race the Rust change fixes cannot arise.

## voice 对照（静态）— 见 `update/plan_2026_09_12_voice_comparison.md`

对本仓库 voice 与 Rust 上游做了静态对照（djalign 静态层），产出独立文档。要点：

- 两端同构：`控制层 + 独立原生 helper`、同一套 stdio 帧协议、`WebRTC + Opus(48k/20ms/PT111)`；
  控制面协议、超时、退出阶段码、`thread/realtime/*` 方法集与通知、历史 item 合约均对齐。
- **发现 1（已修复）**：Go helper 设备原先按 **24 kHz** 打开
  （`voicehost/hostmain.go defaultSessionFormat`），而打包/编码/计时/RTP 按 **48 kHz**
  （`media.go:23/25`、`opus.go`、`audio_pipeline.go:195`、`miniaudio.go:554`），全链**无重采样**。
  已把设备 PCM 格式改为 **48 kHz**（`voicehost/hostmain.go`，`manager_test.go` 断言同步），
  并新增回归测试 `TestDefaultSessionFormatMatchesOpusRTPPipeline` 锁定
  `defaultSessionFormat.SampleRate == opusClockRate`、块 10 ms / 帧 20 ms 的不变式。
  仍建议实机音频回环验证一次。
- **发现 2**：`realtime_conversation` feature stage 仍为 `StageRemoved`，上游 #44921 已
  `Stable` + `default_enabled=true`；按既有归属（voice 进程）**本轮未改**，仅登记。
- **发现 3**：DSP（重采样/AEC/NS/AGC）与 `utils/audio`（data URL + 时长→token 估算）在 Go 无对等；
  helper 的 miniaudio 设备层**需要 CGO**（实测 `CGO_ENABLED=0` 构建失败）。
- **L1 静态契约已落地**：新增 `parity/rust_voice_contract_test.go`（控制协议帧界/SDP 界/17 消息
  round-trip、18 退出码双向、7 个 GST 环境项、17 个 `thread/realtime/*` 名），并在
  `parity/contracts/manifest.json` 登记 `voice-control-protocol` /
  `voice-helper-exit-stages` / `thread-realtime-surface` 三条 contract。
- 验证：`go build ./...`；`go test ./voicehost ./parity -count=1`（带 `CODEX_RUST_ROOT`）green。
- **真机回环已验证**（`CODEX_VOICE_REAL_DEVICE=1`）：48000 Hz 采集墙钟比 0.996–1.040，
  扬声器渲染音调 `speakerPeak=24000`，麦克风峰值 48（静默）→ 265（播放时）确认声学耦合。
  过程中修复一个真实缺陷：选定 device id 打开设备会触发 cgo
  "Go pointer to unpinned Go pointer" panic——已用 `runtime.Pinner` 固定 id 内存，并加
  `initMiniAudioDevice` recover 把后端 panic 转成 typed error（对齐 Rust fail-closed）；
  新增硬件无关回归 `TestSelectedDeviceIDFailsClosedWithoutPanic` 与门控真机用例。
- **DSP 缺口已实现**（纯 Go）：`dsp_aec.go`（10 ms 分块频域回声消除，回声衰减到 17.8%）、
  `dsp_ns.go`（sqrt-Hann STFT 谱减降噪，稳态噪声残留 23.4%）、`dsp_agc.go`（自适应增益 + 限幅）、
  `dsp_apm.go`（AEC→NS→AGC 编排）、`dsp_fft.go`；已接入 `media.go`：播放解码块作回声参考、
  采集 480 样本块经 APM 后再组 20 ms Opus 帧、mute 时 reset。验证：
  `go build ./...`；`go test ./voicehost ./parity ./realtime ./app ./appserver -count=1` green；
  真机回环仍通过。与 Rust 差异：Rust 用 rubato + sonora(WebRTC APM)，Go 为纯 Go 近似；
  重采样由设备固定 48 kHz + miniaudio 内部转换覆盖。
- **重采样器已实现**：`dsp_resample.go`（窗口 sinc 流式重采样 + Rust `Converter` 等价语义：
  256 相位表 / Blackman 窗 / 下采样带限 / 480 样本块 / 时间戳 / FIFO 背压）。验证：48k identity
  逐样本相等、24k→48k 样本数 +2%、44.1k→48k 的 1 kHz 音调重建为 1000.0 Hz、背压报错。
  运行期设备仍固定 48 kHz（identity 直通）；以设备原生率打开需贯通 `pcmPipeline` 的速率。
- **设备原生率路径：N/A（平台数据缺失）**。实测本机 WASAPI 端点的
  `malgo.DeviceInfo.Formats` 为空，拿不到原生采样率列表，"按原生率打开"无法数据驱动；
  miniaudio 请求 48 kHz + 共享模式内部转换已与 Rust 功能等价。强行改原生率会改动已验证的
  48 kHz 路径且无可验证收益，故登记为 N/A；重采样器作为可用组件保留（非 48k 需求时在设备
  回调边界接入即可）。
- **#44921 feature stage 已对齐**：`features/features.go` 的 `realtime_conversation`
  由 `StageRemoved` 改为 `StageStable + DefaultEnabled: true`，与 Rust（#44921）一致；
  新增 `features.TestRealtimeConversationFeatureIsStableAndOnLikeRust`。Go 侧该 key 只经注册表
  暴露（voice RPC 由 experimental API 门控），故为元数据对齐；验证：
  `go test ./features ./tui/... ./app ./parity ./appserver -count=1` green。
- **输入音频准备 `utils/audio` 已实现**：新增 `audioutil` 包（占位符三段文本、canonical MIME、
  `PrepareAudioURL` 校验/规范化、`EstimateAudioTokenCount`（时长 ×10 tokens/s + 32 条 LRU +
  `ceil(bytes/4)` 回退）、wav/mp3/mp4/webm/ogg 时长解析）；接入
  `appserver/audio_preparation.go` 用于用户消息音频（远程 URL 与本地文件），失败替换为
  `input_text` 占位符。新增 `audioutil` 顶层目录已同步 `parity/rust_inventory_test.go` 的
  Go 目录快照。既有 `TestUserMessageInputItemFromTurnUserInputsContentKinds` 的非法 base64
  载荷（Rust 同样拒绝）改为合法载荷。验证：`go build ./...`；
  `go test ./audioutil ./appserver ./app ./parity ./voicehost -count=1` green。
- **语音设置文案对齐**（TUI 表现层第一小步）：`tui/chatwidget/voice_picker.go` 标题/副标题改为
  Rust 快照的 "Select voice" / "Applies to your next voice conversation."；`tui/tea` 保存确认改为
  "Voice set to X. Applies to your next voice conversation."（`model.go` 那半被并行进程的
  `git add -A` 卷入其提交 `5016374`，内容已在 HEAD）。新增断言于
  `tui/chatwidget/voice_picker_test.go`，并更新 `tui/tea/voice_test.go`。验证：
  `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1`（`tui/tea` 中 `TestSessionPickerCtrlTRequiresAThread`
  失败来自并行进程未提交的 `session_transcript*.go` WIP，非本次改动）。
- **split-flap 动画字幕板（TUI 表现层）**：新增 `tui/chatwidget/splitflap.go` 移植 Rust
  `realtime_split_flap.rs` 的动画核心：tile 到达时间表（`min(i*8ms,56ms)`）、flap glyph
  采样（`target+index*7+frame*11`）、标点掩码、runway 尾部动画、reduced-motion 直通、
  滑动窗口保留已稳定 tile。`splitflap_test.go` 复现 Rust 快照帧序列
  （`› ATGG AG` / `› GGTT ET GAT` / `› GATE 73 TEG` 等）。TUI 样式/超链接线集成待做。
- **voice 域 L2 录制-重放门禁**：新增 `realtime/replay_test.go` +
  `realtime/testdata/voice_session.jsonl`，把 realtime 事件流重放进
  `RealtimeHistoryState`，冻结 item 表面 golden（模型文本只记 presence，未知 op 直接失败）。
  验证：`go test ./realtime ./tui/chatwidget -count=1` green。
  注：`go test ./parity` 当前因并行进程的在制重构失败
  （`app/queue_test.go` 把 `TestSessionIDByUniqueActiveNamePrefersMostRecentDuplicateLikeRust`
  改名为 `...RejectsDuplicateLabelsLikeRust`，而已提交的 `contracts/manifest.json` 仍引用旧名；
  相关 `app/named_session_lookup.go` 尚未提交），与本轮改动无关。
- **split-flap cell 集成**：`tui/chatwidget/splitflap_cell.go`（`SplitFlapTranscriptCell`
  包装 historycell：多行动画共享字形索引、runway 仅在末行、RawLines 保原文、
  AnimationTick 供排帧；Rust 的 per-span 颜色在 Go 纯文本 cell 模型下不可表达，已登记）。
- **语音状态快照矩阵**：`tui/bottom_pane/voice_strip_matrix_test.go` 冻结 7 个会话状态的
