  speaking/retrying）。
- **APM 保真度提升**：新增 `voicehost/dsp_filter.go`（80 Hz 二阶 biquad 高通 + 延迟线）；
  捕获链对齐 WebRTC APM 顺序 **高通 → AEC → NS → AGC**；`setStreamDelayMS` 现在真正按毫秒
  延迟回声参考（对齐 `set_stream_delay_ms`）；AEC 增加发散保护（抽头非有限即重置）。
  验证：`go build ./...`；`go test ./voicehost ./tui/chatwidget ./tui/bottom_pane ./realtime ./app -count=1` green。
- **cell 模型扩展（带样式行）**：`tui/history_cell/styled.go` 新增
  `CellStyle`/`StyledSpan`/`StyledLine` + 可选接口 `StyledHistoryCell`（基类接口保持纯文本，
  不破坏既有 cell/调用方）；`tui/chatwidget/splitflap_styled.go` 按 Rust 规则输出 per-span 颜色
  （黑底灰字、翻动深灰、user 青 / assistant 品红余晖、`›` 青 / `•` 品红、runway 深灰），
  并有测试锁定 `PlainLines(DisplayStyledLines) == DisplayLines`（文本不漂移）。
  剩余：渲染侧（`State.AddHistoryLines` 仍存纯文本）尚未消费 styled 行。验证：
  `go test ./tui/history_cell ./tui/chatwidget ./tui/bottom_pane ./app -count=1` green。
- **本地 TUI voice 通知链路修复**（用户实测卡在 `voice ⠴ connecting`）：内嵌 TUI 的 voice
  接线原先每次请求新建并立刻关闭 `RuntimeRouter`，且本地路径根本没有 realtime 通知解码
  （`DecodeThreadRealtimeNotification` 只在 `remote_tui.go` 被调用），SDP answer 到不了 TUI。
  改为 `app/voice_local.go` 的**持久 `localVoiceSession`**（一个长生命周期 router +
  `SetNotificationSink` → 通知解码进 channel），TUI 侧新增 `OnVoiceNotifications` 钩子与
  `waitVoiceNotificationCmd` 单读者泵，会话结束后停止。测试：`TestRealtimeNotificationMessageMapsWirePayloads`
  与既有 voice 用例。验证：`go build ./...`；`go test ./app ./tui/... ./voicehost ./parity -count=1` green。
  残余：`localVoiceSession.Close()` 目前没有显式调用点（进程退出回收），可后续接到 TUI 退出路径。

## Non-TUI recovery: EMA ID-JAG exchange (`batch-mcp-oauth-identity`, #40722)

The August `batch-mcp-oauth-identity` queue in `plan_2026_08_26.md` (`f6805328c4` #40722,
`9b4a0f8a0a` #40739) was never dispositioned, and Go still gates EMA MCP connections off
(`mcp/api.go`: "EMA MCP connections are not enabled in this version"). The earlier "non-TUI
actionable exhausted" status was therefore incomplete. This pass ports the self-contained core of
`f6805328c4` #40722:

- `mcp/ema_auth_policy.go` mirrors `ema_auth_policy.rs`: `EMAAuthFailure`
  (`invalid_grant` / `insufficient_user_authentication` / `reauthentication_required`) with
  `EMAInvalidGrantSource`, `safeMCPEMAOAuthErrorCode` (only known OAuth codes escape),
  `validateMCPEMAPublicClientAuth` (requires an explicitly advertised `none`),
  `validateMCPEMAOAuthEndpoint` / credential-destination rules (HTTPS or HTTP loopback, no
  userinfo or fragment), `ValidateMCPEMAAuthResource` (origin + query + path containment), and
  `advertisedMCPEMACapability`.
- `mcp/ema_claims.go` mirrors `ema_claims.rs`: compact-JWS parsing (three non-empty segments, JSON
  header, non-empty non-`none` alg), `ValidateMCPEMAOIDCIdentityAssertion` (iss/sub plus aud/azp
  binding and expiry), `mcpEMAIDJAGResponse.Validate` (ID-JAG type/issuer/audience/client/subject/
  JTI, exp/iat skew, exact resource binding, signed-scope narrowing, response-scope agreement), and
  `mcpEMAMCPAccessTokenResponse.Validate` (bearer type, no refresh token, non-zero lifetime,
  resource/scope subset).
- `mcp/ema_exchange.go` mirrors `ema_exchange.rs`: `ExchangeMCPEMAIDJAG` performs the
  non-interactive two-step exchange (IdP token-exchange -> ID-JAG, then JWT bearer -> resource
  bearer) with endpoint/issuer/client/scope validation, a 30s bounded non-redirecting form POST,
  and redacted provider errors.
- Tests port Rust's vectors: resource origin/query/path binding, advertised public-client auth, the
  full public-client round trip (form fields, duplicate-free forms, no `Authorization` header,
  an invalid subject fails before HTTP), the signed-claims/resource-token "cannot widen authority"
  table (including invalid JWS headers, changed claims, and token-response changes), identity and
  credential destination binding, and provider-error redaction. Verification:
  `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green; `go vet ./mcp/` clean (also removed a pre-existing
  `status.source = status.source` no-op in `mcp/api.go` that vet flagged); `go build ./...` clean.
- Residual (`#40722`/`#40739`): the identity-resolution layer (`ema_identity.rs`,
  `oauth/ema_identity.rs`) that discovers IdP metadata, resolves a stored refresh token, holds the
  credential lock, and binds OIDC identity plus resource indicators is not ported. Go still has no
  cross-process MCP OAuth credential lock (`RefreshCredentialLock`) or enterprise keyring
  namespace, and the `enterprise_oauth_login.rs` staged-commit/logout-generation work (#43844)
  builds on it. Until that layer lands the exchange primitive has no connection-path caller, so
  EMA connections remain gated off.

## Non-TUI core: MCP OAuth cross-process credential lock (`RefreshCredentialLock`)

Go's MCP OAuth credential store had no cross-process serialization, so two
processes (or two goroutines) could replay the same rotating refresh token or
observe a partially persisted transaction. Rust guards this with
`rmcp-client/src/oauth/refresh_lock.rs`, which `save_oauth_tokens`,
`delete_oauth_tokens`, and `refresh_transaction` all hold. This pass ports it:

- `mcp/oauth_credential_lock.go` mirrors Rust `RefreshCredentialLock`: an
  exclusive `flock` at `$CODEX_HOME/mcp-oauth-locks/<sha256(store_key)>.lock`
  with a 60s acquire bound and 50ms retry, never stealing a held lock, and the
  Rust timeout message (`timed out after 50ms waiting for OAuth refresh lock ...`).
- `OAuthStore.Save`/`Delete` now acquire the lock and delegate to new
  `saveWithLockHeld`/`deleteWithLockHeld` writers, mirroring Rust's
  `save_oauth_tokens_with_lock_held` / `delete_oauth_tokens_with_lock_held`.
- `httpClient.refreshOAuthTokenForRequest` holds the lock across the whole
  transaction: it re-reads the authoritative credential after acquiring it, and
  when the pre-lock snapshot needed refreshing but the locked reread no longer
  does, it adopts the winner from another process without contacting the
  provider (checking that refresh credentials stay bound to the validated
  issuer), then refreshes and persists with the locked writer. The forced-401
  path still refreshes. Provider failure adoption and permanent-error deletion
  use the locked writer, so no nested acquisition can deadlock.
- Tests: the Rust lock-contention vector (timeout without stealing, then
  re-acquire after release), per-store-key scoping, `Save`/`Delete` round trip,
  locked writers avoiding nested acquisition, and a refresh that adopts an
  under-lock concurrent winner with zero provider token requests. Existing
  Rust #43947 adoption tests were updated to the new signature and stay green.
  Verification: `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green; `go vet ./mcp/` clean;
  `go test ./app/ -run "MCP|OAuth" -count=1` green; `go build ./...` and
  `gofmt -l` clean.
- This removes the credential-lock prerequisite noted in the EMA section above.
  The EMA identity-resolution layer (`#40739`) still needs a keyring-backed MCP
  OAuth credential store (`StoredOAuthCredentialSnapshot`, `bound_issuer`,
  `id_token` extra fields), which Go's file-fallback-only `OAuthStore` does not
  model.

## Non-TUI recovery: MCP OAuth callback mix-up defense (`batch-mcp-oauth-identity`, #40691)

`9be8d6e1c3` #40691 hardens MCP OAuth callbacks against RFC 9700
authorization-server mix-up attacks (shared callback URLs across independent
MCP servers). Go already had the callback-ID fallback (`MCPOAuthCallbackID` /
`AppendMCPOAuthCallbackID`) but no callback-mode selection or issuer binding.
This pass ports the mix-up defense core and wires it:

- `mcp/oauth_callback_mode.go` mirrors `rmcp-client/src/oauth_callback.rs`:
  `MCPOAuthCallbackMode` (`callback_specific` / `issuer_bound`),
  `MCPOAuthCallbackModeForDiscovery` (issuer binding only when the server
  advertises `authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported` and publishes a
  metadata issuer), `ResolveMCPOAuthCallbackURL` (stable callback for
  issuer-bound; server-derived callback ID appended for the fallback),
  `ValidateMCPOAuthCallbackRedirect`, and listener-port insertion for portless
  loopback redirects. `AppendMCPOAuthCallbackID` is now idempotent, matching
  Rust.
- Discovery exposes `authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported` and a
  computed `CallbackMode` on `StreamableHTTPOAuthDiscovery`; a supported issuer
  without a metadata issuer falls back to callback-specific, matching Rust's
  `callback_mode(..).unwrap_or(CallbackSpecific)`.
- `OAuthLoginServerOptions.CallbackMode` + `OAuthLoginSession.CallbackMode`
  thread the mode through the login flow. `StartOAuthLoginServer` resolves the
  registered callback and inserts the listener port, and `CompleteCallback`
  validates the authorization-response `iss` against the discovered issuer
  before exchanging the code when issuer binding is active (RFC 9207).
- Wiring: the app-server login (`mcp/api.go`), the dependency login
  (`mcp/oauth_dependency.go`), and the CLI login (`app/mcp.go`) pass the
  discovered callback mode and issuer. The CLI server model now round-trips
  `oauth.callback_url` (`mcpCLIServer.OAuthCallbackURL`) and passes a configured
  or client-ID-resolved callback as the redirect URL.
- Tests: Rust's resolver/validation vectors, callback-mode selection, callback
  ID idempotence, listener-port insertion, session `iss` validation (missing,
  mismatched, and callback-specific), and the CLI `oauth.callback_url` config
  round trip. Verification: `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green; `go vet ./mcp/
  ./app/` clean; `go test ./app/ -run "MCP|OAuth" -count=1` green;
  `go build ./...` and `gofmt -l` clean.
- Residual (`#40691`): `mcp add` does not yet run discovery before saving, so a
  newly added existing client ID does not persist its resolved callback URL
  (Go upserts before discovery; Rust reorders), and the CLI login does not yet
  thread the global `mcp_oauth_callback_url` (only the app-server does).

## Non-TUI recovery: MCP tool output as content items (`batch-mcp-oauth-identity`, #40737)

`75cb7c903d` #40737 changes how MCP call results become model
`function_call_output` payloads: unstructured results become typed content items
instead of a serialized text blob, structured results stay serialized text, and
empty text items stop consuming API array slots. Go previously kept a text body
plus a partial `content_items` list that handled only `text`/`encrypted_content`,
silently dropping MCP image/audio content and preferring content items even when
structured content should win.

- `mcp/tool_executor.go` now mirrors Rust `convert_mcp_content_to_items` and
  `CallToolResult::as_function_call_output_payload`:
  - text with `_meta["codex/encryptedContent"] == true` becomes
    `encrypted_content`, otherwise `input_text`;
  - image becomes `input_image` with a data URL (preserving an existing `data:`
    prefix, else building `data:<mimeType>;base64,<data>` with
    `application/octet-stream` fallback) and a `detail` from
    `_meta["codex/imageDetail"]` (auto/low/high/original, default `high`);
  - audio becomes `input_audio` with the same data-URL rules;
  - unknown or malformed content is preserved as `input_text` carrying the JSON
    content instead of being dropped.
- `mcpFunctionCallOutput` applies Rust's precedence: any encrypted item forces
  content items, else a non-null structured result is serialized to text (no
  content items), else the typed items are used.
- Tests mirror Rust's vectors: text-only, data-URL-preserving and
  data-URL-building images (including a `codex/imageDetail` override), audio,
  unknown-content preservation, encrypted content, and the structured-vs-content
  precedence. Verification: `go test ./mcp/ ./turn/ -count=1` green;
  `go test ./model/ ./codemode/... -count=1` green; `go test ./app/ -run
  "MCP|OAuth|Mcp"` and `./appserver/ -run "MCP|Mcp"` green; `go vet ./mcp/`
  clean; `gofmt -l` clean.
- Residual (`#40737`): the output-truncation change (dropping empty text items
  under byte/token policies) has no Go home - Go truncates MCP response text
  before conversion and `TruncateFunctionOutputItemsWithPolicy` has no
  production caller (same root gap as #44867/#44242 documented earlier).

## Non-TUI recovery: MCP connection identity and startup-timeout reuse (`batch-mcp-oauth-identity`, #40634/#40636)

- `f5853cbe90` #40634 reconnect MCP servers when the authentication mode changes -
  **already satisfied (connection identity)**. Go's MCP connection cache identity
  (`mcpConnectionCacheKey`, applied by `httpClientForServer`/`stdioClientForServer`)
  JSON-marshals the whole `ServerConfig`, whose `Auth` field drives
  `EffectiveAuth`, so changing the auth mode changes the identity and replaces the
  cached client rather than reusing it. New test
  `TestMCPConnectionCacheKeyIncludesAuthModeLikeRust` pins this.
- `20c3f9733f` #40636 restart pending MCP connections when the startup timeout
  changes - **done**. Go previously required `cached.startupTimeout ==
  config.StartupTimeout` for every reuse, so a ready connection was dropped when
  only the startup budget changed (Rust reuses ready connections and replaces
  only pending startups). `httpClient`/`stdioClient` now expose `isInitialized`
  (`httpClient` gained a mutex-free `startupComplete atomic.Bool` mirroring its
  `initialized` flag; `stdioClient` reads its mutex-guarded flag), and the cache
  checks reuse when `isInitialized() || startupTimeout matches`. Test
  `TestMCPStdioConnectionReuseHonorsStartupTimeoutLikeRust` covers pending
  replacement and ready reuse. Verification: `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green;
  `go vet ./mcp/` clean; `go build ./...` and `gofmt -l` clean.

## Triage of remaining `batch-mcp-oauth-identity` items

- `4213b38f3c` #40728 honor attachment-owned permissions for MCP servers -
  **pending (large)**. Rust resolves a per-server permission profile at runtime
  publish time (`McpConfig.server_permission_profiles`,
  `permission_profile_for_server`), makes `PreparedMcpCall::new` fail when the
  server's authority is unavailable, uses the captured profile for tool
  approval/elicitation/sandbox metadata, gives threadless discovery/resource
  reads an explicit default profile, and materializes `:workspace_roots` from
  `PathUri`. Go has no per-server permission map at all
  (`MCPToolCallParams.PermissionProfile` is caller-supplied and only feeds
  sandbox-state meta), so this needs a dedicated pass across the MCP
  runtime/binding/elicitation layers rather than a partial edit.
- `2df6705423` #40343 cache executor metadata from initialization - **N/A (no Go
  consumer)**: Go's initialize response already carries `environmentInfo`
  (#43513) and the server returns it; Rust's client-side cache plus lazy
  `environment/info` fallback exists to serve behavioral-verification gating and
  client metadata accessors Go does not implement, while Go's app-server
  `environment/info` intentionally stays a live probe. Porting the cache alone
  would be inert.
- `339751715c` #40368 use snapshot session sources for MCP projection - **N/A
  (Guardian)**: the change keeps Guardian reviewer isolation decisions aligned
  with the projected MCP config snapshot; Go has no Guardian reviewer session
  model (documented Guardian N/A).
- `2161ec272a` #40257 support cua_repl as a Node REPL-backed MCP server -
  **satisfied (recognition + actor policies)**: Go already recognizes `cua_repl`
  alongside `node_repl` in Guardian review evidence
  (`isNodeReplBackedNamespace`) and in the actor confirmation-policy metadata
  (`IsNodeReplBackedServer`), and the Guardian policy/transcript halves are N/A.
  Minor residual: Go trims trailing underscores instead of Rust's
  strip-prefix/strip-suffix/`split_once("__")` parse, so an unusual namespace
  like `mcp__node_repl____` is still accepted (Rust rejects it).

## Non-TUI recovery: attachment-owned permissions for MCP servers (`batch-mcp-oauth-identity`, #40728)

`4213b38f3c` #40728 makes MCP servers attached to executor environments retain
their owner's permission profile instead of inheriting the thread-wide sandbox
authority. Go previously had one thread-wide profile and no per-server
authority, so attached servers inherited the thread's permissions and every
server stayed callable even when its authority was unresolved.

- `mcp/config.go` + `mcp/permission_authority.go`: `RuntimeConfig` gains
  `PermissionProfile` and `ServerPermissionProfiles` (Rust `McpConfig`), plus
  `SetServerPermissionProfiles` and `ForThreadlessOperations` with Rust's
  resolution rules: the controller-owned Apps server uses the thread authority,
  an attached environment uses its own published authority, local and
  selected-plugin servers use the thread authority, and anything else gets no
  entry. `ForThreadlessOperations` gives the thread and every enabled server the
  restrictive default so discovery/resource reads never inherit execution
  authority.
- `mcp/api.go`: `MCPService` stores the published authority; a non-nil
  `ServerPermissionProfiles` map means authority was published, and
  `HasPublishedPermissionAuthority` / `PermissionProfileForServer` expose it.
  `ApplyRuntimeConfig` carries it across refreshes.
- `mcp/binding.go`: `CaptureBinding` records which servers have published
  authority, and `CallTool` rejects a server with none ("no published permission
  authority"), mirroring `PreparedMcpCall::new` returning `None`.
- `appserver/runtime_router.go`: the thread MCP runtime now publishes the
  resolved authority (`applyMCPPermissionAuthority`) using the thread profile
  plus each selected environment's profile; because Go keeps pre-attachment
  executor servers usable before any environment is selected, that case
  inherits the thread authority instead of failing closed (Rust does not offer
  those servers at all).
- `appserver/mcp_elicitation.go`: `mcpElicitationAuthority` carries
  `ServerAuthorityPublished`, the elicitation authority now uses the server's
  published profile instead of the thread-wide one, and a published runtime
  without an entry for the server declines the elicitation without a prompt
  (`mcpElicitationDeclineWithoutMessage`), mirroring Rust's fail-closed path.
- Tests: resolution rules (Apps/local/selected-plugin/env/unresolved/disabled/
  unavailable), threadless defaults, binding fail-closed and legacy no-authority
  paths, elicitation fail-closed and server-authority use, and the appserver
  publish wiring. Verification: `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green;
  `go test ./appserver/ -count=1` green (124.6s); `go test ./app ./turn ./tool
  -count=1` green; `go vet ./mcp/ ./appserver/` clean; `go build ./...` and
  `gofmt -l` clean.
- Residual (`#40728`): sandbox-state request metadata still uses the thread-wide
  profile (Go's `MCPToolCallParams.PermissionProfile` string is never populated
  from the server authority), threadless discovery/resource-read callers are not
  switched to `ForThreadlessOperations`, the embedded app/exec MCP runtimes do
  not publish authority yet, and `materialize_project_roots_with_path_uris`
  (workspace roots resolved with the executor's path convention) remains
  architecture-different in Go for the same reason as #44676.

## Upstream advance (c4017a87aa) and #44976

Rust checkout fast-forwarded `aee8a55ab6..c4017a87aa` (`c4017a87aa` #44976
"Make context snapshot text rendering consistent"). The commit touches only
`core/tests/common/context_snapshot*` and `core/tests/suite/**/*.snap`
(31 files, no production code, no file additions/removals): it normalizes the
Rust integration-test snapshot renderer (plain tags for recognized guidance,
role/tag-boundary checks, permission-path normalization, bounded clipping with
fingerprints for omitted content). Go's parity layer pins `core/tests/suite/mod.rs`
(unchanged) and the `core/tests/suite` file inventory (unchanged), so this is
**N/A (test-harness/snapshots)**. Verified: `CODEX_RUST_ROOT` at the new head and
`go test ./parity/ -count=1` green.

## #40728 follow-up: production-path enforcement and default authority

- The first pass enforced authority in `Binding.CallTool`, but `CaptureBinding`
  has no production caller: the real MCP tool path calls
  `ToolExecutor.Execute` -> `mcpService().CallTool`. `ToolExecutor.Execute` now
  calls `ensureServerPermissionAuthority`, rejecting a server with no published
  authority when the service published one (Rust `PreparedMcpCall::new` -> None).
  Test: `TestMCPToolExecutorRejectsServerWithoutPublishedAuthorityLikeRust`.
- `applyMCPPermissionAuthority` now falls back to `WorkspaceWritePermissionProfile`
  when no thread profile resolves (Go's effective default sandbox when
  `sandbox_mode`/`default_permissions` are unset), so a server that merely
  inherits the thread authority is not failed closed. Regression caught by
  `TestMCPToolCallsStayBoundToEachThread`, which now passes.
  Verification: `go test ./appserver/ -count=1` green (88.5s);
  `go test ./mcp ./turn ./tool ./app -count=1` green; `go vet`/`gofmt` clean.

## #44341 residual re-assessment (evidence-based)

The earlier residual note ("same-owner refreshes should preserve the live relay;
replacement sessions need fresh replay state") no longer describes a real gap:
- `RemoteControlWebsocketLoop.runConnection` ends only on shutdown, disabled, or
  a stopped connection worker; it does not watch the auth revision, so a
  same-owner token refresh keeps the live relay connection (only the reconnect
  backoff/reset path consumes `AuthRevision`). `RetireForAuthChange` is invoked
  only for logout/account-switch (owner changes) and disables the session.
- `NewRemoteControlWebsocketLoop` is created only by `ServeRemoteControlOnly`
  (`app/remote_control_state_db.go`), one per process; a retirement disables the
  session and ends that loop, so there is no in-process "replacement session"
  that could inherit a stale replay cursor or client tracker.
- Stale-message/queued-RPC rejection and drained persistence permits are part of
  Rust's async transport-layer rework; Go tears the connection down on owner
  change and uses synchronous persistence, so those mechanisms have no Go
  counterpart (architecture-different).

## TUI pass kickoff: #43921 core slice is architecture-blocked

Starting the TUI pass (operator priority: TUI last). `9ec33e1926` #43921 has a
non-TUI core half - persist the request's captured reasoning summary in context
updates and compaction checkpoints:

- Rust: `TurnContext::to_turn_context_item` now writes `summary: self.reasoning_summary()`
  instead of a hardcoded `Auto`, and a new `StepContext::to_turn_context_item`
  overrides `item.summary` with the request-captured `settings.reasoning_summary`;
  `compact.rs` / `compact_remote.rs` / `compact_remote_v2.rs` / `session/mod.rs`
  build the reference context item from the step context instead of the (possibly
  stale) turn context.
- Go is blocked here: there is no `TurnContextItem` producer/model at all -
  `session.MetadataPatch.TurnContext` (and `rollout.Line.TurnContext`) are only
  populated by reading Rust-format rollout lines, never built by Go, and the
  `summary` field of a turn-context item has no writer. This is the same
  architecture gap already recorded for the `442xx` captured-step-settings
  cluster (Go has no `TurnContext` step settings), so the compaction-summary
  persistence half stays blocked rather than half-faked.
- Next TUI work is the remaining #43921 TUI half (reasoning item identity
  tracking, restore guards on resume/thread switch/buffered replay, and the
  status-row shimmer) plus the queued worktree browser/creation and
  async-question TUI redesign.
- Concrete next slice (Go mapping): add `ReasoningItemID` / `ReasoningResumeTurnID`
  / `ReasoningRecoveredAfterRefresh` to `tui/chatwidget/StatusState`, add
  `OnReasoningItemStarted(id)` to `ChatStreamingState`
  (`tui/chatwidget/streaming.go`) that finalizes the previous item when a resumed
  item id differs, resets the reasoning buffers for the new id, and re-runs
  `RestoreReasoningStatusHeader`; and add the restore guards (safety-buffering
  wait, unified-exec wait streak, active compaction, non-empty pending-guardian-
  review status) plus the guardian-review `Working` fallback. Those guard inputs
  live in the tea model, so thread them into `RestoreReasoningStatusHeader` or
  gate the call at its `tui/app` / `tui/tea` call sites.

## TUI pass: reasoning identity and restore guards (#43921, tea model)

Go's tea model already streamed the latest reasoning summary line into the
working indicator (`workingStatusHeader` + `chatwidget.LatestSummaryLine`) and
seeded it on resume/thread switch, but it lacked Rust's active-item identity and
restore guards. This pass ports them:

- `tui/tea/model.go`: added `reasoningItemID` / `reasoningResumeTurnID` model
  state and `SessionResumeResponse` / `AgentThreadSwitchResponse` fields
  (`WorkingReasoningTurnID` / `WorkingReasoningItemID`).
  - `applyReasoningSummaryDelta` now ignores deltas for any item other than the
    active one, adopts the item id from the first delta when none is active (a
    resumed turn's first update can arrive without an item/started event), and
    holds the heading while another status owner is active.
  - `applyReasoningItemStarted` mirrors `on_reasoning_item_started`: a new item
    id closes a resumed snapshot, re-selecting the same item is a no-op, and the
    new item resets its buffer while the previous usable heading is retained.
  - `finalizeReasoningSummary` (item completion) keeps the last useful heading
    through later tool activity and clears only the active identity;
    `resetReasoningSummaryHeader` (turn start/completion/error) clears all three.
  - `reasoningHeaderUpdateBlocked` keeps the heading while safety buffering is
    waiting or a compaction is active.
  - `applyItemStarted`/`applyItemCompleted` now handle `type == "reasoning"`.
- `tui/tea/session_picker.go` / `tui/tea/agent.go` seed the resumed and
  switched-to active item identity from the response; `app/remote_tui.go`
  populates the new fields from `remoteTUIThreadActiveReasoning(thread)`.
- Tests (`tui/tea/reasoning_identity_test.go`): delta gating across items,
  previous-heading retention on a new item, finalize keeping the heading, the
  safety-buffering/compaction guards, and resume identity seeding with unrelated
  reasoning ignored. Verification: `go test ./tui/... -count=1` green;
  `go test ./app/ -count=1` green; `go vet ./tui/tea ./app` clean;
  `go build ./...` and `gofmt -l` clean.
- Follow-up: the pending-guardian-review guard is also ported
  (`m.toolRequestRuntime.PendingGuardianReviewStatus`), with test coverage that
  the heading is held during a review and resumes after it finishes.
- Residual (#43921 TUI): Rust also guards on a unified-exec wait streak (Go's tea
  model does not track a command-lifecycle wait streak), and
  `recover_resumed_reasoning`'s resumed-turn check cannot be ported exactly
  because Go's `protocol.Delta` carries no turn id. The status-row shimmer
  animation, buffered-replay `ReasoningReplay` boundary, and
  `reasoning_recovered_after_refresh` compaction reconciliation remain pending.

## TUI pass: managed worktree browser core (#43286/#43279)

Rust's worktree browser (`tui/src/worktree_browser.rs` plus the presentation
rules in `chatwidget/worktree_picker.rs`) was entirely unported: Go had the
portable worktree core (`worktree.Manager.List/Owner/RemoveManaged`) and
worktree-aware session discovery, but no browser model.

- `tui/worktree_browser.go` ports the browser data layer and presentation:
  - `WorktreeBrowserRequest` / `WorktreeBrowserEntry` / `WorktreeOwner`
    (`None`/`Unavailable`/`Archived`/`Resumable`) / `WorktreeThreadSummary` /
    `WorktreeBrowserAction` (`Resume`/`Copy`/`Remove`).
  - `ListManagedWorktreeEntries` mirrors Rust `list`: managed checkouts of the
    repository containing the cwd, with a recorded owner thread id classified
    `Unavailable` until resolved.
  - `ResolveWorktreeOwnerSummaries` mirrors the owner-resolution phase of Rust
    `fetch`: each unavailable owner is looked up once, classified archived vs
    resumable, and given a bounded title (name, else first non-empty preview
    line, else "Untitled conversation", 80-rune truncation with an ellipsis).
  - `WorktreeBrowserRow`/`WorktreeBrowserSubtitle`/`WorktreeUpdatedAgo` mirror
    the picker's list rows ("No attached thread", "Owner thread unavailable",
    "updated 5m ago", "Archived \- ..."), `WorktreeActionsTitle`, and
    `WorktreeBrowserActionItems` (resume only for resumable owners; delete
    refused while the requesting session is inside the worktree, with Rust's
    "Switch to another checkout before deleting this one" reason).
  - `WorktreeDeleteConfirmationItems` mirrors the confirmation view (Cancel
    first, "Keeps thread history; may disrupt other sessions"), and
    `RemoveManagedWorktree` uses the safety-checked manager path.
- Tests: title fallback/truncation (rune-safe), relative-time buckets, row
  presentation per owner kind, owner-summary resolution (resumable/archived/
  unresolved/nil lookup), action enablement (including the sibling-prefix and
  inside-checkout cases), the delete confirmation, and a real-git integration
  test that lists a created+bound worktree as `Unavailable`. Verification:
  `go test ./tui/... ./app/ -count=1` green; `go vet ./tui` clean;
  `go build ./...` and `gofmt -l` clean.
- Residual (#43286/#43279 TUI): the browser is not yet wired into the TUI - no
  "Create a new worktree" / "Browse worktrees" selection views, no
  `AppEvent` request/response plumbing or stale-request gating in the tea model,
  and managed-worktree creation for interactive sessions/forks (Rust #43120) is
  still queued. The `#43921` status-row shimmer remains blocked because Go never
  runs the terminal startup probe, so `tui.DefaultColors` (terminal default
  fg/bg) is never populated; `renderWorkingHeader` keeps its theme-styled sweep.
  The async-question TUI chain (`bottom_pane/async_questions`) is likewise
  unported: Go has no pending-question state, so the already-ported
  `context.AnsweredQuestion` framing still has no consumer.

## TUI pass: async question editor core (#42891/#42889/#44328)

The async-question chain was unported end-to-end: the app-server dropped the
structured questions before they reached the client, and the TUI had no
pending-question state, so the ported `context.AnsweredQuestion` framing had no
consumer. This pass lands the protocol surface and the core editor.

- **Protocol surface (non-TUI core)** - Rust's v2 `ThreadItem::AgentMessage`
  serializes `delivery: AgentMessageDelivery | null` and
  `questions: Vec<AsyncUserInputQuestion> | null` (confirmed in the pinned
  `typescript/v2/ThreadItem.ts` export). Go's `agentMessage` marshaller omitted
  both, so `request_user_input_async` items reached the TUI as plain assistant
  messages. `appserver/protocol.go` now emits `delivery` (from the item's async
  marker) and `questions` (from the item data, which `BuildThreadItem` already
  copied from the metadata) with Rust's explicit-null shape, and
  `app/remote_tui.go` carries both onto the TUI's `protocol.ThreadItem`
  (`Delivery` plus `Metadata["questions"]`). Test:
  `appserver/async_message_test.go` pins the async payload and the explicit-null
  shape; `app/app_test.go` pins the payload -> TUI item mapping.
- **Editor state (`tui/bottom_pane/async_questions.go`)** - ports Rust
  `bottom_pane/async_questions/{mod,state}.rs`: `AsyncUserInputQuestion`
  parsing (title plus optional options; unknown shapes ignored), replay-deduped
  arrival by message ID, per-question drafts, navigation with Rust's
  no-store-on-no-op semantics, `AcceptAnswer` removal/clamping, and
  `BuildAsyncQuestionAnswer` (bounded `AnsweredQuestion` framing, whitespace
  trim, `turn.MaxUserInputTextChars` limit on the framed message).
- **TUI integration (`tui/tea/async_questions.go`)** - async agent-message
  completion appends the questions; the collapsed state renders Rust's
  `"N questions - <binding> to answer"` summary, the edit binding focuses the
  editor (stashing the main composer draft, since Go has one composer where
  Rust has two), the focused question title plus `"N of M"` render above the
  composer, Enter/Tab submit the framed answer (queued while a turn is
  running), `edit_queued_message`/`prompt_stack_back` navigate drafts, Ctrl+]
  skips, and Escape collapses while preserving the draft
  (`tui.question_esc_back`, default enabled; read via
  `app.interactiveQuestionEscBack`). Rust #44328: a new prompt submitted or
  queued clears the previous prompt's questions while retaining their seen IDs
  (local commands and rejected submissions preserve them).
- **Keymap/config** - `chat.prompt_stack_back` ("alt-down"/"shift-right") and
  `chat.skip_question` ("ctrl-]") join the action catalog, the
  `edit_queued_message` description matches Rust's, and
  `NormalizeKeybindingSpec` maps Ctrl+5 to Ctrl+] like Rust's
  `key_hint::normalized_parts` (#42889).
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app ./appserver` clean;
  `gofmt -l` clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app ./protocol -count=1` green;
  `go test ./appserver -count=1` green (90.1s); `go test ./parity -count=1`
  green (`CODEX_RUST_ROOT` at `c4017a87aa`). Committed as `4e4db01`.
- Residuals (#42891 chain): selectable option answers (#42894) and inline
  "Other" answers (#42897) are not ported, so options render only in the
  transcript message and the user types the answer; question state is not yet
  tracked separately through the input queue/retries/reconnect or restored on
  thread switch/replay (#42903); buffered `ThreadSnapshot` replay does not
  re-add questions (Rust's `protocol.rs` path); the `async_questions`
  shadow/overlap keymap validation pass is not mirrored; and Rust's 5s footer
  flash for oversized answers maps to a persistent Go notice.

## TUI pass: selectable async answers with inline Other (#42894/#42897)

Follow-up on the async-question editor: suggested answers were only rendered in
the transcript, so the user had to retype a suggestion. This pass makes them
selectable while keeping free-text answers available through an appended Other
choice.

- `tui/bottom_pane/async_questions.go`: model suggestions are bounded on arrival
  (32 labels, each <= 512 bytes; filtering everything out leaves a free-text
  question), each pending question tracks a choice selection with a default of
  the first suggestion, and the appended Other row is `len(options)`. New
  helpers cover `HasOptions`/`ChoiceCount`/`OtherSelected`/`FocusIsNotes`,
  wrapping navigation (`MoveSelection`), paged/jump navigation, the Other
  placeholder (a suggested option named "Other" gets "Other (write an answer)")
  with a bounded draft preview, and `AnswerText`/`AnswerIsNamedChoice` so the
  answer is the focused suggestion's label or the live composer draft.
- `tui/tea/async_questions.go`: the expanded editor renders numbered choices
  with a `>` selection marker plus the Other row and a contextual
  submit/skip/prev/next hint line; list bindings navigate (arrows always,
  while the printable defaults k/j yield to typing and open Other), digit
  shortcuts submit a named choice, typing or pasting opens Other, blank Other
  answers are rejected, and a suggested choice the terminal cannot display in
  full is blocked with "Expand terminal to read the entire option".
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app ./appserver` clean;
  `gofmt -l` clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app ./protocol -count=1` green;
  `go test ./parity -count=1` green (`CODEX_RUST_ROOT` at `c4017a87aa`).
  Committed as `c01a2ba`.
- Residuals: the clipping guard approximates Rust's visible-option window using
  the rows available below the transcript (Go renders the whole choice list, so
  a page spans every row and the selected choice is either fully rendered or
  the block is blocked); key autorepeat from the digit that opened Other is not
  suppressed (bubbletea does not distinguish a repeat from a fresh press); and
  the `async_questions` shadow/overlap keymap validation pass is still not
  mirrored.

## TUI pass: async question countdown, queued navigation, buffered questions (#42903)

Closing the async-question chain's state-preservation slice.

- `tui/bottom_pane/async_questions.go`: each pending question now records its
  message id and an auto-resolution deadline (30s when it arrives while the
  editor is collapsed). `AppendAt` takes the model clock so the countdown is
  deterministic, `SnoozeAutoResolution` clears every deadline once the editor is
  opened or used, and `TimerRemaining`/`Countdown` drive the collapsed summary.
- `tui/tea/async_questions.go`: the collapsed summary mirrors Rust's two-part
  form (countdown in the last 20 seconds on the first line, the edit binding on
  its own line), refreshed by a one-second `bubbletea.Tick` loop that stops once
  no countdown is active; forward navigation from the last question collapses
  the editor and hands the key to the queued-message edit so the latest queued
  message becomes the main composer draft; and buffered live notifications from
  a background subagent introduce their questions when switching to that thread
  (historical replay still never does).
- `tui/bottom_pane/pending_input_preview.go`: while questions are pending the
  queued follow-up group stays visible (including its header when empty) and the
  duplicated "edit last queued message" hint yields to the question summary.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app ./appserver` clean;
  `gofmt -l` clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app ./protocol -count=1` green;
  `go test ./parity -count=1` green (`CODEX_RUST_ROOT` at `c4017a87aa`).
  Committed as `07a3dac`.
- Residuals: Rust also captures/restores question drafts, selections, expanded
  state, and seen IDs per thread (`capture`/`restore`) and disables delivery
  while the thread is unavailable (`pause_unavailable_thread`); Go's tea model
  has no per-thread input-state store and no thread-unavailable state, so both
  have no Go target (architecture-different). The composer-inline-input and
  paste-burst flush refinements and key-autorepeat suppression also remain, as
  does the `async_questions` shadow/overlap keymap validation pass.

## TUI pass: managed worktree commands and browser (#43120/#43286/#43942)

The managed-worktree core (`tui/worktree_browser.go`) and the worktree-aware
session discovery were already ported, but nothing in the TUI used them: there
was no `/worktree` command, no checkout picker, and no worktree browser.

- `tui/tea/worktree_browser.go`: the `/worktree` chooser ("Continue current
  conversation" / "Start new conversation" / "Browse worktrees"), the
  asynchronous browser listing with Rust's stale-request gating, owner-aware
  rows with search filtering, the resume/copy/delete action list, the delete
  confirmation, and the removal result. Creation runs through a
  `StartManagedWorktreeFunc` hook and owner metadata through an
  `OnWorktreeOwnerLookup` hook; the owner resume reuses the existing session
  resume path and copy uses the clipboard hook.
- `tui/worktree_browser.go`: exports `ManagedWorktreeRepositoryAvailable` (the
  local Git-repository prerequisite via `gitutil.DiscoverGitRoot`) and
  `WorktreePathWithin` (the delete guard), and the model checks availability
  exactly as Rust does (worktrees feature on, local worktree operations, Git
  repository).
- Command gating: `/worktree` is registered in the slash command set, is
  unavailable during a task (Rust `available_during_task` false), and reports
  Rust's messages - "Enable worktrees in your Codex configuration to create a
  worktree." when the feature is off, "Unrecognized command '/worktree'" when
  local operations are disabled, and "Managed worktrees require a local Git
  repository." outside a repository. Remote sessions receive only the feature
  flag, so managed operations stay local.
- `app`: `interactiveWorktreeSettings` resolves the feature flag and the
  desktop worktree pool; `interactiveWorktreeOwnerLookup` reads owner thread
  summaries from the session store; `interactiveStartManagedWorktreeHandler`
  creates the checkout, forks (`ForkAll` + cwd retarget) or starts a fresh
  thread inside it, and binds the thread to the worktree, cleaning the checkout
  up if any step fails.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, including new model tests
  for availability, chooser creation, stale-result rejection, search, actions,
  copy, the delete guard, and removal, plus app-level integration tests that
  create a real managed checkout from a temp Git repository and assert the
  fork/new thread binding and owner lookup. Committed as `ce7a080`.
- Residuals: the browser list is windowed by terminal height but not
  mouse/scroll-driven; Rust's worktree "shell" and stale-request guards for
  working-directory changes (#43298/#43376) are not mirrored; `/worktree` with
  arguments is treated as a prompt because the command takes no inline args in
  Go (`CommandSupportsInlineArgs`); and the remote picker does not yet surface
  Rust's disabled-command snapshot copy.

## TUI pass: thread-switch transcript reset (#43994)

Rust resets transcript state before replaying a replacement thread so history
queued by the previous thread cannot repopulate the new one. Go replaced
`State.Messages` on every switch but kept transcript-scoped derived state.

- `tui/tea/thread_reset.go`: `resetThreadScopedState` clears the tool-call
  display maps (both the model's fields and the embedded `TranscriptComponent`'s
  shadowed copies, which the two halves of the code use), the thread
  start/completion trackers, transcript separators and the last turn error, the
  rendered file-change cache, retry and compaction activity, the tool-request
  runtime, review mode, pending/rejected steers, and any open transcript
  overlay. Composer drafts, pending async questions, queued follow-ups, and
  settings are preserved.
- Wired into every thread transition: the app-server `thread.started`
  replacement, resume/fork (`applyResumeResponse`), agent switch, `/new` and
  `/clear` (`startFreshNamedSession`), and `/cd`
  (`applyWorkingDirectoryChangeResult`).
- Verification: `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, including new
  `thread_reset_test.go` coverage for all four transition paths. Committed as
  `36388f5`.

## TUI pass: server model metadata for restoration and status (#43360/#43359)

Resumed threads kept the local model, and `/status` reported the local provider,
because the TUI dropped the app-server thread metadata.

- `SessionSummary` gains `Model` (populated from the session record metadata and
  from the app-server thread's `model`), and `AgentThreadSwitchResponse` gains
  `Model`/`Provider`; the local (`app/agent_tui.go`) and remote
  (`app/remote_tui.go`) switch handlers populate both.
- `applyResumeResponse`, `applyWorkingDirectoryChangeResult`, and
  `applyAgentSwitchResult` apply the server-reported model/provider when
  present, leaving the current selection untouched when the server omits them.
- `tui/state.go`: the status card now shows the model provider id verbatim once
  a thread is attached (Rust #43359), so built-in providers are no longer
  hidden, while the entry stays absent before any thread exists.
- Verification: `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for the
  summary metadata, resume/switch application, and the status-card gating.
  Committed as `e1e1db2`.
- Residuals: Go's `thread/started` protocol event still carries only the thread
  id, so the Rust behavior of applying the model from that notification is not
  mirrored (the local embedded path resolves the model locally); and the default
  provider id is not resolved when the user config leaves `model_provider`
  unset, so `/status` omits the provider line for the implicit OpenAI provider
  whereas Rust prints `openai`.

## Remaining untriaged TUI commits (next passes)

The audit range still has TUI commits with no plan status; the Go-feasible
candidates are `#43253` (read-only conversations when resume meets an active
writer), `#43177`/`#43261` (server model defaults for fresh TUI startup and
background tasks), `#43340`/`#43330` (remote named permission profiles and
preserving saved permissions on remote resume/fork), `#43576` (group adjacent
computer actions), `#43889` (transcript viewer restoration/half-page scrolling),
and `#44027` (image attachments in agents-overview background tasks). The rest
are CI/release/Python-SDK/Guardian/voice or Rust-stack specific and stay out of
scope.

## TUI pass: image attachments in agents-overview background tasks (#44027)

The dashboard submitted its new-task prompt as plain text, so pasted images were
lost, and a remote workspace could not have read local image paths.

- `tui/agents_overview`: the view renders pending attachment labels above the
  new-task prompt (`SetAttachments`/`AttachmentLabels`), and the body height
  accounts for those rows.
- `tui/tea`: `AgentsOverviewDispatchFunc` now takes a `SubmitRequest` (prompt
  plus attachments); the dashboard's ctrl+v pastes a clipboard image when one
  exists and otherwise inserts text, bracketed paste goes to the dashboard
  input instead of the hidden main composer, a successful dispatch consumes the
  attachments, and a failed dispatch restores the prompt and images - or
  reports their paths for reattachment when a newer draft is present.
- `app`: `agentsOverviewTaskInputs` builds the first turn's inputs (images
  first, then the prompt), snapshotting local images into bounded data URLs when
  the endpoint is not local (Rust `snapshot_local_user_input`), and the remote
  dashboard rejects images before starting a thread when the resolved model is
  text-only (`model/list` modalities; servers that cannot list models skip the
  check).
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new coverage for the
  rendered attachment labels, the dispatch payload, failure restoration and
  reattachment notice, the input builder (local vs snapshotted), and the
  data-URL MIME detection. Committed as `81fbcc9`.
- Residuals: Go snapshots the source bytes under a 32 MiB sanity guard where
  Rust decodes and resizes the image to fit (`PromptImageMode::ResizeToFit`),
  so very large images may be rejected instead of downscaled; the dashboard
  input is plain text, so Go does not build mention text elements for the first
  task prompt; and the local embedded TUI keeps its "shared agents unavailable"
  branch (Rust's embedded overview is the same N/A surface).

## Triage dispositions (evidence-based, this pass)

- `095da4b7e8` #43889 "Fix transcript viewer restoration and half-page
  scrolling" - **already satisfied (architecture-different)**. Go's transcript
  overlay is a bubbletea viewport: half-page scrolling is
  `viewport.HalfPageUp/Down` (Rust's `page_height` fix exists so a
  partially-filled viewer scrolls by half of the rendered content height, which
  the viewport already models and clamps), and `setRawOutputMode` already
  re-syncs the overlay when one is open instead of reflowing the inline
  transcript (`if m.overlay != nil { m.syncTranscriptOverlay() }`). No Go
  change is warranted; the terminal-restore half is Rust's custom terminal
  backend, which Go does not have.

## TUI pass: server model defaults for remote new threads and tasks (#43177/#43261)

Remote thread starts always sent the client's locally-resolved model and
reasoning effort, so the app server's own configuration for the destination
directory was ignored.

- `app/remote_tui_managed_defaults.go`: `remoteNewThreadModelDefaults` now reads
  `config/read` for the destination cwd (`IncludeLayers`) and returns the
  server's effective config alongside the managed requirements defaults; a
  failed `config/read` still leaves the launch selection untouched (the older-
  server fallback).
- New `applyServerEffectiveLaunchDefaults` seeds `model` and
  `model_reasoning_effort` from the server's effective config when the launch
  did not choose them explicitly - i.e. no CLI `--model`, no generic `-c key=`
  override, and no selected-profile user layer per
  `config.HasLaunchSetting`. When the server configures no model it falls back
  to the server model catalog's default (`isDefault`, else the first entry) and
  finally to the bootstrap value.
- Applied to the remote interactive thread start
  (`remoteManagedThreadStartParams`) and to the agents-overview background-task
  start, which now also reads config for the task's destination directory
  (`#43261`).
- Test doubles that assert the exact remote RPC sequence now answer
  `model/list` (the catalog lookup the new default adds).
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, including the restored
  #44693 end-to-end launch-defaults tests and a new precedence table for the
  server-vs-explicit choice. Commits `243eef0` and `5462f20`.
- Residuals: Rust's fast-mode-specific catalog fallback (resolving a fallback
  model when the server clears the model and fast mode is enabled) is not
  modeled; Go applies the catalog default only when the server configures no
  model. The embedded/local TUI keeps its local config resolution, which is the
  same source the server would read.

## Process note (this pass): restore the #44693 launch-defaults test file

The `#43177` commit accidentally replaced
`app/remote_tui_managed_defaults_test.go` wholesale (dropping the #44693
end-to-end coverage). Commit `5462f20` restores the original file - its only
diff from the pre-incident revision is the new `model/list` handler - and keeps
the new precedence test in `app/remote_tui_launch_defaults_test.go`.

## TUI pass: apply app-server thread settings (#43330/#43340 mechanism)

The remote TUI dropped `thread/settings/updated` entirely, so a resumed or
forked task kept the previous task's permissions, model, cwd, and provider -
the state #43330 and #43340 exist to fix.

- `tui/tea/thread_settings.go`: `ThreadSettingsUpdatedMsg` plus
  `applyThreadSettingsUpdated`, which applies the active thread's cwd, model,
  model provider, reasoning effort, service tier, approval policy, sandbox, and
  personality to the TUI state. Optional settings (effort/service tier/
  personality) clear the local override when the server omits them, matching
  Rust's `Option` assignment; the service-tier commands are refreshed and the
  apps scope is invalidated on a cwd change. Updates for other threads are
  ignored, matching Rust's thread routing.
- `app/remote_tui.go`: the remote client handles
  `NotificationThreadSettingsUpdated` and forwards it thread-scoped to the
  model.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for the
  applied settings, the null-clearing behavior, other-thread gating, and the
  notification routing. Committed as `c7f783f`.
- Residuals: Go's state has no named `activePermissionProfile` field, so
  #43340's named-profile *selection* surface and the profile half of the
  applied settings remain unported (approval policy and sandbox policy do
  apply); the thread's `collaborationMode` map is not applied (Go models plan
  mode separately and applies model/effort directly); and #43330's rejection of
  explicit permission overrides on remote resume/fork has no Go target because
  Go's remote resume/fork requests carry no permission overrides at all (they
  are already omitted, so the previous task's settings are not sent).

## TUI pass: adjacent computer actions grouped (#43576)

MCP calls to the `cua_repl` server each rendered as their own history cell, so a
run of computer actions filled the transcript. Rust groups adjacent CUA calls
into one compact cell; Go now does the same.

- `tui/history_cell/computer_activity.go` ports
  `history_cell/computer_activity.rs`: `ComputerActivityCell` groups calls
  (replay-deduplicated) with start/complete/mark-failed, renders the
  "Using/Used computer · N actions [· M failed]" header, selects preview rows by
  failure and screenshot presence (up to three, or the active call alone while
  running, chronological order preserved), previews a failure through its first
  error or `Script error:` line, shows the "Captured screenshot · <title>"
  summary, and appends the "N more · ctrl+t" hint. `TranscriptLines` renders
  every grouped call's full detail and `RawLines` the untruncated transcript.
  `IsComputerActivityServer` mirrors `McpInvocation::is_computer_activity`
  (server == `cua_repl`).
- `tui/history_cell/mcp.go`: `McpToolResult` gains `HasImage`, set from
  `image`/`image_url` result blocks so previews can prefer screenshots.
- `tui/tea`: `renderMCPToolCallItem` folds calls whose server is `cua_repl` into
  one group history message (upserted in place); any other item - including
  non-CUA MCP calls and agent messages - flushes the group, as do turn
  completion/interruption and active-call failure (which also marks the running
  calls failed). Groups with running calls stay open, matching Rust's active-cell
  semantics. The model now records the CUA server's `HasImage` results.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for grouping
  and replay dedup, active/failure/screenshot previews, the hidden-rows hint,
  the title fallback, the transcript/interruption paths, and the model-level
  boundaries (one grouped message per run, new group after another item or an
  interrupted turn). Committed as `61cd8df`.
- Residuals: Rust's header uses the animated activity indicator with the
  configured motion mode; Go history cells render a static bullet (Go's
  animation lives in the status row), so the grouped header does not animate.
  Call durations are not shown in the compact rows (Rust's MCP cell tracks them
  but the compact CUA preview does not render them either).

## TUI pass: read-only conversations when another app owns the thread (#43253)

The remote TUI resumed threads with `thread/read`, so it never noticed that
another app owns the conversation (turns would fail later) and it lost the
thread's saved settings. Rust resumes through the app server and falls back to a
read-only history snapshot on the active-writer conflict.

- `app/remote_tui.go`: the remote resume handler now calls `thread/resume`
  first, builds the TUI response from the resumed thread (summary, messages,
  status, token usage, reasoning headings), and carries the response's thread
  settings through a new `remoteTUISettingsFromResume` (cwd, model, provider,
  effort, service tier, approval policy and reviewer, sandbox, active permission
  profile, disabled plugins, workspace roots). When the resume fails with the
  active-writer conflict (`remoteTUIResumeConflict`, message match on "already
  has an active writer") it falls back to `thread/read` with `ReadOnly` set;
  other errors propagate.
- `tui/tea`: `SessionResumeResponse` gains `ThreadSettings` and `ReadOnly`;
  `applyResumeResponse` applies the settings through the shared applier and
  enters/leaves the external-writer state.
- `tui/tea/read_only_thread.go` ports `ExternalWriterNotice`: the composer is
  replaced by a card ("🔒 This conversation is open in another app" with the
  right-aligned "R to Retry" and the "Close it there and press R to continue
  here." line), the footer advertises `r` retry / `Esc`, `Ctrl+C`, `q` exit /
  the transcript shortcut, `R` re-runs the resume (and a successful retry clears
  the state through `applyResumeResponse`), and every other key plus the
  submit/queue/steer paths are blocked.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for the
  notice rendering and input blocking, the retry path (including applying the
  resumed settings), the advertised keys, the conflict detection, and the
  resume-settings mapping. Committed as `279426d`.
- Residuals: the conflict is detected by matching the app-server error text
  where Rust matches a typed error; the local embedded resume handler still
  reads the store directly, so an active writer held by another process is not
  detected on that path (its `thread/resume` path is only used by the remote
  client); and the read-only card does not freeze animations the way Rust does
  (Go has no active turn while read-only, so nothing animates).

## TUI pass: remote named permission profile selection (#43340)

The `/permissions` picker only offered built-in presets and never told the
connected server about a selection, so named remote profiles were unusable.

- `app/remote_tui_permissions.go`: `remoteTUIListPermissionProfiles` mirrors
  `permission_discovery::fetch`'s bounded pagination (limit 100, up to ten
  pages, cursor loop guard, duplicate-ID rejection, the "Permission discovery
  exceeded its pagination limit. Try /permissions again." error) and maps the
  server's `permissionProfile/list` summaries to the picker's custom profiles;
  an older server (method-not-found, or the invalid-request
  "permissionProfile/list"/"configRequirements/read"/"config/read" messages)
  maps to `ErrNamedPermissionProfilesUnsupported`.
  `interactiveRemoteUpdateThreadPermissions` selects a profile through
  `thread/settings/update` (`permissions` field) with the same unsupported
  mapping. Both hooks are wired into the remote TUI options.
- `tui/tea`: opening `/permissions` starts discovery (the result refreshes the
  open menu and is cached), puts the picker in explicit-profile mode, and lists
  the server's profiles. Selecting a non-built-in profile asks the server and
  tracks the selection until the `thread/settings/updated` confirmation clears
  it (Rust #43340 pending-selection tracking). Guards mirror Rust's messages:
  a pending selection or a running turn block further changes ("Wait for
  permissions to update before changing permissions." / "Wait for the current
  turn to finish before changing permissions."), a disconnected thread blocks
  selection ("Wait for the task to connect before selecting permissions."), a
  failure preserves the current permissions and reports "Failed to select
  permissions: ...", an older server reports "Named profiles require a newer
  app server.", a pending selection blocks `/cd` ("Wait for permissions to
  update before changing directories."), and an active named remote profile
  refuses `/cd` ("Changing directories with a named profile is not
  supported.").
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for the
  picker listing discovered profiles, the server-routed selection and pending
  confirmation, the pending/running/unsupported/failure guards, the directory
  guards, the discovery pagination/duplicate/unsupported mapping, and the RPC
  error mapping. Committed as `f547280`.
- Residuals: Rust threads an explicit `selected_profile` through forked, side,
  and resumed session starts so the choice survives forks and prompt edits, and
  it reconciles pending selections on reconnect; Go applies the server's
  confirmation through `thread/settings/updated` instead and does not carry the
  selection through fork/side starts or reconcile it explicitly on reconnect.
  Rust also blocks *forks* while a selection is pending (Go blocks permission
  and directory changes but not forks), applies a 10-second discovery timeout
  (Go relies on the TUI request context), and prechecks `config/read`'s
  `default_permissions` before listing profiles (Go lists unconditionally).

## TUI pass: deferred directory-change guards (#43376) and tmux size monitor (#43603)

- `#43376` "Defer resume picker and directory changes to a fresh TUI stack" -
  **functional guards ported; the loop deferral is N/A**. Rust queues `/cd` for
  a fresh event-loop iteration (to bound stack usage) and rechecks the source
  session before applying it; Go's bubbletea command already runs the change
  off the update (goroutine stacks grow dynamically), so the deferral itself has
  no Go counterpart. The guards did: `applyWorkingDirectoryChangeCommand` now
  rejects another `/cd` while one is pending and reports Rust's remote message
  ("Changing directories is not supported for remote workspaces or remote
  execution environments.") when no local change handler exists;
  `WorkingDirectoryChangeResultMsg` carries the source directory and the result
  applies only when the source thread and directory are still current, clearing
  the pending flag on every completion path. Tests cover the concurrent
  rejection, both stale-result rechecks, the remote message, and the pending
  flag lifecycle. Committed as `04864e5`.
- `#43603` "Recover missed tmux resize notifications in the TUI" - **done**.
  tmux can drop SIGWINCH, leaving the TUI at a stale size; Rust polls the
  terminal size under tmux and synthesizes the resize. `tui/tea/size_monitor.go`
  mirrors the gate (Unix under tmux only; Windows and non-tmux terminals keep
  bubbletea's own SIGWINCH handling), polls every 500ms through an injectable
  seam (`Options.TerminalSize`, defaulting to the process terminal), re-applies
  the layout on a change, and ignores unchanged sizes and probe failures. `Init`
  schedules the poll when enabled. Tests cover the gate, the recovered/stale
  sizes, the probe failure, and the tick gating. Committed as `6e028c5`.
- `#43355` "Let the app server resolve implicit model settings for CLI forks" -
  **N/A (no interactive CLI fork startup in Go)**. Rust's `codex fork` launches
  the TUI on the forked session, and the change clears implicit model/reasoning
  overrides and skips the migration prompt during that startup. Go's top-level
  `codex fork` (and `codex resume`) are non-interactive session-management
  commands that print a summary - the interactive fork path exists only inside
  the TUI (`/fork`), whose remote params already omit a model override and let
  the server resolve it. The broader CLI difference (Rust's `resume`/`fork` open
  the TUI, Go's print summaries) predates this sync range and is not driven by
  any upstream commit in it; it is recorded here as an observation, not a
  range task.
- `#43298` "Defer managed worktree transitions to fresh TUI loop iterations" -
  **N/A (same loop-deferral architecture as #43376)**. The staged futures exist
  to bound Rust's TUI thread stack; Go's goroutine stacks grow, and the worktree
  creation/browser already run through deferred commands with stale-request
  guards.

## TUI pass: open tasks managed elsewhere read-only in the command center (#44969)

The command center attached to a task by reading it, so a task another app
server owned could not be opened at all (and the TUI never requested the
server-owned writer or the thread settings). Rust #44969 falls back to a frozen
read-only history snapshot and keeps the existing external-writer view.

- `app/remote_tui.go`: `interactiveRemoteSwitchAgentThread` now calls
  `thread/resume` first (server owns the writer and returns the thread's
  settings), and on the active-writer conflict (`remoteTUIResumeConflict`) reads
  the thread with turns and returns `ReadOnly: true`. The response is built by a
  shared `remoteTUIAgentSwitchResponseFromThread` for both paths.
- `tui/tea`: `AgentThreadSwitchResponse` gains `ReadOnly` and `ThreadSettings`;
  `applyAgentSwitchResult` applies the resumed settings
  (`applyThreadSettingsValues`), enters the external-writer state
  (`setReadOnlyThread`, which protects the composer while preserving the
  restored draft), and skips buffered request replay for a read-only snapshot so
  the frozen history is not rewritten. The existing read-only notice's `r` retry
  re-runs `onResumeSession` (resume-first + read-only fallback), so a released
  task can be attached without leaving the conversation.
- Also removed the stale `/personality` tooltip left by the #44935 TUI pass
  (`tui/tooltips.txt`), matching Rust's current asset.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with a new app test for the
  conflict->read-only fallback and a new tea test for the applied snapshot
  (read-only flag, resumed cwd, protected composer).
- Residuals: Go's task-open path shares `OnSwitchAgent` with the subagent
  picker, so both routes now resume; Rust only resumes the command-center open.
  Go's single-view model has no bottom-pane "active view", so #44969's
  paste-while-read-only-in-command-center clause is N/A (the dashboard is closed
  while a read-only conversation is displayed, and the composer stays blocked).
  The `thread/read` snapshot is used as-is (Rust's `read_thread_for_viewing`);
  the startup-future boxing half is a Rust stack-size concern with no Go target.

## TUI pass: task tokens and usage estimates in the command center (#44970)

The dashboard's task details had no token or billing information. Rust #44970
shows the task's token totals and estimated credits/cost, fetched on demand for
the selected task and refreshed on a one-minute cadence.

- `tui/status/usage_format.go` ports `format_credit_micros` (compact K/M/B/T
  with one decimal, half-up rounding, computed with `math/big` to match Rust's
  i128 math) and `format_estimated_usd_micros` (negative -> absent, sub-cent
  zero-padded forms, `~$` values), with the Rust rounding vectors as tests.
- `tui/agents_overview`: `View.SetUsageLines(threadID, lines)` stores the
  pre-rendered usage lines shown after the details' model row (Rust
  `details.usage_lines`); `ApplyRefresh` preserves them and prunes threads that
  left the list. When usage lines are present and the details would exceed the
  pane, the original prompt is dropped so usage keeps its space (Rust #44970's
  activity/usage precedence).
- `tui/tea/agents_overview_usage.go`: per-task usage cache (live token totals +
  estimate + fetched-at), a pending-request guard, the plan gate (Business /
  Enterprise usage-based / Enterprise automation), and the disabled capability
  latch. `refreshAgentsOverviewUsageCmd` fetches only when a task's cached value
  is missing or at least 60s old and otherwise schedules the next check with
  `bubbletea.Tick`; `applyAgentsOverviewUsageLoaded` keeps a prior nonzero
  credits/cost and its breakdown when a settlement reports zero, clears every
  estimate (but keeps live tokens) on `Disabled`, and ignores results for tasks
  that are no longer listed. Live totals for background tasks come from
  `thread.token_usage.updated` notifications, which the remote client now
  forwards thread-scoped instead of dropping for non-active threads.
- `app/agents_overview_tui.go`: `interactiveRemoteAgentsOverviewUsage` calls
  `account/usage/read` with the task's `threadId` and maps the response
  (`threadUsage` absent -> disabled), summing only complete non-negative
  breakdown groups into the complete totals.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for the
  formatter vectors, the details usage rendering and prompt precedence, the
  fetch/cadence/gates, zero-settlement preservation, the disabled latch, the
  plan-change clear, the incomplete-breakdown rendering, the app-server reader,
  and the background token-usage routing.
- Residuals: Go has no per-frame draw hook, so the lazy check runs on dashboard
  refresh, selection change, and the scheduled 60s tick rather than on every
  frame; there is no account-updated/lagged message in the Go TUI, so the cache
  clears on a plan-type change (rate-limit snapshot) rather than on Rust's
  `AccountUpdated`/`Lagged` events; and the shared `thread/list` refresh is
  still authoritative for rows, so usage lines are re-applied after each refresh.

## TUI dispositions: #44870 TUI half and #44879 (evidence-based, this pass)

- `68bc5369ba` #44870 enable worktrees by default - **core + disabled-message done;
  daemon background-terminal guard N/A**. The `worktrees` feature is already
  `StageStable` + `DefaultEnabled` (`features/features.go`), it is absent from
  the experimental menu (`tui/chatwidget/settings_test.go` pins that), and the
  disabled message already matches Rust post-#44870 ("Enable worktrees in your
  Codex configuration to create a worktree.", `tui/tea/worktree_browser.go`).
  The remaining half adds `background_terminals_blocker` to Rust's
  `managed_worktree_creation.rs` / `working_directory.rs` so a *local daemon*
  connection surfaces "The local Codex service cannot check background
  terminals. Run `codex app-server daemon update`..." when
  `thread/backgroundTerminals/list` is missing. Go has no such path: the remote
  TUI (including a local daemon endpoint) sets neither
  `OnWorkingDirectoryChange` nor `LocalWorktreeOperations`, so `/cd` is rejected
  with "Changing directories is not supported for remote workspaces or remote
  execution environments." before any background-terminal RPC, and managed
  worktree creation stays local-only (`app/interactive.go`). Go's embedded
  `/cd` forks the session record directly and never calls the RPC, so there is
  nothing to guard. A faithful port would require the local-daemon `/cd` and
  worktree-creation surfaces first (the pre-existing gap noted in the #43253
  pass).
- `33bdf976cc` #44879 fade Astra composer stars and stabilize cursor redraws -
  **N/A (no sparkle composer)**, unchanged: Go has no Astra/Nova composer
  animation or `chat_composer/sparkle.rs` equivalent, so the fade/cursor-cache
  change has no target.

## Non-TUI core: staged enterprise OIDC login and coordinated logout (#43844, part 1)

Rust `b090e901f8` (#43844) adds staged enterprise OIDC login plus a
cross-process logout generation in `rmcp-client`. This pass ports the
self-contained credential-coordination half and its prerequisites:

- `mcp/oauth_enterprise_generation.go` mirrors
  `oauth/enterprise_generation.rs`: the generation file lives under
  `$CODEX_HOME/mcp-oauth-locks/<sha256(storeKey)>.enterprise-generation`, opens
  with private permissions and rejects a non-regular path, `current()` accepts
  only an empty file or exactly one 32-byte generation, and `replace()` writes a
  fresh SHA-256 over 32 random bytes (unpadded base64url, matching
  `CsrfToken::new_random_len`) with a sync. Reads/writes are documented as
  requiring the credential lock.
- `mcp/enterprise_oauth_login.go` ports the policy helpers and credentials:
  `enterpriseCallbackSettings` (registered client ID required, HTTP loopback
  callback only, URL/listener port agreement, Rust's error text),
  `enterpriseAuthorizationURL` (drop `resource` and any existing `prompt`,
  append `prompt=consent`, preserving the remaining pairs),
  `validateEnterpriseOAuthCredentials` (refresh token + valid OIDC identity
  assertion, credential-free error text), `EnterpriseOAuthCredentialGuard` /
  `DeleteEnterpriseOAuthTokens` (replace the generation, then delete the grant
  under the held lock; a false return means no grant while attempts are still
  invalidated), and `CommitEnterpriseOAuthCredentials[T]` (generation check,
  then the caller's authority proof, then persist - all under the same lock).
- `OAuthTokenSet` gains `IDToken` (captured from the exchange's `id_token` extra
  field and persisted in the fallback entry), because Rust validates the staged
  grant from the wrapped token response; this also unblocks a future EMA stored
  identity resolver (`stored_ema_identity_is_usable` reads the stored id_token).
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./mcp/...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
  `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green (new tests: generation lifecycle/name/cross
  process invalidation/invalid state, callback settings, authorization URL,
  credential validation, logout invalidation, staged commit generation and
  authority checks, id_token capture/persistence), plus `go test ./app/ -count=1`
  green.
- Residual (#43844): the enterprise login *handle*
  (`perform_enterprise_oauth_login_return_url` -> authorization URL -> staged
  completion) is not ported yet. It needs RMCP's strict issuer metadata
  discovery (`resolve_ema_idp_authorization_manager`: published metadata bound
  to the configured issuer, token-endpoint validation, public-client auth), and
  Go's discovery helpers do not reproduce RMCP's exact multi-candidate
  OIDC/RFC-8414 order in-repo. The flow's resource-stripping HTTP client is N/A
  for Go: `OAuthLoginSession` only sends a resource indicator when
  `Resource` is set, so an enterprise login that omits it never sends one.
  Rust's keyring-only storage is also architecture-different: Go's MCP OAuth
  store is the private `.credentials.json` fallback.

### #43844 part 2: enterprise login discovery, authorization, and staged completion

- `mcp/enterprise_oauth_flow.go` ports the remaining enterprise login surface:
  - `enterpriseDiscoveryURLs` / `enterpriseExpectedIssuerForDiscoveryURL` /
    `enterpriseIssuerIdentifiersMatch` mirror rmcp
    `AuthorizationManager::generate_discovery_urls`,
    `expected_issuer_for_authorization_metadata_url`, and
    `issuer_identifiers_match` exactly (the spec-2025-11-25 candidate order:
    root `oauth-authorization-server`, root `openid-configuration`, or, with a
    path, path-inserted OAuth, path-inserted OIDC, path-appended OIDC, then the
    canonical root fallback). The rmcp order was taken from the cached crate
    source (`rmcp-3.1.3/src/transport/auth.rs`).
  - `resolveEnterpriseAuthorizationMetadata` ports
    `resolve_ema_idp_authorization_manager`: the published metadata must be bound
    to the configured issuer (slash-tolerant at discovery, then an exact compare,
    matching Rust), the authorization and token endpoints are validated, and
    public-client token endpoint auth must be advertised. Candidate handling
    follows rmcp: a transport/context failure aborts, while a non-200 or
    malformed response tries the next candidate.
  - `StartEnterpriseOAuthLogin` captures (or initializes) the generation before
    discovery, discovers metadata, and builds the authorization URL with
    `openid offline_access` scopes and no resource indicator;
    `EnterpriseOAuthLoginHandle.Complete` exchanges the callback through the
    existing `OAuthLoginSession`, validates the grant, and returns staged
    `EnterpriseOAuthCredentials` without persisting. The caller commits with
    `CommitEnterpriseOAuthCredentials` (or `CommitGeneration`).
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./mcp/...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
  `go test ./mcp/ -count=1` green, with new tests over an httptest IdP for the
  strict discovery vectors (bound issuer, root-slash issuer, mismatched issuer,
  missing public-client auth, no published metadata) and the staged flow
  (authorization URL has `prompt=consent` and no `resource`, browser completion
  does not persist, the explicit commit stores the grant, and an incomplete
  grant is refused), plus `go test ./app/ -count=1` green.
- Residual (#43844): the handle owns no loopback listener (Go's
  `OAuthLoginServer` owns the callback server), so the caller supplies the bound
  redirect URL rather than the handle starting a browser and listener as Rust's
  `wait()` does; rmcp's resource-metadata-first discovery step is not repeated
  for an IdP issuer (the enterprise resolver only requires discovered
  authorization-server metadata, and an IdP publishes no protected-resource
  metadata); and the grant is stored in Go's private `.credentials.json`
  fallback rather than Rust's keyring-only store.
