## TUI pass: smooth summary shimmer in the status row (#43921 residual)

Rust #43921 replaced the stepped per-letter status highlight with a smooth,
grapheme-aware brightness sweep (`summary_shimmer.rs` +
`StatusIndicatorWidget`). Go still rendered the old stepped highlight from the
animation tick, so this pass ports the new behavior:

- `tui/summary_shimmer.go` ports `summary_shimmer`: a two-second sweep with the
  band half-width `max(width * 0.1, 3.0)` so short labels keep at least six
  columns of wave, per-grapheme blending of the terminal foreground into the
  background (`BlendRGB`, matching Rust's `blend`), reduced motion returning the
  text unstyled, and an unknown palette (or non-truecolor terminal) returning
  static dim text instead of animating. Graphemes come from `github.com/rivo/uniseg`.
- `tui/terminal_default_colors.go` ports the palette cache
  (`terminal_palette.rs` default colors): an attempted/value cache,
  `SetDefaultColorsFromStartupProbe`, and a `SetDefaultTerminalColorsForTest`
  seam (Rust's `with_test_default_colors`).
- `tui/tea`: the model records `workingHeaderStartedAt`, restarts the shimmer
  phase only when the header text changes (`StatusIndicatorWidget::update_header`
  semantics), seeds it when a turn starts (`syncTaskRunningTimer`) and clears it
  when the turn ends, and `renderWorkingHeader` now emits the shimmer spans
  (plain / dim / truecolor band) instead of the stepped single-letter highlight.
  All `workingStatusHeader` writers go through `setWorkingStatusHeader`.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
  `go test ./tui/... -count=1` green (new tests: the Rust midpoint brightness
  vector `[128,156,212,240,212,156,128]`, reduced-motion and unknown-palette
  fallbacks, the frame-to-frame smoothness/overlap rule, grapheme preservation
  for combining marks and ZWJ emoji, the palette cache seam, header-phase
  restart, and the status-row motion/fallback rendering), plus
  `go test ./app/ -count=1` green.
- Residual (#43921): Go's TUI never runs the terminal startup probe, so at
  runtime the palette cache reports the default colors as unavailable and the
  status header takes Rust's dim fallback; the animated truecolor path is
  implemented and unit-tested through the palette seam but has no runtime
  producer until Go gains a startup-probe/OSC 10-11 query (Rust's
  `terminal_probe::default_colors`). The remaining #43921 residuals (buffered
  replay `ReasoningReplay` boundary, `reasoning_recovered_after_refresh`
  reconciliation, unified-exec wait streak) are still open.

### #43921 shimmer follow-up: runtime terminal default-colors source

The shimmer added in the previous pass took Rust's dim fallback at runtime
because Go's palette cache was never populated. Rust's `terminal_palette`
prefers the startup probe's OSC colors and otherwise falls back to the native
console palette; Go now implements that fallback:

- `tui/terminal_default_colors.go`: the cache now lazily queries the platform
  source on first lookup (`Cache::get_or_init_with(query_default_colors)`
  semantics), `SetDefaultColorsFromStartupProbe` still short-circuits the query,
  and `DefaultTerminalColors()` exposes the Rust `default_colors()` accessor.
  The pure `decodeConsoleDefaultColors` ports
  `decode_console_default_colors` (attribute nibbles select the foreground and
  background palette entries; `COMMON_LVB_REVERSE_VIDEO` does not change them).
- `tui/terminal_default_colors_windows.go` queries
  `GetConsoleScreenBufferInfoEx` on `syscall.Stdout` with a byte-exact
  `CONSOLE_SCREEN_BUFFER_INFOEX` layout and decodes the color table, mirroring
  Rust's `query_console_default_colors`. `terminal_default_colors_other.go`
  reports the palette unavailable on other hosts.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
  `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new decode tests covering the
  Rust vectors (attribute intensity indices, COLORREF byte order, and
  reverse-video immunity) plus an explicit-unavailable path for the shimmer
  fallbacks. A piped-stdout probe confirmed the query fails gracefully
  (`default colors: unavailable`, color level truecolor) rather than crashing
  when stdout is not a console.
- Residual (#43921): Rust prefers the startup probe / OSC 10-11 response before
  the console palette, and the startup probe also feeds cursor and keyboard
  state. Go's TUI never runs that probe (bubbletea owns the terminal read
  path), so on Windows Go uses the console color table and on other hosts the
  palette stays unavailable; an OSC query over the TUI's own reader is the
  remaining work. The other #43921 residuals (buffered-replay
  `ReasoningReplay` boundary, `reasoning_recovered_after_refresh`,
  unified-exec wait-streak guard) remain open.

## TUI pass: task "Last message" in the agents-overview details (#44752 residual)

Rust's task details show the latest delivered agent message; Go's details pane
only had Prompt/Model/usage. This pass ports the section:

- `tui/agents_overview`: `Row.LastMessage` carries the previewed message, and
  `renderDetails` appends a blank line, the dim `Last message` label, and the
  message rendered through the injected markdown renderer (falling back to the
  plain width-wrapped preview), mirroring Rust
  `agents_overview_view::render_details`'s `row.details.last_message` block.
- `app/agents_dashboard.go`: the remote dashboard fills each task's message from
  its latest turn (`thread/turns/list` with `limit: 1`), taking the final
  `agentMessage` text and applying Rust's `preview_agent_message`
  (`markdown.UnwrapMarkdownFences` then the bounded preview). The local
  (no-daemon) fallback uses the session record's materialized items when present.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green (new tests: the details
  section with and without a message, the injected markdown renderer path, the
  `preview_agent_message` fence/limit behavior, the last-agent-message scan for
  both app-server and session items, and a scripted-transport dashboard refresh
  asserting the `thread/turns/list` limit and the resulting row message).
- Residuals: Rust also overrides the row preview from the latest turn's first
  user message and fans the per-thread reads out concurrently (16 at a time);
  Go keeps the server-derived `thread/list`/`thread/read` preview and issues the
  turns-list reads on its existing sequential dashboard read path. The local
  fallback only shows a message when the listing materialized items.

## TUI pass: unified-exec wait streak owns the status row (#43921 residual)

Rust guards the reasoning heading while a unified-exec wait streak is active, so
an empty-stdin background-terminal poll keeps the "Waiting for background
terminal" status until the process produces output. Go's chatwidget already had
the `CommandLifecycleState` wait-streak machine; the TUI never fed it.

- `app/remote_tui.go`: `terminalInteraction` notifications are forwarded to the
  TUI for the active thread as `codextea.TerminalInteractionMsg`, and
  command-execution items now carry their `processId` in item metadata alongside
  the source the TUI already reads.
- `tui/tea`: the model owns a `chatwidget.CommandLifecycleState`. Unified-exec
  startup command items register their process
  (`TrackUnifiedExecProcessBegin`), completion ends it and releases the streak
  (`noteUnifiedExecCommandCompleted`), and a terminal interaction routes through
  `OnTerminalInteraction`: an empty stdin starts/extends the streak and sets the
  waiting status, a typed stdin flushes it and renders the interaction cell, and
  a process change flushes the previous streak. `reasoningHeaderUpdateBlocked`
  now includes an active streak, so reasoning deltas cannot replace the waiting
  status; the flush clears the waiting header and re-derives the reasoning
  heading (`finishUnifiedExecWaitStreak` / `restoreReasoningStatusHeader`), and
  the waiting status renders the tracked command as the indicator detail line.
  Turn completion and failure flush any leftover streak.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green (new tests: the waiting status
  and reasoning guard, the untracked-process gate, flush on typed stdin and on
  process completion, the app notification routing and thread gating, and the
  processId metadata mapping).
- Residuals (#43921): `reasoning_recovered_after_refresh` (using a restored
  stream's complete item at finalization) and the buffered-replay
  `ReasoningReplay` boundary remain; Rust also drains the wait streak through its
  deferred-item/`InterruptManager` path, while Go routes the same events
  directly.

### #43921 residual: reconciled reasoning completion after a refresh

Rust marks a reasoning item restored by a thread switch/session refresh and, when
its completed item arrives, reconciles the (possibly partial) streamed deltas
with the item's complete summary. Go streamed deltas but finalized with whatever
was buffered.

- `app/remote_tui.go`: `remoteProtocolItemFromPayload` now maps a `reasoning`
  item, joining its `summary`/`reasoningContent`/`content` arrays (plus `text`)
  so the completed item carries the complete summary.
- `tui/tea`: the model records `reasoningRecoveredAfterRefresh` when a switch or
  resume restores an active reasoning item, clears it on
  `applyReasoningItemStarted`/finalization, and `completeReasoningItem` replaces
  the partial buffer with the completed item's complete summary (re-deriving the
  heading through the normal guard-aware path) before finalizing. Ordinary
  completions keep the streamed heading.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green (new tests: the reconciled
  completion and the ordinary-completion path, plus the reasoning item mapping);
  `go test ./parity/ -count=1` green.
- Residual (#43921): the buffered-replay `ReasoningReplay` boundary remains - Go
  relies on the server snapshot plus streaming deltas, so restoring a
  background thread's active item mid-replay is not implemented.

### Next (TUI residuals, evidence-based)

- `#43921` buffered-replay `ReasoningReplay` boundary: Go's remote client drops
  reasoning deltas for non-active threads (`NotificationReasoningSummaryTextDelta`
  / `...PartAdded` return early), and the active item is derived heuristically
  from the snapshot's trailing reasoning item of an in-progress turn
  (`remoteTUIThreadActiveReasoning`, which also lets `remoteTUIThreadMessagesFromThread`
  skip it). Rust instead tracks the active item client-side from buffered
  notifications and, during replay, removes it from the snapshot and re-attaches
  it when its first buffered event (or the end of replay) arrives. A faithful Go
  port therefore needs: (1) forwarding background reasoning-summary/part-added
  deltas as thread-scoped events into the bounded buffer, (2) per-thread active
  reasoning-item tracking in the model, and (3) a replay pass that suppresses the
  snapshot's active item and restores it mid-replay. Not started.
- Shimmer on non-Windows hosts: `platformTerminalDefaultColors` returns
  unavailable, so the animated truecolor path only engages on Windows consoles;
  the OSC 10/11 query over the TUI's own reader is the remaining source.
- User-verification TUI (`#43708`/`#43712`): still blocked on the
  `openai/userVerification` elicitation variant plus a native provider (Go's
  `userVerification/verify` always returns the typed `unavailable` error).

### #43921 residual closed: buffered-replay ReasoningReplay boundary

Rust buffers a background thread's events (including reasoning deltas) and, when
switching to it, restores the active reasoning item mid-replay so the live
heading survives deltas the bounded buffer already evicted. Go dropped
background reasoning deltas and only seeded the heading from the read snapshot.

- `app/remote_tui.go`: reasoning-summary text deltas and summary-part-added
  notifications for a non-active thread are forwarded thread-scoped (a part
  boundary maps to the newline delta Rust records) so the model's bounded
  per-thread buffer holds them; the active-thread path still records the
  client-side heading.
- `tui/tea`: `replayBufferedReasoning` ports the boundary. On a switch, after
  the snapshot heading is applied, a snapshot-identified active item wins;
  otherwise the first reasoning item with buffered deltas becomes the active
  item, its buffered summary lines seed the heading through the guard-aware
  `restoreReasoningStatusHeader`, and the buffer is consumed with the thread's
  other replayed events. This closes the residual recorded in the previous pass
  (supersedes its "not started" line).
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green (new tests: buffered restore on
  switch, snapshot-precedence, and the thread-scoped background-delta
  forwarding), plus `go test ./parity/ -count=1` green.
- Residuals (#43921): Rust also suppresses the tracked active item when the
  snapshot renders it as a completed transcript entry regardless of position; Go
  keeps its snapshot-side heuristic (the trailing reasoning item of an
  in-progress turn, `remoteTUIThreadActiveReasoning`) because the app-server
  reasoning item carries no status and the snapshot exposes no active-item
  marker.

## TUI pass: permission-discovery residuals closed (#43340)

The earlier #43340 port listed three residuals (fork guard, discovery timeout,
`config/read` precheck); a deeper comparison with Rust
`permission_discovery.rs` / `chatwidget/permission_discovery.rs` /
`permission_popups.rs` closed those and more:

- `app/remote_tui_permissions.go`: discovery now mirrors Rust's `fetch` -
  a remote thread without an active named profile first reads the effective
  config (`config/read`, no layers) and only discovers when
  `default_permissions` is a string, otherwise it returns no profiles in
  non-explicit mode; the whole request is bounded by Rust's 10-second budget
  with the `Permission discovery timed out. Try /permissions again.` message.
  `OnListPermissionProfiles` now reports the explicit-profile mode.
- `tui/tea`: opening `/permissions` shows Rust's "Update Model Permissions"
  loading view while discovery runs; a non-explicit result opens the legacy
  built-in preset popup and later opens skip discovery (Rust
  `open_permissions_popup` gating); a failed discovery shows the raw error with a
  `Retry` item that re-runs the request; and a successful named-mode result opens
  the named-profile popup as before. `/fork` and `/side` (both forks) are blocked
  while a named-profile selection is pending with Rust's
  `Wait for permissions to update before forking.` message.
- `tui/chatwidget/permissions.go`: named profiles now use Rust
  `PermissionDiscovery::disabled_reason` - the server's `allowed` flag or the
  requirements' allowed profiles/approval policies/reviewers disable the entry -
  instead of only the server flag.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green (new tests: the loading view
  and result replacement, the legacy fallback and its no-rediscovery gate, the
  retry view and re-run, the fork/side pending guards, the
  `default_permissions` precheck, the 10-second timeout, and the
  requirements-based named-profile disable reasons).
- Residual (#43340): Go has no `explicit_permission_profile_mode` config flag
  from the server, so the first `/permissions` always discovers once before it
  can know the server is in legacy mode (Rust knows from config); the local
  discovery override for session-flag permission layers and Rust's
  `PermissionDiscovery::local` preset composition are not modeled (Go's embedded
  TUI has no loader and uses the legacy popup directly).

## Newly discovered unported TUI feature (pre-range): backtrack / edit an earlier prompt

Rust `tui/src/app_backtrack.rs` (902 lines) implements Esc-to-edit-prompt:
the first `Esc` in the main view primes the feature with a base thread id, a
second `Esc` opens the transcript overlay and highlights a user message, and
`Enter` forks the thread *before* the selected turn and restores that prompt in
the new composer (`ForkSessionForPromptEdit`), with side-conversation
restrictions ("Editing previous prompts is unavailable in side conversations.")
and "No previous message to edit." when nothing is selectable. Go currently has
only the input-gating helpers (`tui/app/input.go` `ShouldHandleBacktrackEsc`
etc., with no consumer) and the tooltip text, and its fork API is
thread-scoped: Rust's `fork_thread_at` takes `last_turn_id` / `before_turn_id`
while Go's `SessionSelectionFork` carries only a thread id. The gap predates the
sync31 range (last touched by upstream #43676/#42199/#40696), so it was never in
a plan; it needs: (1) a turn-bounded fork in the app-server/session later, (2) a
selectable user-message list in the transcript overlay, (3) the Esc prime/confirm
state machine, and (4) composer restore. Recorded as the next sizeable TUI item.

## Residual inventory after this pass (TUI, evidence-based)

Small in-range residuals were closed this session (#44957 dashboard model patch,
#43340 discovery flow, #43921 shimmer/wait-streak/reasoning-reconciliation/
buffered replay, #44752 last message, enterprise OAuth). What remains each needs
a dedicated multi-file pass, and two were re-scoped after inspection:

- Composer text elements (#44027 mention residual, broader than recorded): Rust's
  composer records `text_elements` (byte ranges plus placeholders) for mentions
  and image placeholders and sends them in `UserInput::Text { text,
  text_elements }` from both the main composer and the dashboard. Go has no
  composer element model at all: `chatwidget.UserMessage.TextElements` and
  `turn.TurnUserInput.TextElements` exist but nothing populates them (the
  composer's `DraftState.InsertElement` only appends placeholder text, and
  `interactiveSubmitInputs`/`agentsOverviewTaskInputs` send plain text plus
  skill inputs derived from textual mention bindings). Closing this needs
  element tracking in the composer draft (ranges maintained across edits), an
  accessor through the composer/bottom pane, `SubmitRequest` plumbing, and the
  turn-input builders - a composer-core change rather than a dashboard tweak.
- Attachment image pipeline (#44027 residual): Rust reads up to
  `MAX_PROMPT_IMAGE_INPUT_BYTES` (1 GiB), decodes, resizes to fit 2048px with a
  triangle filter when larger, preserves the source bytes for preservable
  formats, and otherwise re-encodes as PNG while copying RGB ICC profiles and
  EXIF. Go enforces a 32 MiB guard and sends source bytes unresized (no decode,
  resize, or metadata copy). A faithful port needs image decode/encode plus
  ICC/EXIF chunk handling that Go's stdlib encoders do not provide.

  the section above.
- Non-Windows shimmer palette (OSC 10/11), user-verification TUI
  (#43708/#43712, provider-blocked), and Rust's non-trailing active-reasoning
