### Permission-discovery timeout mapping fix and clean repo-wide verification

The full-suite run exposed that the new timeout test could see the transport's
read deadline as a bare `net.Error` timeout (`read pipe: i/o timeout`) before the
context observed its own deadline, so `remoteTUIListPermissionProfiles` now maps
the elapsed budget through `remoteTUIPermissionDiscoveryTimedOut` (context
deadline, `os.ErrDeadlineExceeded`, or a `net.Error` timeout) to Rust's
`Permission discovery timed out. Try /permissions again.` message; the earlier
budget widening alone was not the root cause.

With that fix, and with the parallel voice process's audio work committed
(`d1e6831`), a full `go test ./... -count=1` is green again: **92 packages `ok`,
no `FAIL`/`panic`**, so the recorded voice-WIP caveat above no longer applies to
the current tree.

## TUI pass: prompt image decode/resize pipeline (#44027 residual)

Rust's `snapshot_local_user_input` reads a bounded image, decodes it, resizes it
to fit 2048px when needed, and either preserves the source bytes (PNG/JPEG/WebP)
or re-encodes (GIF as PNG). Go's dashboard sent source bytes under a 32 MiB guard
without decoding, so large images were rejected and non-preservable formats were
sent with a sniffed MIME.

- `utils/promptimage.go`: implements `LoadForPromptBytes` /
  `LoadForPromptBytesWithLimits` and upgrades `LoadDataURLForPrompt` to mirror
  Rust `load_for_prompt_bytes` / `load_data_url_for_prompt`: magic-byte format
  detection (PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP), decode (`golang.org/x/image/webp` for WebP),
  `ResizeToFit` (fit within `MaxDimension` with bilinear scaling, the closest Go
  equivalent of Rust's triangle filter), `Original` passthrough, and
  `ResizeWithLimits` through the existing dimension helpers. Byte preservation
  applies to PNG/JPEG/WebP when no resize is needed; GIF is always re-encoded as
  PNG; JPEG re-encodes at quality 85 with alpha flattened onto white (Rust
  `encode_image`). `EncodedImage` gains `SourceWidth`/`SourceHeight`.
- `app/agents_overview_media.go`: `localImageDataURL` now reads the file under
  Rust's `MAX_PROMPT_IMAGE_INPUT_BYTES` (1 GiB) bound and runs the pipeline in
  ResizeToFit mode, returning a data URL with the detected format's MIME; the
  32 MiB guard and the extension/content-sniff MIME helper are gone.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./utils/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./utils/ ./app/ -count=1` green, with new tests for
  byte-preservation (PNG/JPEG), GIF-to-PNG conversion, the 3000x100 -> 2048x68
  ResizeToFit vector, Original mode, unsupported/undecodable payloads, the
  ResizeWithLimits entry point, and the dashboard snapshot end to end.
- Residuals (#44027): Rust copies an RGB ICC profile and EXIF (orientation) into
  re-encoded output; Go's stdlib encoders cannot, so re-encoded images lose them
  (the same documented gap as the event image-prep port), and a resized WebP is
  re-encoded as PNG because Go has no WebP encoder. Rust's content-digest image
  cache is not ported (Go has no repeated-prepare hot path).

### #43340 residual closed: explicit-profile config syntax

Rust's `Config::explicit_permission_profile_mode` is true when an explicit
default profile or the profiles config syntax is present; Go's discovery
precheck only recognized a string `default_permissions`, so a config that defines
a `[permissions]` table without a default still fell back to the legacy preset
popup instead of discovering the server's named profiles.

- `app/remote_tui_permissions.go`: `remoteTUIExplicitPermissionProfileConfig`
  mirrors Rust's gate - a string `default_permissions` or a `permissions` table
  with at least one profile definition (ignoring a `default` id entry) puts the
  server in explicit-profile mode, so the precheck discovers rather than
  returning the legacy (non-explicit) result.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./app ./utils` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
  `go test ./app/ ./tui/... ./utils/ -count=1` green, with a new table test for
  the explicit-mode vectors (default profile, profiles table, persisted id only,
  empty, unrelated).
- Residual (#43340): Rust's `persisted_profile_id_was_provided` half of the gate
  (a config-level persisted profile id) is not modeled separately, and Rust's
  local-discovery override for session-flag permission layers and
  `PermissionDiscovery::local` preset composition remain unported (Go's embedded
  TUI has no loader and uses the legacy popup directly).

### #43340 residual re-assessment: selected_profile threading (evidence-based)

The earlier residual said Rust threads an explicit `selected_profile` through
forked, side, and resumed starts so the choice survives. Current-state analysis
shows Go already matches the observable behavior:

- Remote fork: Rust's `fork_thread_at_with_presentation` clears
  `permissions`/`approval_policy`/`approvals_reviewer`/`sandbox` and the
  permission config overrides when the TUI is in remote mode with
  `ForkPermissionMode::InheritSaved`, so the server inherits the source thread's
  saved settings. Go's remote `/fork` sends no permission fields at all.
- Side fork: Rust passes `selected_profile = None`; Go likewise (the side fork
  params carry no permissions).
- Embedded fork: Rust applies the selected profile to the fork params; Go's
  embedded `/fork` forks the session record, and `session.Metadata` carries
  `ActivePermissionProfile` / `ApprovalPolicy` / `SandboxPolicy`, which the store
  fork preserves, so the new thread inherits the saved profile.

Consequently no `selected_profile` threading edit is required for the current
architectures; the remaining #43340 items are the persisted-profile-id half of
the explicit-mode gate and Rust's local-discovery override (recorded above).

### #44027 residual closed: RGB ICC and EXIF preserved on re-encode

Rust's `apply_image_metadata` copies the source's RGB ICC profile and EXIF payload
into re-encoded output (PNG iCCP/eXIf, JPEG APP2/APP1). Go's stdlib encoders
cannot, so re-encoded prompt images lost color profile and orientation data.

- `utils/promptimage_metadata.go` ports the container handling: PNG chunk parsing
  (iCCP decompression via zlib, eXIf payload) and chunk insertion with correct
  CRCs after IHDR; JPEG segment parsing (APP1 `Exif\0\0` payload, APP2
  `ICC_PROFILE` chunks reassembled by sequence) and segment insertion after SOI
  with ICC chunking at 65519 bytes. The RGB-only filter mirrors Rust (profile
  bytes 16..20 must be `RGB `), so CMYK/other profiles are dropped.
- `utils/promptimage.go`: the re-encode path extracts the source metadata after
  decoding and applies it to the encoded bytes (JPEG quality-85 path and the PNG
  path). The byte-preservation path needs no work because the source metadata
  travels with the source bytes.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./utils/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./utils/ ./app/ ./tui/... -count=1` green, with new tests for
  PNG iCCP/eXIf round-trip through a forced resize (plus independent CRC
  verification and a decodability check), JPEG APP1/APP2 round-trip with
  decodability, multi-segment ICC reassembly, and the non-RGB drop rule.
- Residual (#44027): a resized WebP is still re-encoded as PNG because Go has no
  WebP encoder (Rust re-encodes WebP losslessly); Rust's content-digest image
  cache remains unported.

## TUI pass: composer text elements reach the turn input (#44027 mention residual)

Rust's composer textarea tracks text elements (byte ranges plus placeholders) and
sends them as `UserInput::Text { text, text_elements }`, so a mention reaches the
model as a structured slot. Go's composer is the bubbles textarea, which has no
element model, so `turn.TurnUserInput.TextElements` was never populated even
though the field exists.

- `tui/tea/composer_elements.go`: `ComposerTextElement` plus the range rules
  (`transformComposerElements`, rune-aligned diff of the value before/after an
  edit): edits away from an element shift it, edits touching it drop it, and any
  surviving range is re-validated against its placeholder so a stale range is
  never emitted. `registerComposerElement` records an element,
  `composerTextElementsForPromptValue` maps the tracked ranges onto the trimmed
  prompt that is submitted, and the model re-syncs the tracked ranges after every
  composer update (`syncComposerElements`).
- `tui/tea`: mention selection registers the mention text (excluding the
  separator space) as an element; `SubmitRequest.TextElements` carries the
  elements captured before the composer reset for submit, queue, and steer, and
  `cloneSubmitRequest` copies them.
- `app`: `turnTextElementsFromComposer` maps them onto `turn.TextElement`
  (byte range plus placeholder) for the interactive prompt text input, the
  steering text input, and the agents-overview first-turn prompt.
- Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
  clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for the range
  transform (shift/drop/placeholder-mismatch), the trimmed-prompt mapping, the
  end-to-end submit request carrying a registered element, and the dashboard
  prompt mapping (including invalid-range filtering).
- Residual (#44027 elements): Rust's textarea treats elements as atomic - the
  cursor cannot enter one and any edit or delete that touches it expands to the
  whole element. Go's bubbles textarea cannot express that without an
  element-aware editing model, so a partial edit inside a mention drops the
  element (and its payload) while Rust would have removed the whole mention;
  closing that needs a dedicated textarea port.

### #43921 residual closed (Unix): terminal OSC default-colors probe

Rust's palette falls back to an OSC 10/11 query when the startup probe has not
installed colors; Go's non-Windows path always reported the palette unavailable,
so the summary shimmer never animated there.

- `tui/terminal_default_colors_unix.go`: `probePlatformTerminalDefaultColors`
  duplicates stdin, switches the duplicate to non-blocking, writes the combined
  OSC 10/11 query to stdout, reads until the existing `ParseDefaultColors`
  recognizes a reply or the 100ms deadline expires, then restores and closes the
  handle. Every failure reports the palette unavailable so callers keep the dim
  fallback. Semantics mirror Rust's `terminal_probe::default_colors` (an
  exclusive bounded window before input polling; consumed bytes are not
  replayed).
- `tui/terminal_default_colors.go`: `ProbeTerminalDefaultColors(timeout)`
  installs the result through `SetDefaultColorsFromStartupProbe`, and the query
  bytes/timeout constants are pinned to Rust's `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT`.
  `tui/tea.Run` calls it before the input loop only when both stdio handles are
  real terminals (`probeTerminalColorsForTUI`), so tests and piped sessions are
  never touched.
- Verification: Windows `go build ./...`, `go vet ./tui/... ./app`, `gofmt -l`,
  and `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` all green; the Unix probe file
  compiles for `GOOS=linux` and `GOOS=darwin` (CGO disabled) via a standalone
  package copy; new tests pin the probe query/timeout and the startup gating.
- Residuals (#43921 palette): runtime behavior on a Unix terminal cannot be
  exercised in this environment (compile-validated only), and Windows keeps the
  console color table because Rust's Windows path prefers an OSC reply read
  through the console input queue (`ReadConsoleInputW`/`WriteConsoleInputW` with
  record replay), which Go does not port.

### #43921 residual closed (Windows): OSC palette preference with console replay

Rust's Windows palette prefers the terminal renderer's OSC 10/11 replies (Windows
Terminal and xterm.js can render a theme unrelated to the ConPTY console table)
and falls back to the console color table. Go only used the console table.

- `tui/terminal_color_responses.go` ports `windows_replay.rs`:
  `terminalColorResponseRanges` reports the byte ranges of complete, valid OSC
  10/11 replies (1024-byte bound, bracketed-paste payloads skipped, invalid rgb
  payloads rejected) and `terminalDefaultColorsFromResponses` requires both slots
  and keeps the first reply per slot.
- `tui/terminal_default_colors_windows.go`:
  `probePlatformTerminalDefaultColors` now writes the combined OSC query to the
  console output handle, waits on the console input handle with the shared
  deadline, reads `INPUT_RECORD`s, maps reply bytes back to their records, and
  replays every non-reply record with `WriteConsoleInputW` (modifiers, UTF-16
  input, mouse and focus events survive). When the console is unavailable or no
  valid pair arrives, the console color table remains the platform source. The
  STD_*_HANDLE constants are the unsigned DWORD forms.
- Verification: Windows `go build ./...`, `go vet ./tui/... ./app`, `gofmt -l`,
  and `go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` all green; new tests cover the response
  ranges (valid pair, bracketed paste, incomplete, invalid, oversized), the
  pair/first-reply rules, the console record byte extraction, and the replay
  omission filter. Runtime console behavior could not be exercised here because
  the harness stdio is piped (the probe then reports unavailable), matching the
  Rust fallback path.

## TUI pass: Esc-to-edit-prompt backtracking (pre-range `app_backtrack.rs`)

Rust's `tui/src/app_backtrack.rs` (902 lines) was the last sizeable unported TUI
surface: the first Esc primes prompt editing, the second opens the transcript
overlay with the newest user message highlighted, Esc/Left and Right step the
selection, and Enter forks *before* the selected turn and restores that prompt in
the composer. `tui/app/prompt_edit.go` already held the fork/selection core
(`ApplyPromptEdit`, `ApplyPromptEditWithStore`) but nothing called it, and only
the input-gating helpers in `tui/app/input.go` existed.

- `tui/app/backtrack.go` (new): the pure state machine and transcript projection
  (`BacktrackState` prime/reset, `StepBackwardBacktrack`/`StepForwardBacktrack`,
  `UserCount`/`HasBacktrackTarget`/`NthUserPosition`,
  `BacktrackSelectionForPrompt`/`BacktrackSelection`), mirroring Rust's
  `user_count`/`nth_user_position`/`has_backtrack_target`/`backtrack_selection`.
  Go's transcript message carries only text, so the projection degenerates to
  "every RoleUser message" (the session header is only the first message and a
  thread switch resets the transcript); local images/text elements have no
  message-model carrier.
- `tui/app/prompt_edit.go`: `promptEditBeforeTurnID` now resolves the ordinal
  against the same visible projection the transcript renders - review-mode
  prompts and display-empty inputs are skipped, a steered prompt is rejected
  ("the selected prompt is a steer and cannot be branched independently"), and a
  turn that is still in progress is rejected (Rust
  `backtrack_fork_before_turn_id`). The buffered-prompt fixture now completes its
  live turn before editing, as Rust requires.
- `tui/chatwidget/transcript_overlay.go`: `SetHighlightRange` /
  `ClearHighlightRange` / `HighlightRange` render the selected message's line
  range in reverse video (Rust `PagerView::set_highlight_cell` +
  `user_message_style().reversed()`), re-asserting the attribute after an inner
  SGR reset, and scroll the range into view.
- `tui/tea/backtrack.go` (new) + `tui/tea/model.go`: the Esc/Enter state machine
  (`shouldHandleBacktrackEsc`/`shouldRejectSideBacktrackEsc` reuse the existing
  `tui/app/input.go` gates; `normalBacktrackMode` mirrors Rust's
  `is_normal_backtrack_mode`), the overlay preview (Esc/Left/Right/Enter), the
  per-message line-range highlight (through the new
  `renderTranscriptMessagesWithRanges`), the "Esc again to edit previous
  message" composer footer, the "No previous message to edit." notice, the
  side-conversation rejection, and the `OnPromptEdit` model hook.
- `app/prompt_edit.go` (new): `interactiveRemotePromptEditHandler` and
  `interactivePromptEditHandler` wire `OnPromptEdit` to `ApplyPromptEdit` for the
  remote and embedded TUIs - read the source thread's turns, apply the prompt
  edit (fork before the selected turn, or a fresh thread for the first prompt),
  then attach the branched thread through the resume response so the transcript
  shows the pre-edit history and the composer restores the selected prompt.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./... -count=1` green (92 packages `ok`, no `FAIL`/`panic`), including
new tests for the state-machine math, the overlay highlight (reverse video, SGR
reset re-assertion, scrolling, invalid ranges), the tea Esc/preview/confirm flow
(fork, composer restore, failure restore, no-target notice, typing cancel,
side-conversation rejection), the review/steer/in-progress ordinal guards, and
the end-to-end remote prompt-edit round trip.

Residuals (later closed or dispositioned in the follow-up sections below): an
edited prompt restored text only, Rust's `has_older_history` fallback for the
first prompt is not modeled, and Rust's local-discovery/persisted-profile
interactions are unchanged.

## TUI pass: backtrack prompt restore keeps attachments and text elements

Backtracking a submitted prompt restored the rendered transcript entry, which
appends an "Attachments:" listing for image prompts, so editing an earlier prompt
put that listing into the composer instead of the image. Rust restores the
`UserHistoryCell` fields (`message`, `local_image_paths`, `remote_image_urls`,
`text_elements`).

- `tui/state.go`: `Message` gains `UserPrompt` / `UserPromptLocalImages` /
  `UserPromptRemoteImages` / `UserPromptTextElements` (plus the
  `MessageTextElement` byte-range type) and `State.AddUserPromptMessage` records
  them alongside the rendered entry; `AddMessage` leaves them empty so history
  messages and tests keep the text fallback.
- `tui/tea`: the interactive submit and the committed-steer paths record the
  submission's prompt, attachments, and text elements through
  `userPromptMessageState`; `restoreComposerPrompt` restores the prompt text,
  re-attaches the local/remote images, and re-registers the composer elements so
  a restored mention stays structured.
- `tui/app/backtrack.go`: `BacktrackSelectionForPrompt` resolves the prompt from
  the recorded user-prompt state (falling back to the rendered text for history
  messages) and maps the elements onto `turn.TextElement`.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./tui/... ./app -count=1` green, with new tests for the prompt state
recorded at submit time (a prompt with a local and remote image), the pure
extraction helper, and the end-to-end restore (composer text without the
attachment listing, re-attached images, and re-registered elements).

Resumed threads are covered too: `remoteTUIThreadMessagesFromThread` and
`interactiveSessionMessagesFromRecord` now split a replayed user message's
content parts into local images (`local_image` path) and remote images
(`input_image` URL), matching Rust's `user_message_display_from_inputs`, so
backtracking a prompt from before a resume restores its images. Rust's
`has_older_history` first-prompt fork fallback stays unmodeled, but Go loads the
full thread history (no pagination), so the server can never report older history
and starting a fresh thread for the first prompt is the correct Go behavior.

Full-suite note: `go test ./... -count=1` ran with one load-sensitive failure in
`appserver` (`TestRuntimeRouterTurnStartResizeNoticeEnabledEmitsDeveloperFragmentLikeRust`,
a 2-second `waitForRuntimeAgentRequest` budget with a 2048x2048 PNG data URL);
it passes in isolation and 3x, and the package is otherwise green, so it is a
pre-existing timing flake rather than a regression from this change.

## TUI gap inventory: missing slash commands (`/export`, `/recap`)

A comparison of the Rust `SlashCommand` enum (strum kebab-case names) against
Go's `tui/slash_command.go` found two user-visible commands with no Go
counterpart (neither is in the sync31 range; both are pre-range gaps like
`app_backtrack.rs`):

- `#export` (Rust `app/transcript_export.rs`) - **done this pass**.
- `#recap` (Rust `app/recap.rs`) - **open**. Manual `/recap` runs a temporary
  structured turn (`temporary_structured_request.rs`) with a JSON-schema
  response and shows `ThreadRecapHistoryCell` / `ThreadRecapLoadingCell`; an
  automatic recap is scheduled when an unfocused conversation is idle, with a
  TUI opt-out. It needs the temporary-structured-turn machinery plus the recap
  history cells, so it is recorded as the next TUI slice.

## TUI pass: `/export` complete Markdown conversation export

Rust's `/export` writes the complete conversation as Markdown. A bare `/export`
opens a destination picker ("Copy to clipboard" / "Save to file"); "Save to
file" prompts for a filename defaulting to `codex-session-<thread>.md`, and
`/export <path>` writes directly. Files are created exclusively so an existing
transcript is never clobbered, and `~`/relative paths resolve against the home
directory / session cwd.

- `tui/state.go` / `tui/slash_command.go`: new `CommandExport` ("export", "export
  the conversation as markdown") with `supports_inline_args`.
- `tui/chatwidget/transcript_export.go` (new): the destination picker view
  (`TranscriptExportPickerViewID`, "Export conversation" / "Save the complete
  conversation as Markdown").
- `tui/tea/export.go` (new): `/export` dispatch (args write directly, no args
  open the picker), the filename prompt, the clipboard/file delivery, and
  `writeTranscriptFile` (exclusive create, `~` expansion, cwd-relative). The new
  `TranscriptExportFunc` model hook renders the Markdown.
- `tui/tea/modal.go`: the picker's clipboard/file options route to the export
  flow.
- `tui/chatwidget/slash_dispatch.go`: `CommandExport` joins the inline-args and
  side-conversation allowlists and stays unavailable during a task, matching
  Rust's `supports_inline_args` / `available_in_side_conversation` /
  `available_during_task`.
- `app/transcript_export.go` (new): `interactiveRemoteTranscriptExportHandler`
  and `interactiveTranscriptExportHandler` read the thread's persisted turns
  (remote websocket / in-process router) and render Rust's section layout
  (`# Codex conversation` + `## User/Assistant/Plan/Reasoning` and indented
  `## Activity`), skipping hidden review prompts and the review markers.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for `/export
<path>` writing the file, the existing-file failure, the picker opening, the
clipboard option, the unavailable-callback failure, the Markdown section layout
including review-prompt exclusion, and the end-to-end remote export round trip.

Residuals: Go's export renders from persisted turns with role-based headings, so
Rust's exact `raw_lines`/`display_lines(0)` formatting per cell (raw agent
reasoning visibility, proposed-plan cell formatting) is approximated; and the
embedded local export reads the same turn projection as the remote path.

### Full-suite flake note (appserver, pre-existing)

`go test ./... -count=1` intermittently reports one `appserver` failure under
full-suite load, and it moves between tests
(`TestRuntimeRouterTurnStartResizeNoticeEnabledEmitsDeveloperFragmentLikeRust`,
then `TestRuntimeRouterMCPEventStreamStartStopForwardsAndScopesLikeRust`). Both
are async-delivery races: each asserts a notification is present immediately
after `router.Handle` returns, and the latter also fails under `-count=3` in
isolation while passing with `-count=1`. Neither is touched by the TUI changes in
this pass, and `go test ./appserver -count=1` is otherwise green; recorded as a
pre-existing test-synchronization issue rather than a regression.

## TUI pass: `/recap` conversation recap (manual + automatic)

Rust's `/recap` (`app/recap.rs` + `temporary_structured_request.rs`) summarizes
the current conversation on demand and, by default (`tui.auto_recap = true`), for
an unfocused conversation that has been idle for three minutes after at least
three completed turns. Go had neither the command nor the machinery.

- `tui/app/recap.go` (new): the pure half - the bounded recap-history projection
  (the last eight user turns within 900 bytes, reserving half the budget for the
  latest user request, UTF-8 safe), the prompt and JSON-schema construction, the
  response parsing (trim, cap at 320 chars), and the `RecapState`/`RecapProgress`
  scheduling state machine (focus-loss anchor, completed-turn and revision
  accounting, the three-completed-turn / two-turn-between-recaps / delay gates,
  marks, and the once-per-revision retry gate).
- `tui/history_cell/recap.go` (new): `ThreadRecapHistoryCell` (the labeled
  "Conversation recap" checkpoint with the indented wrapped body) and
  `ThreadRecapLoadingCell`, matching the Rust snapshots byte for byte.
- `tui/tea/recap.go` (new): the `/recap` flow (empty-history, in-progress, and
  failure notices; the transient loading row; automatic recaps without the
  loading row), the deadline `bubbletea.Tick` check, focus-gain cancellation, the
  one-retry-per-revision path, and the `RecapGeneratedMsg` rendering.
- `tui/state.go` / `tui/slash_command.go` / `chatwidget/slash_dispatch.go`:
  register the `recap` command with Rust's side-conversation and during-task
  gates.
- `app/recap.go` (new): `interactiveRemoteRecapGenerateHandler` runs the
  temporary structured turn over the app server - `config/read` (fail closed),
  an ephemeral `thread/start` with a read-only sandbox (or the active custom
  profile) and every feature/tool/MCP server disabled, `turn/start` with the
  recap schema, the assistant response collected from `item/completed` +
  `turn/completed`, and a best-effort `thread/unsubscribe`.
- `tui/tea/model.go` / `app/remote_tui.go`: `SessionResumeResponse` gains
  `CompletedTurns` (counted from the thread's completed turns) so a resumed
  thread seeds the automatic-recap accounting.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for the history
projection (chronology, eight-turn cap, oversized/UTF-8 budgets), the canonical
schema and parsing, the scheduling state machine (deadline/focus/turn gates,
recaps-per-turns, reset, restore, retry gating), the cell snapshots, the manual
`/recap` flow (loading row, recap cell, empty/in-progress/failure paths), the
automatic flow (deadline, focus-gain cancellation, one retry per revision), and
the end-to-end temporary structured turn against a fake app server (ephemeral +
read-only + disabled tools, the schema-constrained prompt, and the collected
response).

### `tui.auto_recap` config closed

The automatic recap now honors the local preference: `interactiveAutoRecap`
reads `tui.auto_recap` from the config values (default enabled, matching Rust's
`Tui::default`), `SettingsWriteResult` carries it, both TUI option builders pass
it as `Options.AutoRecap`, the settings-application path updates the model, and
the model disables the deadline check when it is false. Tests pin the config
resolution (default/true/false/invalid) and that a disabled setting neither
schedules nor runs a recap.

Residuals: the embedded local TUI has no app-server runtime agent to run a
temporary structured turn, so its `/recap` reports the failure message (the
remote TUI is fully wired); and Rust's temporary-thread `ThreadStartParams` sends
empty `runtime_workspace_roots` / `environments` / `dynamic_tools` /
`selected_capability_roots` plus a feature "system" thread source, which Go's
`omitempty` protocol structs cannot express.

## TUI pass: Daybreak eligibility and cyber refusal copy

Rust chooses the cyber refusal copy from the account's Daybreak access state
(`tui/src/daybreak.rs`) plus the current model, and renders it through
`history_cell::new_cyber_policy_error_event(notice)`. Go's cell hardcoded a
pre-Daybreak message and a plan-routed Trusted Access link, and had no
eligibility model.

- `tui/daybreak.go` (new): `DaybreakNotice` (Apply/Astra/Limited), the model
  mapping (`gpt-6-astra`/`gpt-6-astra-wm` -> Astra, `gpt-5.6-sol` -> the account
  notice, everything else -> Limited), and the `/accounts/verified_access`
  program mapping (the apply copy only when no cyber program is active and no
  grants exist; unknown programs are ignored).
- `tui/history_cell/notices.go`: `SafetyAccessBlockCell` now carries Rust's
  title/body/action list, and the bio and cyber copy (all three Daybreak
  variants, with Rust's exact Unicode punctuation and the Learn more / Apply for
  Daybreak links) replaces the plan-routed Trusted Access URL.
- `tui/chatwidget/turn_runtime.go`: `OnCyberPolicyError` resolves the notice
  through a new `DaybreakNoticeForModel` hook, defaulting to the neutral Limited
  copy.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for the
verified-access vectors (including the unknown-program and active/grant cases),
the model mapping, and the three refusal-copy variants.

Residual (next slice): Go's tea TUI has no production cyber-policy refusal
surface - `TurnCompletedMsg` carries only a plain error, so the tea error path
renders a generic turn error instead of the `SafetyAccessBlockCell`, and the
Daybreak eligibility read (app-server `getAuthStatus` + local auth +
`{chatgpt_base_url}/accounts/verified_access`, cached with a 3s timeout) is not
ported. The chatwidget turn-runtime path that calls the cell has no non-test
caller today, so the copy is now Rust-faithful but not yet reachable from the
primary TUI.

### Cyber refusal surface wired into the primary TUI

The Daybreak-aware copy is now reachable: the server classifies a cybersecurity
policy refusal (`codexErrorInfo: "cyberPolicy"` on the turn error / a
`*codexapi.APIError` with kind `cyberPolicy` locally), and both TUI turn paths
surface it as a refusal instead of a generic error.

- `app/daybreak.go` (new): `turnErrorIsCyberPolicy` reads the kind out of the
  wire `codexErrorInfo` (bare string or tagged object, separator-insensitive) and
  `cyberPolicyError` classifies the local `*codexapi.APIError`.
- `app/remote_tui.go` / `app/interactive.go`: a cyber-policy turn error now sends
  `codextea.CyberPolicyErrorMsg` for the active thread instead of the generic
  error event / turn error.
- `tui/tea/daybreak.go` (new): `CyberPolicyErrorMsg` renders
  `historycell.NewCyberPolicyErrorEvent(notice)` for the active thread, resolving
  the notice through the new `OnDaybreakNotice(model)` hook (nil -> the neutral
  Limited copy, which is Rust's pending/failed default).

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for the wire
and local error classification, the remote notification path sending the refusal
message, the apply/limited refusal cells, and the background-thread guard.

### Cyber refusal surface: account-scoped Daybreak eligibility read

The Apply/Astra copies now come from the account's verified-access state, not
just the neutral default.

- `app/daybreak.go`: `daybreakNoticeCache` (session cache whose pending/failed
  state is the neutral Limited copy), `prefetchDaybreakNotice` (one bounded
  background read), `loadDaybreakNotice` (app-server `getAuthStatus` with
  `include_token` and no refresh, cross-checked against the local ChatGPT
  `auth.Store.Load()` token, then the authenticated `verified_access` GET, with a
  second credential read to discard a rotated result), `fetchDaybreakNotice`
  (`Authorization: Bearer`, `ChatGPT-Account-ID`, 3s timeout, fail-closed), and
  `daybreakNoticeForModel` (non-`openai` providers never read Daybreak).
- `app/remote_tui.go`: a local `openai` session prefetches the notice at startup
  and resolves `OnDaybreakNotice` through the cache, so a cyber refusal shows the
  apply/astra/limited copy that matches the account.

Verification: `go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests
for the authenticated request shape, the fail-closed paths (non-2xx, missing base
URL, mismatched server token, non-ChatGPT local auth), the program mapping, and
the provider/cache gating.

Residual: the read is prefetched once per TUI session (matching Rust's
`OnceCell`), and Rust's additional prefetch triggers (reconnect, app-server turn
events) are not re-issued because the cache never invalidates within a session.
Background-thread refusals still render the generic thread-scoped error rather
than the refusal cell.

## Remaining TUI surface inventory (evidence-based, after /recap + /export)

With the slash-command table now matching Rust's (68 names plus Go-only
compatibility commands) and `/recap`/`/export`/Daybreak landed, the remaining
unported TUI surfaces found by comparing the Rust `tui/src` module list are:

- `footer_hint.rs` (`wrap_hint_rows`) - no Go counterpart, and its two Rust
  consumers differ: Go's agents-overview footer renders a single
  `footerSpans()` line (`tui/agents_overview/render.go`) where Rust packs hints
  into rows with a width-aware separator and word-wraps oversized rows, and Go's
  MCP elicitation form (`tui/bottom_pane/mcp_server_elicitation.go`
  `RenderLines`) renders no footer tips at all where Rust shows
  submit/navigate/validation/`esc to cancel` tips. Porting `WrapHintRows` and
  both consumers is the next bounded footer slice.
- `resume_picker_transcript_preview.rs` - Go's resume picker advertises
  "ctrl+t preview" in its footer but the key has no implementation, and
  `SessionSummary.Preview` is only a short text preview. Rust lazily loads the
  newest six speaker-tagged transcript lines (paginated `thread/items/list` pages
  or a bounded legacy rollout tail scan) and toggles that pane.
- `startup_draft.rs` - an editable, non-submitting composer while startup work
  continues; Go's startup gating has not been compared against it yet.
- `dynamic_tools.rs` (70 KB) - TUI-hosted task tools for a TUI connected to an
  *external* app server; Go's TUI is client-only, so this is likely
  architecture-N/A but needs a recorded confirmation.
- `named_session_lookup.rs` / `session_queue_commands.rs` - resolve a displayed
  session label through the app server and queue input via `thread/queue/add`;
  Go has some `thread/queue` support and needs verification against these.

### Footer hints: `wrap_hint_rows` ported; agents-overview footer wraps

- `tui/footerhint` (new leaf package): `WrapHintRows` is a faithful port of Rust
  `footer_hint::wrap_hint_rows` (width clamped to at least one column, hints
  measured by a caller-supplied display width, an oversized hint kept on its own
  row, and an empty input producing one empty row). It lives in its own package
  because the agents-overview renderer must not import the root `tui` package
  (root `tui` tests import the renderer, so a direct import is a test cycle).
- `tui/agents_overview/render.go`: `footerSpans()` became `footerHints()` (each
  hint is its own span group) plus `footerHintRows(width)`, which chooses the
  separator from whether every hint fits on one line (`"  "` else `" "`), packs
  the hints with `WrapHintRows`, and hard-splits a single oversized hint at
  display-width boundaries. The render loop now emits every footer row and the
  body height subtracts the actual footer row count, so a narrow terminal shows
  additional footer rows instead of clipping hints mid-label (the previous
  single line was truncated at 120 columns in the existing tests).

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for the two
Rust `footer_hint_tests.rs` vectors and a narrow-terminal test asserting every
hint label survives on the wrapped footer rows.

Disposition: the other Rust consumer, the MCP elicitation footer tips
(`mcp_server_elicitation.rs` `footer_tips` / `footer_tip_lines`), is
architecture-different rather than unported: Go's elicitation form
(`tui/bottom_pane/mcp_server_elicitation.go`) has no per-field focus or
select-vs-text state - its fields are rendered as modal options with the generic
"Enter choose | Esc cancel" footer - so Rust's submit-all / `ctrl + p` / `ctrl +
n` field-navigation tips and its validation-error tip have no Go counterpart to
render into. Recorded for a possible elicitation-modal redesign rather than
forced into the modal footer.

## TUI pass: resume-picker transcript preview

The resume picker advertised `ctrl+t transcript` / `ctrl+e expand` but Go's
expanded rows showed only the session metadata and no conversation preview, and
nothing loaded one.

- `tui/resume_picker_preview.go` (new): `TranscriptPreviewLine` /
  `TranscriptPreviewSpeaker`, the `TranscriptPreviewState`
  loading/failed/loaded enum, the picker's per-session cache accessors
  (`TranscriptPreview`, `SetTranscriptPreview`, `BeginTranscriptPreview`), and
  `renderTranscriptPreviewLines`, which mirrors Rust's preview framing - the
  loading and failure rows, the "No transcript preview available" empty case, and
  the `  │ ` / `  └ ` framed, width-4-wrapped content lines.
- `tui/resume_picker.go`: `SessionPickerState` gains the preview cache; expanded
  rows (both densities) append the preview section, matching Rust's dense and
  comfortable rendering (the dense path previously ignored expansion entirely).
- `app/transcript_preview.go` (new): the loader. `thread/items/list` is preferred
  (newest-first, first page of 6 then 100-item pages, bounded by a 400-item scan
  budget, cursors deduped), falling back to the thread's loaded turns; items map
  to user/assistant lines with the assistant display rewrite (hidden-markup
  stripping plus inline visualizations), the newest nonblank lines are taken per
  message, and the result is reversed to chronological order. The embedded TUI
  uses the same turns mapping through the in-process router.
- `tui/tea`: `TranscriptPreviewFunc` / `TranscriptPreviewMsg` and the
  `OnLoadTranscriptPreview` hook; `ctrl+e` marks a newly expanded row as loading
  and requests its preview, and the result is recorded in the picker cache.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for the preview
rendering states (loading/loaded/empty/failed and dense expanded), the ctrl+e
load/record/no-re-request flow including the failure path, the turns mapping
(chronology, six-line bound, per-message line order, non-message skipping), and
the end-to-end paginated `thread/items/list` request shape.

### Resume-picker `ctrl+t` transcript overlay

The picker's other advertised key now works: `ctrl+t` opens the selected
session's full transcript in the pager overlay.

- `tui/tea/session_transcript.go` (new): `SessionTranscriptFunc` /
  `SessionTranscriptMsg` and the `OnReadSessionTranscript` hook;
  `openSelectedSessionTranscript` opens the overlay with a "Loading transcript..."
  frame (alt-screen pager mode) and starts the load, and the result renders into
  the overlay through the shared transcript renderer.
- `tui/tea/modal.go`: the picker's `ctrl+t` requests the overlay for the
  selected row (a session without a thread id reports "No transcript available
  for this session" instead).
- `app/transcript_preview.go`: the remote handler reads the thread and maps it
  with `remoteTUIThreadMessagesFromThread`; the embedded handler reads the local
  record and maps it with `interactiveSessionMessagesFromRecord`.
- Regression fix in this pass: `updateTranscriptOverlayKey` scoped the backtrack
  Esc handling to the *transcript* overlay. It previously ran for every pager,
  so Esc in the /diff or session-transcript overlay could close it and add a
  spurious "No previous message to edit." notice.

Verification: `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean; `go test
./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for the ctrl+t overlay
open/load/render flow, the no-thread notice, and the non-transcript-pager Esc
guard.

The expanded-row details now match Rust's layout too: `renderExpandedSessionRows`
renders the fixed-width `Session:` / `Created:` / `Updated:` (long relative time
plus timestamp) / `Directory:` (home-relative `~`) / `Branch:` (with the branch
icon) block followed by the `Conversation:` header the preview lines hang off,
replacing the simplified Thread/Path/Directory lines. Tests pin the long
relative-time form, the label layout, and the directory display.

Residual: the picker's transcript overlay does not implement Rust's in-picker
loading frame/cancellation bookkeeping (Go opens the shared pager overlay
immediately and ignores a late load after the overlay changes).

## Non-TUI core: unique session-label lookup (#43315)

Rust's #43315 ("resolve session labels uniquely before acting on them") made the
session commands reject ambiguous labels instead of targeting the most recent
duplicate, match the fallback preview text when no name is set, revalidate listed
candidates with `thread/read`, and require a UUID when pagination prevents
verifying uniqueness. Go still had the pre-#43315 behavior in every command path.

- `app/named_session_lookup.go` (new): ports Rust `named_session_lookup::lookup` -
  the displayed label (trimmed name, else preview), exhaustive local matching
  across the requested collections without a server `search_term`, the
  `Multiple sessions match '...'` ambiguity error, the
  `Cannot verify a unique session label across server pages; ...` pagination
  error, and `thread/read` revalidation (skipping renamed candidates and keeping
  the listed entry for older servers that cannot read unloaded threads). It also
  hosts the local-store equivalents (`localSessionLabel`,
  `uniqueSessionRecordByName`).
- `app/session.go`: the remote resume/archive/unarchive lookups now use the
  shared resolver (delete resolves active+archived in one lookup so a duplicate
  across collections is ambiguous), the remote queue lookup uses it too, and the
  local lookups (`sessionIDByName`, `sessionIDByUniqueActiveName`) now match the
  title-or-preview label and reject distinct matches.
- `parity/contracts/manifest.json`: the `queue-by-name-*` contract is renamed to
  `queue-by-name-unique-session`, gains `tui/src/named_session_lookup.rs` as a
  Rust source, and its verifiers are updated to the new tests.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l` clean;
`go test ./... -count=1` green (no `FAIL`/`panic`), with new tests for the remote
lookup (preview matching, distinct-match rejection with Rust's message, the
pagination UUID requirement, renamed-candidate skipping, and the older-server
unloaded-thread fallback) plus updated local fixtures that now assert the
ambiguity rejection.

The local archive/unarchive/delete resolver now enumerates every match too:
`sessionIDsBySQLiteName` returns all valid state-database matches (deduped) and
`sessionIDByNameWithArchiveFilter` merges them with the rollout candidates and
records before rejecting duplicates, so a duplicate label in any of those
sources is reported as ambiguous.

Residual: Rust's TUI-side display of an ambiguous resume error (shown in the
running TUI without replacing the current conversation) is not compared against
Go's resume error surface.

## TUI surface triage: `startup_draft.rs` (N/A) and `dynamic_tools.rs` (open)

Closing out the remaining two inventory items with evidence:

- `startup_draft.rs` (20 KB) - **N/A (Go startup architecture)**. Rust launches a
  provisional composer *before* the selected session exists (the draft screen
  buffers typing and forwards it once startup finishes), because its startup
  orchestration resolves the resume/fork/new selection behind the running TUI.
  Go resolves the CLI selection, creates the session, and builds the runner,
  brokers, and services *before* constructing the tea model and starting the
  program (`app/interactive.go` / `app/remote_tui.go`), so there is no window in
  which a provisional composer would exist; the composer is interactive as soon
  as it appears, and MCP startup messages stream into the transcript while it
  stays editable. Porting the module would require restructuring Go's startup to
  start the TUI before session creation.
- `dynamic_tools.rs` (70 KB) - **open, and the largest remaining TUI feature**.
  Rust's TUI registers a task-management dynamic-tool namespace with an external
  app server and serves the server's `item/tool/call` requests for it:
  `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`,
  `send_message_to_thread`, `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `set_thread_title`,
  `set_thread_archived` (with bounded limits, a "maximum 1,000 UTF-8 bytes"
  prompt, and untrusted-data guidance). Go registers no dynamic tools and its
  server-request dispatcher returns `-32000 "Dynamic tool calls are not
  available in TUI yet."` (`app/remote_tui.go` `ServerRequestDynamicToolCall`),
  and none of the nine tool names appear anywhere in the tree. A faithful port
  needs the namespace specs (registered through `ThreadStartParams.DynamicTools`
  for the external-app-server case), the call handler with the bounded
  list/read/wait semantics, and the thread operations each tool performs.

### Implemented: `codex_tui` task-management dynamic tools

The namespace is now registered and served, replacing the "not available in TUI
yet" error.

- `app/dynamic_tools_specs.go` (new): the namespace spec with Rust's nine tools,
  descriptions, input schemas (limit/turnLimit/output bounds, the "Maximum 1,000
  UTF-8 bytes" prompt, the wait target shape), and `deferLoading`; the
  non-delegation filter (Rust `DELEGATION_TOOLS`); and the raw serializer for
  `ThreadStartParams.DynamicTools` (validated with `turn.ValidateDynamicTools`).
- `app/dynamic_tools.go` (new): `ExecuteDynamicTool` and the executor -
  `deny_unknown_fields` argument parsing via `DisallowUnknownFields`, the nine
  tools (`list_threads`/`list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `create_thread`,
  `send_message_to_thread`, `fork_thread`, `set_thread_title`,
  `set_thread_archived`, `wait_threads`), the delegation wrapper and tool-output
  turns (`TurnToolOutput{name, namespace, output}`), the history-pagination
  fallback for `thread/turns/list`, the create/fork config inheritance from
  `thread/resume`, and the bounded wait loop.
- `app/dynamic_tools_helpers.go` (new): Rust's response shaping (the
  `success_response` shrink loop and `truncate_response`'s skip-key rules),
  thread/turn/item summaries, the delegation prompt wrap/parse helpers, the
  failure-response text budget, and the app-server access helpers.
- `app/remote_tui.go`: the TUI's thread starts carry the namespace, and
  `ServerRequestDynamicToolCall` is served by `ExecuteDynamicTool`; started
  background tasks notify the TUI.
- `tui/tea/dynamic_tools.go` (new): `DynamicToolThreadStartedMsg` tracks
  dispatched tasks and refreshes an open dashboard.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui ./tui/tea ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for the
spec shape/filter/validation, the list/read/title/archive/send/wait tool results
and their request params, the unknown-argument and unknown-tool failures, the
response truncation budget, the thread-start registration, the `item/tool/call`
dispatch wiring, and the tea dispatch tracking. A full `go test ./... -count=1`
is green apart from two pre-existing load-sensitive flakes (`appserver` MCP
event-stream forwarding and `tool` unified-exec exec-server recovery, both
passing in isolation).

The transport was confirmed live rather than assumed: `ThreadStartParams.DynamicTools`
is persisted to `record.Metadata.DynamicTools` (`appserver/router.go`), the
runtime rebuilds the specs (`dynamicToolsFromRecordMetadata`) and registers a
model-visible handler per tool (`turn.RegisterDynamicToolHandlers`, with
`DeferLoading` respected), and `item/tool/call` is routed back to the registering
connection through `dynamicToolServerRequestCaller` (`appserver/runtime_router.go`),
which is exactly the request the TUI now serves.

Residuals: Go uses the app-server dynamic-tools callback transport rather than
Rust's local streamable-HTTP MCP server (`dynamic_tools_mcp.rs`), which is the
faithful transport for a daemon-connected TUI; forks and side conversations do
not carry the namespace (Rust threads the `codex_tui` MCP config through
`ThreadForkParams.config`, and Go's fork params have no dynamic-tools field); the
dashboard does not mark a dispatched task as "working" before its next list
refresh; `wait_threads` polls instead of consuming a status broadcast (same
observable payload); and the item summaries cover the common `ThreadItem`
variants with a type/id/name/status fallback for the rest.

## Non-TUI/TUI core: backend banner payload and CTA mapping

Another previously uninventoried surface: Rust's `backend_banners.rs` +
`backend_banners/actions.rs` parse the backend-owned banner (supplied by the
account usage read) and map its CTAs to existing CLI actions.

- `app/backend_banners.go` (new): `BackendBanner` with Rust's bounded parse
  (required `banner_type`/`title`/`description`/`ctas`, an explicit
  `inline`/`dismissible` presentation, title/description byte and line limits,
  <=8 CTAs, validated blocked/fallback model slugs), `ResolveBannerAction`
  (`notify_owner`/`contact_owner` -> the add-credits owner nudge,
  `request_increase` -> a validated `http(s)` request URL or the usage-limit
  nudge, `reset_usage` -> the reset flow, the plan-aware credits/pricing
  destinations, and the `/admin/` account-id rule), `ActionableActions` (label
  and action filtering), `Dismissible`, and `BannerCopy` (control-character
  stripping plus the `{time}` reset substitution through
  `status.FormatResetTimestamp`).

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui ./tui/tea ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new tests for
Rust's two `backend_banners_tests.rs` vectors (the eight-action limit and the
unsupported/unrenderable field set), the CTA resolution vectors (owner nudges,
request URLs, plan-aware credits and pricing, unknown actions), the unsafe
destination rejections (`javascript:`/credentials/no host), the admin
account-id rule, and the reset-time copy substitution.

Residuals: the banner is not yet sourced from Go's account usage read
(`app/rate_limits.go` ignores any banner field), the inline banner UI
(`bottom_pane::ActionableBanner` with `BannerDismissal`) is unported, and the
backend-authorized fallback / task-local Reserve application
(`app/backend_banner_fallback.rs`) is not wired, so the parsed banner is not yet
rendered or acted on at runtime.

## TUI core: assistant directive grammar and file-citation rewiring

Another uninventoried surface: Rust's `assistant_directives.rs` is the shared
grammar for annotations embedded in assistant Markdown
(`::git-create-pr{cwd="/repo" isDraft=true}`, `:::code-comment{body="Keep \"x}\"
literal."}`), with two quote-escaping modes and a caller-shareable byte-scan
budget. Go had no generic parser: `tui/markdown/file_citations.go` carried its
own ~120-line approximation.

- `tui/assistant_directives.go` (new): `AssistantDirective`,
  `QuoteEscaping` (`Literal`/`Backslash`), and
  `ParseAssistantDirective[WithBudget]`, porting the marker (one-to-three
  colons), the name rule (ASCII-alphabetic first byte, `{` required), the
  attribute loop (duplicate keys rejected before a long value is scanned),
  quoted values with the mode-dependent escaped-delimiter rule and
  single-quote support, unquoted values ending at whitespace/`}`, the exact
  `raw` boundary (the directive source without trailing Markdown), and the
  shared scan budget charged for inspected source.
- `tui/markdown/file_citations.go`: `parseFileCitationDirective` now delegates to
  the shared grammar (citations try `Literal` then `Backslash`, other directives
  the reverse, matching Rust's scanner order with one budget), deleting the
  bespoke parser and its helpers.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui ./tui/markdown ./app` clean;
`gofmt -l` clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green, with new
tests for Rust's five `assistant_directives_tests.rs` cases (unquoted git
receipts, triple-colon escaped quotes and embedded braces, single-quoted values,
the six ambiguous/incomplete rejections, the malformed-retry budget with an
attempt bound, and the inspected-values-only budget) plus a file-citation test
pinning the shared grammar (duplicate-key rejection and the closing-brace `raw`
boundary) on top of the retained literal/backslash/unquoted citation vectors.

## Non-TUI core: `codex queue` unsupported-server detection and UUIDv7 (#39385)

Verifying Go's queue command against Rust's `session_queue_commands.rs` (the
remaining open item in the "Remaining TUI surface inventory") found two
unported behaviors:

- `is_unsupported_queue_error` was a loose text match
  (`"method not found"` / `"-32601"`). Go now ports Rust's exact matcher: a
  typed `*remoteRPCError` is unsupported only for `-32601`, or `-32600` with the
  experimental gate message (`thread/queue/add requires experimentalApi
  capability`) or the older-server deserialization failure
  (`Invalid request: unknown variant \`thread/queue/add\``). An ordinary `-32600`
  failure such as a queue bound violation is no longer eligible to be reported
  as an unsupported server. The embedded-router text fallback stays because
  `localSessionRouterRequest` flattens JSON-RPC errors to text.
- The queued submission's `client_user_message_id` now uses `Uuid::now_v7()`
  semantics (`newQueueClientMessageID`, `uuid.NewV7()` with a v4 fallback) for
  both the local and remote request paths; Go previously sent a UUIDv4.

Tests: `TestQueueUnsupportedServerErrorRecognizesOnlyUnsupportedLikeRust` ports
Rust's `session_queue_commands_tests.rs` table (method-not-found, both `-32600`
forms, the queue-bound non-match, a transport failure, plus `nil` and an
unlisted code) and asserts the wrap/non-wrap decision;
`TestNewQueueClientMessageIDIsUUIDv7LikeRust` pins the version nibble. The
`queue-cli-command` parity contract lists both new verifiers.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `gofmt -l` clean; `go test ./app -count=1` and
`go test ./parity -count=1` (contract verifier existence + inventory) green.

Residual (documented, not deferred silently): Rust's queue command also
*reuses a running local daemon* - `start_app_server_for_session_command` probes
the default control socket when the launch overrides are replayable
(`can_reuse_implicit_local_daemon`), routes through it
(`AppServerTarget::LocalDaemon`), and only then applies the embedded+daemon
conflict guard and the "local app-server daemon" wrap branch. Go's session
commands (queue/archive/delete/unarchive/resume/fork) are explicit-remote or
embedded only - `resolveSessionRemoteEndpoint` never probes the control socket -
so Go has no `implicit_local_daemon` case. Porting it also requires the
legacy-daemon method-probe fallbacks already recorded for `/cd` (#43279); it is
a dedicated session-command architecture pass rather than a queue-local edit.

## TUI: archived-session unarchive prompt (`unarchive_prompt.rs` + `session_start.rs`)

A Rust `tui/src` module-list comparison found another unported surface that no
earlier plan section covered: Rust's startup recovery when the selected
conversation is archived. `session_start::complete_session_start` treats the
server's archived guidance specially - instead of failing the resume/fork it
draws `unarchive_prompt::UnarchivePrompt` ("This conversation is archived",
`Unarchive and resume` / `Cancel`, `Press enter to continue or esc to cancel`),
and on confirmation unarchives the thread and retries the start by id.

- `tui/tea/unarchive_prompt.go` (new): `unarchivePromptState` ports
  `UnarchivePrompt` - the render (archive notice, thread id, the two numbered
  selection rows, the key hint), `handle_key` (ctrl+c/ctrl+d quit; up/down/k/j
  toggle the highlight; 1/y/Y confirm; esc/2/n/N cancel; enter accepts the
  highlight), `archivedSessionGuidance` (Rust's guidance extraction that drops
  any trailing JSON-RPC code), and `archivedSessionGuidanceForThread` (the
  requested-thread prefix guard, so an unrelated startup failure can never
  unarchive a session). `openUnarchivePrompt` turns the existing picker modal
  into the prompt in place (keeping the modal pointer so the picker's terminal
  mode is undisturbed) and `updateUnarchivePromptModal` runs the unarchive
  action then retries the original resume/fork.
- `tui/tea/session_picker.go` + `tui/tea/modal.go`: the resume and fork
  selection paths detect the archived guidance for the selected thread and open
  the prompt instead of reporting "Resume failed"; the modal routes keys/render
  to the prompt. Confirmed cancel matches Rust's outcome per target - the
  embedded TUI exits, the remote TUI opens the agents overview (Rust's command
  center) - and ctrl+c/ctrl+d quit.
- `app/interactive.go`: the embedded resume handler now rejects an archived
  record with the same guidance the app server returns
  (`archivedSessionStartError`), and the embedded fork action refuses an
  archived source (`store.Fork` previously forked it), matching
  `appserver.threadResumeArchivedError` / the thread-fork gate. The remote
  handlers already surfaced the server's guidance.

Tests mirror Rust's vectors: `TestArchivedSessionGuidanceLikeRust` (the
`session_start_error_surfaces_archived_guidance_without_rollout_path` case plus
unrelated/different-thread non-matches), `TestUnarchivePromptRenderLikeRust`
(the resume and fork prompt snapshots), `TestUnarchivePromptKeysLikeRust`
(`confirmation_requires_an_accept_key`),
`TestModelArchivedResumePromptsToUnarchiveAndRetriesLikeRust` (prompt ->
unarchive -> retried resume applies the restored thread), and
`TestInteractiveResumeAndForkRejectArchivedSessionsLikeRust` (embedded
resume/fork guidance and the unarchive-then-resume unblocking). A new
`unarchive-prompt` parity contract lists all five verifiers.

Verification: `go build ./...`; `go vet ./tui/... ./app` clean; `gofmt -l`
clean; `go test ./tui/... ./app ./parity -count=1` green.

Residuals: Rust's prompt also drives the startup-draft/provisional-composer
path (Go resolves the selection before the TUI exists, the recorded
`startup_draft.rs` N/A) and offers "Quit" as an explicit third highlight; Go
models Quit as ctrl+c/ctrl+d only, which matches Rust's key handling. The remote
cancel path opens the agents overview rather than Rust's daemon command center.

## TUI module-list gap scan (this pass): `task_mentions.rs` and `experimental_features.rs`

After closing `unarchive_prompt.rs`/`session_start.rs`, a fresh comparison of the
Rust `tui/src` top-level module list against Go's `tui/` files (plus a symbol
grep per module) found two more Rust modules with no Go counterpart. The other
previously-unmentioned modules are ported under Go names without a plan mention
(`clipboard_html.rs` -> `tui/markdown/clipboard_html.go`,
`theme_picker.rs` -> `tui/theme_picker.go`, `color.rs` -> `tui/color.go`, ...),
so they are not gaps.

- `tui/src/task_mentions.rs` (365 lines) - **core ported this pass; the search
  and popup wiring remains**. Rust's same-host task discovery and bounded
  task-reference prompt context. Landed now:
  - `tui/task_mentions.go` (new): `TaskMention`, `MaxReferencedTasks` (16),
    `MaxTaskTitleChars` (160), `MaxReferencedThreadIDBytes` (768),
    `TaskMentionRequestHeading`, `ValidThreadPath` (`thread://` + <=64
    alphanumeric/_/-), `ParseTaskLink` (byte/char-boundary exact, escaped titles,
    MAX+1 iteration bound, `end = start + index + len(path) + 5`), and
    `FormatTaskLink` (backslash then `](` then `]` escaping), plus the
    UTF-8-boundary helpers that mirror Rust `str::get` returning `None`.
  - `tui/bottom_pane/task_mentions.go` (new): `ApplyTaskReferences` (typed
    dedupe, self-thread skip, 16-task and 768-byte bounds, the
    `## Referenced chats with Codex:` JSON block before the first uncovered
    request heading, binding consumption semantics, task-link re-encoding, and
    text-element byte-range maintenance) and `DecodeTaskLinks`.
  - `tui/mention_codec.go`: `EncodeHistoryMentions` now delegates to the new
    `EncodeHistoryMentionsAtElements` (element-span task-link encoding with
    Rust's `valid_thread_path` guard that keeps thread:// paths out of the
    scanner path), and `DecodeHistoryMentionsWithAtMentions` now recognizes task
    links and populates the new `DecodedHistoryText.TaskMentionRanges`.
  - Tests mirror Rust `task_mentions_tests.rs`:
    `TestTaskMentionHistoryRoundTripsLikeRust`,
    `TestTaskReferenceContextMergesWithIDEContextLikeRust`,
    `TestTaskReferenceHeadingInsideTitleLikeRust` (all green), and the new
    `task-mentions` parity contract lists them.
  - Wired this pass (continuing the same item):
    - `mentions_v2` gains `MentionTypeTask` (label "Task", tag-padded), the
      task ordering rank (Plugin < Skill < Task < File/Dir) with Rust's
      task-task Equal short-circuit so provider order survives, and
      `BuildSearchCatalogWithTasks`/`TaskCandidate` (whitespace-normalized title
      clamped to MAX_TASK_TITLE_CHARS, cwd description, title/cwd/snippet search
      terms, `@title` -> `thread://id` selection). Tasks stay visible only in
      Results mode, matching Rust's `SearchMode::Tools` acceptance.
    - `app/task_mentions.go` (new): `searchTaskMentions` mirrors
      `spawn_search`'s discovery half - the exact thread/search (limit 50,
      recency desc, archived false) and thread/list (limit 50, updated desc,
      archived false, state-db only) params, the search-first merge deduped by
      thread id (list-only threads contribute an empty snippet), the
      self/ephemeral/ambient-suggestions filters, the name -> preview -> id title
      fallback, the 50-result bound, and the stable current-cwd-first ordering
      (byte-boundary `get`, descendant separator rule, Windows
      case-insensitive prefix).
    - `tui/tea`: `OnSearchTasks` + `TaskMentionSearchResultMsg` with the same
      generation/query guard as the file search; an empty query clears the task
      rows without a request. The remote TUI provides the hook
      (`interactiveRemoteTaskMentionSearch`), which is also the enablement gate
      (Rust `set_task_mentions_enabled(task_tools_available)`, since only the
      remote TUI registers the `codex_tui` namespace).
    - Submit-time application: `applySubmitTaskReferences` decodes the composer's
      `<sigil><mention>|<path>` bindings and runs `ApplyTaskReferences` at the
      four turn-input builders that assemble the text item (embedded turn +
      embedded steer, remote turn/start + remote turn/steer), after the IDE
      context is applied - mirroring Rust's single chatwidget call site.
  - Task-tool gating landed this pass: `TaskToolsAvailableMsg` records the
    threads whose app server accepted the `codex_tui` namespace
    (Rust `AppServerSession::task_tools_available`), the mention popup only issues
    the task search for those threads (`chat_widget.set_task_mentions_enabled`),
    and a fork inherits the parent's capability
    (`AppServerSession::fork_thread_with_permission_mode`). The capability is
    reported by `remoteAppServerTUIClient.startThread`, which now also mirrors
    Rust's dynamic-tools downgrade: a server that rejects the namespace
    (`-32600`/`-32602` naming `dynamicTools`/`dynamic tool`/`namespace`/
    `inputSchema`) starts the thread without it and reports task tools
    unavailable, instead of failing the bootstrap. Tests:
    `TestModelTaskMentionsRequireTaskToolsLikeRust`,
    `TestRemoteStartThreadRetriesWithoutDynamicToolsLikeRust`.
  - The dashboard task prompt intentionally does not run `ApplyTaskReferences`:
    Rust's only call site is the chatwidget submit path, and
    `submit_agents_overview_prompt` appends the plain text + elements.
  - The 100 ms search debounce landed too: the search command sleeps Rust's
    `SEARCH_DEBOUNCE` and drops a superseded request before it reaches the app
    server, using a shared `atomic.Uint64` generation so the check is race-free
    (Rust's `AtomicU64`); test `TestModelTaskMentionSearchDebouncesLikeRust`.
  - Remaining: the MCP-transport variant of the task-tool namespace
    (`dynamic_tools_mcp.rs`). Four items from this list landed as well:
    `DecodeTaskLinks` now has a production caller - `DecodeTaskLinks` moved to
    `tui/task_mentions.go` and `chatwidget.UserMessageDisplayFromInputs` decodes
    stored task links back into `@title` for display, mirroring Rust
    `chatwidget::user_message_display` (`decode_task_links`) - and the persisted
    capability directory is
    now ported (`app/task_tools_capability.go`, Rust
    `tui-thread-reference-capabilities`): a successful remote thread start writes
    the per-thread marker and the TUI's gate consults it for threads this process
    has not seen (`OnTaskToolsAvailable`), with positive capability only - a
    downgraded start does not forget an earlier one, matching Rust's
    set-plus-marker semantics. Tests: `TestTaskToolCapabilityMarkersLikeRust`,
    `TestModelTaskMentionsUsePersistedCapabilityLikeRust`,
    `TestTaskMentionCodecMatchesRust`,
    `TestUserMessageDisplayFromInputsDecodesTaskLinksLikeRust`. Fork-side
    persistence landed too: `inheritRemoteTaskToolCapability` writes the child
    marker when the parent has one, called from the remote fork handler
    (Rust `AppServerSession::fork_thread_with_permission_mode`), so a later
    process resuming the fork still offers task references.

## Non-TUI core: `config/batchWrite` mutation and reload semantics

While wiring the `/experimental` write path, the app-server-side half of Rust's
config write was found unported: `reload_user_config` was a dead field in Go, and
a config write never refreshed the derived runtime state.

- `appserver/config_mutation.go` (new): `configEditsAreSessionDefaultsOnly`
  mirrors Rust's `session_defaults_only` check (only `model`,
  `model_reasoning_effort`, `plan_mode_reasoning_effort`, `service_tier`,
  `personality`). `applyConfigBatchWriteMutation` ports Rust's post-write
  handling: a non-session-defaults write always clears the plugin/skill caches
  (`handle_config_mutation`), and `reloadUserConfig` additionally rebuilds the
  user config and refreshes the loaded threads' config-derived runtime state
  (`reload_user_config`), mapping Rust's per-thread `refresh_runtime_config` onto
  Go's `configureMCPFromConfig` + `mcpRuntimes.invalidateAll` (Go resolves other
  thread state per request). Errors stay non-fatal, like Rust's warnings.
- `appserver/runtime_router.go`: `handleConfigBatchWrite` calls the new mutation
  hook; `effectivePluginsChanged` now also invalidates the MCP runtimes and
  refreshes the hook runtimes (Rust `on_effective_plugins_changed`), and the
  plugin-uninstall, marketplace-upgrade (only when a root actually changed), and
  the four plugin-share handlers refresh the same state Rust refreshes
  (`plugin_uninstall` -> effective change, `plugin_share_*` -> cache clear only).
- Client side: the remote settings writer and the project-trust writer now send
  `reloadUserConfig: true`, matching Rust's `config_update::write_config_batch`
  (the hooks writer already did).

- The same mutation handling now also runs for the single-edit
  `config/value/write` RPC (Rust `handle_config_mutation_result`) and for
  `experimentalFeature/enablement/set` (cache clear, plus a user-config reload
  when anything was accepted - Rust's non-empty-enablement reload).

- Auth-change refresh landed as well: the account login/logout/session-switch
  paths now use `refreshPluginCachesAndMCPRuntimes` (clear the plugin/skill
  caches + invalidate the loaded threads' MCP runtimes, Rust's
  `spawn_effective_plugins_changed_task`), and a successful MCP OAuth login
  invalidates the MCP runtimes through the completion handler (Rust
  mcp_processor). Tests:
  `TestAuthChangeRefreshesPluginCachesAndMCPRuntimesLikeRust`,
  `TestMCPOAuthLoginCompletionInvalidatesRuntimesLikeRust`.

Tests: `TestConfigEditsAreSessionDefaultsOnlyMatchesRust`,
`TestConfigBatchWriteHonorsReloadUserConfigLikeRust`,
`TestConfigValueWriteClearsDerivedCachesLikeRust`,
`TestExperimentalFeatureSetRefreshesLikeRust`,
`TestEffectivePluginsChangedRefreshesDerivedStateLikeRust`,
`TestMarketplaceUpgradeWithoutChangesDoesNotRefreshLikeRust`; new
`config-batch-write-mutation` parity contract. Verification: `go build ./...`;
`go test ./appserver` (96s), `./app`, `./tui/...`, `./parity` green.

Residuals: Rust also reloads the user config after plugin install and the
marketplace/plugin-share remote paths through the async effective-plugin
callback (Go refreshes the caches/runtimes but does not rebuild the user config
in those handlers, since Go re-reads config per operation); the Rust
`ConfigValueWriteParams` has no reload flag, so the single-edit path matches
Rust by clearing caches only; voice settings writes (`app/realtime_settings.rs`)
are handled by the parallel voice process.
- `tui/src/experimental_features.rs` (154 lines) - **divergent**, not a missing
  symbol. Rust's TUI discovers features through the app server
  (`experimentalFeature/list`, paginated, 10-page/5s bounds, duplicate-name and
  repeated-cursor guards) and persists them through `config/batchWrite`
  (`features.<name>` TOML edits, `WriteStatus::OkOverridden` /
  configured-vs-selected readback warning). Go's app server implements both RPCs,
  but the TUI's `/experimental` menu (`tui/tea/settings_commands.go`
  `openExperimentalMenu`, `m.featureSettings`) and its writes
  (`settingsWriteKindExperimental`) use the local config directly and never call
  the RPCs, so a remote TUI shows local feature definitions instead of the
  server's catalog.

  Landed this pass (the write half of the rule): `setExperimentalFeatures` now
  emits Rust's edit values - `true` when enabling, `nil` (clear the override)
  when disabling a default-enabled feature, `false` otherwise - pinned by
  `TestModelExperimentalWritesClearDefaultOverridesLikeRust`. The remote TUI
  already routes these edits through the server's `config/batchWrite`
  (`interactiveRemoteSettingsWriteHandler`), so the emitted `features.<name>`
  TOML is correct end-to-end.

  Server-catalog popup landed this pass:
  - `tui/tea/experimental_features.go` (new): `ExperimentalFeatureEntry`,
    `ExperimentalFeaturesReaderFunc`, the result message + generation guard, the
    Beta-only catalog mapping, the popup body status lines, and the
    discovery-state transitions (loading -> rows / "No server experiments
    available." / "Server experiments unavailable. Reopen /experimental to
    retry; restart this Codex client if requests remain unanswered.", stale
    generations dropped) from Rust `bottom_pane/experimental_features_view.rs`.
  - `settings_commands.go`: `/experimental` now opens empty (server-driven) and
    populates from the catalog when a reader is wired; the compiled registry
    (`chatwidget.NewExperimentalFeaturesView`) is only the fallback. The write
    path now warns when the readback differs from the selection ("Changes were
    saved, but the configured values differ from your selections. A
    higher-priority setting may override them."), mirroring Rust's
    `FeatureWriteResult.warning`.
  - `app/experimental_features.go` (new): `fetchExperimentalFeatures` ports
    `experimental_features::fetch` (<=100/page, <=10 pages, 5s bound,
    duplicate-name drop, repeated-cursor guard, Rust's error strings) and the
    embedded reader resolves the catalog through the same config service the app
    server's handler uses (thread cwd included). Both transports are wired
    (`interactiveRemoteExperimentalFeatures`, `interactiveLocalExperimentalFeatures`).
  - `features/api.go`: `stageFromFeature` now maps Deprecated/Removed to their
    own wire stages (they were reported as `beta`, so a Beta-only menu would have
    offered removed flags); pinned by `TestFeatureStageMappingMatchesRust`.
  - Tests: `TestFetchExperimentalFeaturesMatchesRust`,
    `TestFetchExperimentalFeaturesGuardsMatchRust`,
    `TestModelExperimentalMenuUsesServerCatalogLikeRust`,
    `TestModelExperimentalWritesClearDefaultOverridesLikeRust`,
    `TestModelExperimentalReadbackWarningLikeRust`, `TestFeatureStageMappingMatchesRust`;
    new `experimental-feature-catalog` parity contract.

  Popup write state machine landed this pass (`tui/tea/experimental_features.go` +
  `settings_commands.go` + `modal.go`):
  - Read-only rows: `ExperimentalFeatureOption.Writable` ports Rust's
    `writable` rule (the unmigrated `prevent_idle_sleep`/`guardian_approval`
    controls are read-only unless the compiled registry still advertises them with
    a matching default), the row label gains ` (read-only)`, and toggles are
    refused. Rows stay navigable like Rust's (its `is_disabled` only dims).
  - In-flight save guard: Enter starts one save and keeps the popup open with
    `Saving experimental features…`; toggles and further saves are refused; a
    second Enter (or Esc) closes the popup while the write finishes.
  - Unconfirmed retry: a failed save keeps the popup open with the error as its
    status and the dirty keys retained, and a later accept re-sends them even
    after the row is reverted to its baseline (Rust's "corrective write").
  - Readback: the accepted save applies the readback enablement to the rows
    (skipping the unmigrated controls), clears the unconfirmed keys, and closes
    the popup unless the readback warned (the overridden message from
    `experimental_features::write`).
  - Special controls: their edits follow Rust's `build_feature_enabled_edit`
    (disable clears the override only for a default-off feature) instead of the
    generic `experimental_features::write` rule, and they are never retried
    through the generic path.

  Still open: the 100 ms popup frame tick (Go's catalog/write results arrive as
  messages, so no tick is needed - recorded as architecture-N/A), and the live
  runtime application of feature flags (Rust `AppEvent::UpdateFeatureFlags`
  updates the chat widget's runtime feature state; Go's
  `chatwidget.SettingsRuntimeState` has no production caller, so flags take
  effect per turn/restart), plus the voice gate for `realtime_conversation` in the
  catalog merge.

These two are the next TUI work items (`task_mentions` first - it is the
companion of the ported dynamic-tools namespace).

Upstream is therefore fully triaged through `ee6814bfa4`: the newest non-release
